# Decisions log

## 2026-09-28 — answers to server-model open questions

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which devices may connect to the bridge? | Any device that has SSH access to the herdr server (devbox). Auth = SSH access, not a Tailscale device allowlist. Primary target: **great performance on MacBooks** (M2 Max). |
| 2 | Which terminal app on the Mac? | **Ghostty**. Used for "open in real terminal": game launches Ghostty running `herdr --remote dev@devbox --session <s>`. |
| 3 | How to show stopped sessions? | Workers **sleeping behind their desks (zZz)**. |
| 4 | "Fire" semantics / visual | Worker takes their laptop and **leaves the building carrying a cardboard box with their stuff**; workspace is closed. |
| 5 | Initial building seeding | **Manual**: user creates buildings and assigns sessions to them. |
| 6 | Bridge language | **Go** (fast compilation, good performance). |

Implications:
- (1) Bridge listens on localhost / a 0600 unix socket on the server only; clients reach it through an SSH tunnel. Anyone who can SSH in is authorized; nothing is exposed on the tailnet directly.
- (1)+(2) A plain browser tab cannot open an SSH tunnel or launch Ghostty → likely thin native shell (e.g. Tauri) or a small Mac helper. Pending follow-up research.

## 2026-09-28 — answers to engine open questions

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Browser-based game OK? | Yes, **if performance is acceptable** on MacBooks (to be proven by benchmark). |
| 2 | Install `playwright-core` on the Mac (~/h3d-tools)? | **Approved.** |
| 3 | Install mesa on the server? | Approved if useful. Only needed for Godot pixel output on server, or possibly WebGPU-backend screenshots via lavapipe; not needed for the three.js WebGL2 test path. |
| 4 | Art style | **Cartoon, "Minions"-like**: small, round, goofy workers. Original characters inspired by that vibe — no Illumination/Minions IP (names, exact designs, likeness). |

| # | Question | Decision |

Parent link = shared `h3d_run`. Backup of original in orchestrator scratchpad.

## 2026-09-28 — Ghostty + herdr upgrade

- **Ghostty is optional**, not part of the core loop. herdr servers run headless on the server; all play happens in-game
  (live laptop screens, prompt/approve/keys through the bridge). Ghostty only backs an optional "open raw terminal" button.
- **herdr 0.9.1 upgrade approved.** Mac upgraded via brew (0.9.0 → 0.9.1). Server `herdr update` refuses to run inside a
  herdr pane → Theo runs it from a plain SSH shell, then `herdr integration install claude`.

## 2026-09-29 — M6b (#28): arrival style and the desk monitor

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Drop from the sky, or step out of a void? | **Drop from above.** The hire falls into its chair from 1.6 m over the seat (0.36 s, a little stretched, its shadow growing on the seat), lands flattened, springs back and settles, with a puff of dust round the chair: 0.85 s in all, one `drop` phase. It suits the cartoon crew and needs no new material (a portal wants transparency and a shader). The desk is the worker's from the first frame (never `away`): look-at, the card, sitting at its screen and prompts work while it plays; only the figure moves. Only the floor you are on shows it; anywhere else it resolves at once. |
| 2 | Where the monitor sits, how big | **Centred, facing the chair; the dock on the pod's outer side.** Glass 0.74 × 0.40 m (a 32" monitor; the M5 lid was 0.31 × 0.2) under the worker's hat line: its top is at 1.17 m, so the hat (the model cue) shows over it from across the pod, and the cards (1.62 m) and the empty desk's "+" (raised to 1.45 m) stay clear. The docks sit at the pods' outer corners, so every docked laptop's lid (and sticker) faces an aisle; the worker's photos, mug and plant stand on the other side, towards the neighbour. |
| 3 | Readable at a glance | Standing, a monitor shows the **glance view**: the last lines that say something (no blank rows, empty frames or agent footer), wrapped at 22 columns, 5 lines, bold and lifted to 4.5:1, under a status bar with the status's word. At the Mac's 3024 × 1890 that is ~12 px text at 4 m, ~16 px at 3 m. Straight behind a worker its own body hides its monitor (it sits right in front of it), so monitors read diagonally along the aisles. Seated, the whole terminal. |
| 4 | A new worker at a desk someone is still leaving | **The walk-out gives the desk up for good.** The bridge frees a desk as its worker goes, so a hire (or a freelancer re-seated by its orchestrator) can land on it while the old worker is still packing. From the moment the world seats someone else there, the older walk never shows that desk `away` again, not even after the 0.85 s drop is over (`Arrivals.yieldDesk`). It gets up and walks off at once (no more packing or waving in the new worker's chair); its box keeps what it had packed, the laptop on top, and the rest of the desk's things stay put as the new worker's. |

## 2026-09-29 — M10 follow-up (#47): the plan-limits HUD and screen-filling dialogs

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Pill or fold away? | **An edge tab where there is room, else fold away.** Over a screen-filling dialog (`dialog.panel`, the Issues / Pull requests boards and the item modal) the plate would hide the close button and the first row, at any width. The dialogs are centred and sized to the viewport, so no spot in the top bar or a corner is guaranteed free (at 1280 px the board leaves 50 px each side, at 1200 px 16). The widget measures the gap right of the widest open dialog: with ≥ 48 px it slides into a 32 px tab at the right edge (worst percentage over a severity bar, so never colour alone); with less it folds away like the narrow-viewport case. Both are recomputed on resize. |
| 2 | Shift+L over such a dialog | **Peek.** Shift+L and a click on the tab open the expanded panel above the dialog without touching the saved `open` pref; Shift+L again, the plate's header, a pointer-down outside it, or closing the dialog ends the peek. A peek belongs to the topmost screen dialog when it started: closing that one ends it even if another (the board under a closed item modal) stays open, and opening another on top ends it too; the plate then docks for the dialog that is on top. A meter the user hid stays hidden (Shift+L does nothing). Narrow viewports (< 760 px) keep the old fold: Shift+L is a no-op there. Shift+L is handled before the dialog guard in `App.onKey`, since dialogs otherwise own the keys. |
| 3 | On close | The plate is back in the saved state (strip or expanded) in the same task the dialog closes, with no animation and no focus move (focus only follows when it was on the tab or the plate). |
| 4 | Clicking over a modal | A modal `<dialog>` makes the rest of the page inert, top layer included, so a tab or peeking plate left in the page can't be clicked. While a screen dialog is open the widget therefore lives *inside* it (still a fixed-position popover in the top layer) and goes home when the last one closes. |
| 5 | Small dialogs (confirm, hire, start-run, send-home, prompt, presets, open-PR) | **Unchanged**: the plate stays above them (hire and start-run also show the limits inline). Only the Changes panel steps it aside, as before. |

## 2026-09-30 — #51 (and #13): images reach the agents through Drop Zone, over HTTP

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The transport of an image: the `/ws` chunks #13 specified, or HTTP? | **HTTP: `POST /upload` with the raw bytes**, through the helper to the bridge, beside the logos (M3d). The WebSocket reads whole messages up to 256 KiB (`maxClientMsg`; fragmenting one message does not get round that cap), so an image of up to 25 MiB would need base64 or binary chunks with an offset protocol, a per-connection transfer state, acks, cancellation and audit redaction. HTTP gives the streaming, the status codes, the body cap and the cancellation (a closed request) for free, does not queue behind or block the control traffic of the one socket, and the logo routes are the precedent for the topology (URL derived from the WebSocket URL, token kept, Host/Origin guards, the token and `Origin` not forwarded). It changes #13's stated transport; the rest of #13 (Drop Zone, one file per request, 25 MB, markers) stands. |
| 2 | Drop Zone's contract | Facts read from its source (`internal/upload/handler.go`, `store.go`): `POST /upload` multipart with the `file` part; an optional `name` field **only counts if it comes before the file part**; one file is stored under its sanitised stem and original extension, **two or more file parts become a `tar.zst`** (so one request per image); the file is spooled and claimed into a date folder **only after the whole body parsed**, with `-2`, `-3` for a collision (so the path is never predicted); success is `200 text/plain` with the absolute path and **one** line feed. It has no authentication, size cap or type check: the bridge owns those. The client never supplies the upstream URL (`--dropzone-url`, default `http://127.0.0.1:8080`) and the bridge never writes the drop directory itself. |
| 3 | Size | **25 MiB** (`25 << 20` = 26214400 bytes), counted on the bytes read (`http.MaxBytesReader` in the bridge, plus one byte past the cap as a sentinel in the streaming), never on `Content-Length`. A screenshot is a few MB; a retina PNG with a lot of detail can pass 10. MiB not MB, so the number is a power of two the game can show and compare exactly. Over the cap is `413`, and the request to Drop Zone is **aborted without the multipart footer**: a plain `LimitReader` would cut the image short and still finish the body, storing a truncated file. The helper allows the cap plus 4 KiB (also for chunked bodies) and leaves the exact verdict to the bridge. |
| 4 | Type checking | **Sniff, do not decode.** The first 512 bytes go through `http.DetectContentType`; only PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP pass, the extension follows the sniff (`.jpg` for JPEG), the part carries the sniffed type, and the client's `Content-Type` and file name are hints nobody looks at (an SVG or HTML labelled `image/png` is `415`). Decoding would cost memory for a 25 MiB screenshot and prove little; the agents' own readers say if a file is corrupt. |
| 5 | Name | `h3d-<label>-<n>`: the label a hint from the client, cut down to `[a-z0-9-]` and 24 characters (`hire` when empty), `n` the image's sequence in the draft. Not the client's file name. The number in the stored name need not match the `[Image #n]` marker (that is document order); no file is renamed after a reorder. |
| 6 | Lost answer | **An orphan is accepted.** If the connection drops after Drop Zone stored the file, nobody references it and a retry stores another (`-2`). The bridge cannot delete it (Drop Zone has no delete, and the bridge does not write its directory) and should not guess what is its own. |
| 7 | Concurrency and time | At most 4 uploads at once (a fifth is `429` `upload_busy`, not queued: a queue would hold a client's body open), 2 minutes for the whole upload (`504` `upload_timeout`), 5 s to connect, 30 s for Drop Zone's answer after the last byte, no redirects (an image is never replayed elsewhere). The pipe to Drop Zone is closed when the context ends: the HTTP transport waits for its writer, which waits on the pipe, so cancelling a context alone would hang. |
| 8 | Audit | Rows `image.upload` with label, `n`, size, type and outcome; never the bytes, and not the path (an audit row is not where a path is needed). |

## 2026-09-30 — #51: dialogs never close by accident; the hire dialog is a full-screen sheet

The bug: `click` with `e.target === dialog` closed a dialog when a press inside (a text selection) was released on the backdrop, because the click lands on the two targets' common ancestor, the `<dialog>`. Ten dialogs had that check; all are replaced by one helper, `backdropPolicy` in `game/src/ui/dismiss.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Backdrop policy per dialog | **`none`** (only the close button and Esc close it) for every dialog that holds typed text or a consequential choice. **`sheet`** (closes on the backdrop only when the primary-button press AND the click both landed on the `<dialog>` element itself, outside its box; a press that began inside, on its padding or with another button never counts; a pointer cancel, a second press or closing resets it) for the read-only ones. |
| 2 | The table | `none`: hire, start-run (extra instructions), presets, the P prompt, open-PR (title and body), the Changes / settings panels (`panels.ts`, typed fields), the Settings window (#91), send-home (a worktree choice with a delete in it: a stray click must not confirm anything, and dismissing is not the safe default), the board item modal (comment box). `sheet`: About, credits, the HUD sheet, the Issues / Pull requests board list. |
| 3 | `closedby` | Set to `closerequest` on `none` dialogs as progressive enhancement only (Chrome 134, Firefox 141; Safari has no stable support, and `closedby="none"` would also take Esc away). The policy never depends on it: not adding a backdrop listener is the fix. |
| 4 | Esc | Unchanged, with each dialog's own guard (hire: "the hire is still on its way"; presets: editor / delete confirmation first). |
| 5 | Drafts | What is typed in the hire dialog stays until a hire is acknowledged: per desk (`<session>:<desk>`, not the desk number alone), in memory and in `localStorage` (`h3d.draft.hire.<key>`, every access in try/catch, memory only when storage is blocked, full or corrupt), saved on each edit, restored on reopen. Stored as `{ text, fields?, segments? }` (`ui/drafts.ts`) so the shared composer (#13) stores its document in `segments` under the same key. The task and a label the user typed are kept; the folder is not (it is re-checked against the bridge, so restoring it silently would skip that). An acknowledged hire clears the draft **only if it is unchanged** since it was sent, so a hire answered after Esc and a new dialog never eats the newer text; a failed hire keeps it; a hire acknowledged with `prompted: false` (the worker exists, the task did not arrive) keeps the text (the toast says so). A first task handed in by "Ask a worker…" / "Review with a worker" wins over a draft for that open and is not saved until edited, so the old draft stays until then. Moving to another desk ("Hire at Desk N instead") takes what is typed along; an empty form takes that desk's own draft. Not done: presets' editor draft on Esc (Esc still goes back to the list, as before) and start-run / open-PR / P-prompt persistence (the P prompt already keeps its own in-memory draft per worker). |
| 6 | Hire layout | A near-full-screen sheet (12 px gutter, none on a phone; `dvh` and safe-area aware), header / tabs / body / footer as rows of the shell, so **Hire and Cancel never scroll away** (no sticky inside a scroller). The body is two panes: the first-task editor (no 180 px cap, grows with the window) and the options (harness, model, effort, label, folder, worktree) beside it (from 760 px wide) or under it. Every pane says `min-width/min-height: 0`; on desktop the options pane scrolls on its own, on a phone the whole body does. |
| 7 | The editor slot | The task editor is a small class (`TaskEditor` in `hire.ts`: text, focus, busy) in its own pane that re-rendering the options never touches, so its caret, selection and scroll survive a model or limits update. The composer replaces that one class. |
| 8 | Model list | Up to 10 models: the same chips. More: a filter above chips in a multi-column grid, with the count (`12 of 26`), no inner scroll box (the options pane scrolls as a whole), the checked model always visible (a filter never looks like a changed choice), a "no model matches" line. Enter in the filter does not hire; Esc clears it first. A harness switch clears it. Native radios: arrows still pick. |
| 9 | Limits plate (amends #47 row 5) | **Hire is a screen dialog now.** A sheet with a 12 px gutter leaves no room for the edge tab, so over it the plate is away (`data-dock="away"`); Shift+L (outside a text field, where it types an L) still peeks it, and the dialog's own inline limits line stays. The start-run, send-home, prompt, presets and open-PR dialogs stay small: the plate keeps above them. |

## 2026-09-30 — #51 (M11 #13): the shared prompt composer

Built as a standalone module (`game/src/ui/composer/`, `game/src/net/upload.ts`); the hire dialog, the P overlay and the start-run instructions are wired to it in a later round.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `contenteditable` with chips, or a textarea with a thumbnail strip? | **`contenteditable` with atomic chips.** #51 asks for the image inline, at the caret, in order with the text; a strip cannot say where in the text a picture was. One component for every prompt, not a hire-only fork. The editor is vanilla (no editor library): plain text and chips only, so the browser edits text natively and everything it cannot be trusted with goes through our model (chips, paste, drop, Enter, delete beside a chip, undo). If IME or history acceptance fails on the Mac browsers, the fallback is a framework-neutral editor core behind the same `Composer` API, not scattered browser hacks. |
| 2 | Model shape | **One string plus the occurrences.** The document is a string in which each image is one U+FFFC, and the list of image occurrences in that order. A caret or a selection is an offset; ranges are `slice`s; "between two chips" and "two chips side by side" need no cases. Text is cleaned at the boundary (CRLF/CR → LF, U+FFFC and U+200B dropped). An occurrence (one chip) points at a **resource** (the bytes and their upload): the two ids differ so one upload can serve two chips and every chip stays editable. The marker number is never stored: it is the occurrence's place in the document. The DOM is drawn from the model and read back on `input`; chips are `contenteditable=false` wrappers with separate preview, retry and remove buttons; an empty text run is a U+200B text node so a caret has a place beside a chip. |
| 3 | Serialiser | `[Image #n: <path>]` in **document order**, path exactly as Drop Zone returned it (absolute, no `]`, no control characters or line breaks, else an error and nothing is sent), text verbatim, brackets the user typed are never read. No trailing hint by default (an option adds one line; no experiment has said it is needed). Cap in UTF-8 bytes, checked on the final text: hire 32 KiB, `worker.prompt` 16 KiB, passed in by the host. `resolve()` never returns partial text. |
| 4 | Uploads | Per image, as soon as it is pasted (eager), at most 2 at a time, `pending → uploading → ready(path) \| error`, one `AbortController` and an `attempt` per resource so an answer for a removed or retried image is ignored. A removed chip cancels its upload; undoing the removal queues it again. `resolve()` waits for the images the document uses, and is `ok:false` with the failed images (never a partial prompt) if any failed; it does **not** retry a failed image by itself (a broken Drop Zone is not hit by every Enter): the chip's Retry does, and a ready image is never sent twice. HTTP `POST /upload` per the shared contract (`net/upload.ts`, like the logos). |
| 5 | Limits | 20 images per prompt (our opinion, not the ticket's), `UPLOAD_MAX_BYTES` = 25 MiB each, PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP by type or (no type) extension, empty files refused, each with a friendly message; the bridge stays the authority (sniffed bytes). Object URLs are kept while the draft, its history or an open preview can show the image, then revoked. |
| 6 | Undo | **One history** in the draft for text and images (100 steps; typing within 1 s is one step); the browser's own undo is never used (`beforeinput` history types and Ctrl/⌘+Z, Shift+Z, Y are ours). Covered: typing, IME commit, text paste, chip insert/remove, selection replacement. Not covered: upload progress and preview open/close are not history. |
| 7 | Enlarged view | A second native modal `<dialog>` (top layer, so it shows over a modal host; inert below). **Esc closes only it**: a capture-phase keydown on `window` closes it and swallows the key, and `cancel` of the preview and of the host (capture, before the host's own handler) are refused while it is open (the research probe found two nested dialogs can both close on one Esc). A held Esc keeps repeating after the view is gone, so when Esc (or a close request) closed it, the keydown repeats and the host's `cancel` stay swallowed until the Esc keyup; a lost keyup cannot strand the guard: a new (non-repeat) Esc, any other key, window blur, or 1 s without a repeat lifts it. Closes on its close button, Esc, or a press *and* release on the empty surface without a drag; never on the image. On close the caret (also a backward selection), scroll and focus come back from the draft's own record; nothing is edited while it is open. |
| 8 | Drafts | The host keeps the `Draft` object: text, caret, images (bytes and uploaded paths) and history survive closing the dialog, and uploads go on meanwhile. The composer never disposes a draft. `clearIf(revision)` clears only what was sent, so an edit made while a send was pending survives it. |
| 9 | Deliberately out | Recent drops, office-context markers and non-image `[File: …]` chips (#13's later parts); IndexedDB / localStorage persistence of pending images across a reload (a text-only save is left to the host); dragging chips to reorder (`moveImage` exists in the model, no gesture yet); zoom/pan and next/previous in the enlarged view; a drop-position indicator (the editor is highlighted); dedupe of identical images (two pastes are two chips); re-encoding or downscaling (bytes go up as pasted); HEIC/TIFF. Checked only in Chromium: macOS Safari/Chrome (clipboard screenshots, IME, VoiceOver) need the manual pass in research-composer.md. |


## 2026-09-30 — #51: the composer goes into the hire dialog

`TaskEditor` in `hire.ts` mounts the shared composer where the textarea was (same slot, same `#hire-task` hook, no re-layout). The P prompt and the start-run extra instructions are **not** wired yet.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What a desk keeps | **The composer's `Draft` per desk, in memory** (`docs`, keyed like the text drafts, `<session>:<desk>`): text, caret, images with their bytes and uploaded paths, undo history, and uploads that go on while the dialog is closed. The text (images left out) is still mirrored to the `localStorage` draft on each change, so a reload brings back the words and **not the pictures** (an image lives as a `Blob`; persisting bytes is #13's later part). Moving to another desk takes the draft along (an empty form takes that desk's own). A first task handed in ("Ask a worker…") starts a draft of its own that joins the desk from its first edit. |
| 2 | Hire | Hire sets the dialog busy, `await draft.resolve({ maxBytes: 32 KiB })`, and only on `ok` sends `worker.hire` with the serialised text as `args.prompt` (`… [Image #1: /abs/path] … [Image #2: /abs/path] …`, document order). The desk's "+" is held from the moment the request is sent, not while images go up. The form's `prompt` (and the "starts with `--`" check) is the text with each image as a bare `[Image #n]`; an image alone is a non-blank first task (the bridge only trims the final text). |
| 3 | An image did not upload | **No `worker.hire` at all**, the whole prompt stays, the editor is editable again; each failed chip says why and has its Retry, and the dialog says it in words ("Nothing was hired: image 1 (name) did not upload (…). Retry it … then hire again. Your prompt is kept.") until every image is uploaded or gone. `resolve()` never retries a failed image by itself. A hire answered after Esc reports by toast instead. |
| 4 | Success | The desk's draft (memory, storage) is cleared **only if unchanged since the request** (`Draft.clearIf(revision)`), and not after `prompted: false`; a refused hire keeps text and images. A newer draft that took the desk while the hire was pending (Esc, reopen with a first task, edit) keeps its memory and stored text: ownership is by identity, never by comparing text: whoever saves the desk's draft (a task or label edit, the desk's own draft on open, a move) is registered as its owner, and only the owner of the desk clears memory and storage, so a replacement with the very same text but a new label survives. |
| 5 | Layers and keys | The enlarged view is a modal nested in the hire dialog's DOM: Esc closes it alone (its capture-phase key guard and the two `cancel` refusals), the next Esc closes the dialog under the existing "hire is still on its way" toast. The dialog's `close` closes an open enlarged view (it holds a window key listener). Enter hires, Shift+Enter is a new line, **Ctrl/⌘+Enter hires too** (the textarea always did; the composer's Enter policy now matches). Fixed in the game: the Shift+L limits peek and the "typing" guard in `App.onKey` now count a `contenteditable` as a text field (Shift+L typed an L **and** peeked the plate). |
| 6 | Left for #13 | The **P prompt** (`prompt.ts`) and the **start-run extra instructions** (`startRun.ts`) keep their textareas: the P overlay has its own per-worker draft, edit counter, read-only-while-sending and caret handling around `worker.prompt` (16 KiB cap), and the extra instructions are an argument of `run.prepare`, not a prompt of its own, so neither is a few lines. The composer already takes `submitPolicy`, `WORKER_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES` and a per-worker `Draft` for the P overlay. |

## 2026-09-30 — #56 (M16 phase 2): images in the transcript view, the Images tab and the desk prints

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the bytes live | **Not in items.** An item carries an `ImageRef` (sha256 of the bytes, sniffed mime, size, `origin` input/agent, `kind` generated/viewed, the item it is in). Bytes are served by `transcript.image` for a hash **this connection was sent**, so a client cannot ask for what it was not shown and never names a path. Inline images are decoded and hashed once per transcript line (remembered by line offset), filed in a 64 MiB LRU (`imgcache`) plus an optional 0600/0700 disk cache, and read again from their line when neither has them: the cache can be small and lose things. |
| 2 | Path images | Only under an allowlist: Drop Zone's data, `~/.codex/generated_images`, the worker's cwd and the exact file of a Codex `view_image`. The check is lexical first (a path outside every root is refused **before any file system call**, so an outside file is not even looked at), then again after `EvalSymlinks`, then regular file, image bytes, <= 25 MB. The read itself does not reuse the checked path: it walks down from `/` along the real path one component at a time with `O_NOFOLLOW` (`O_PATH` for the directories on Linux, so a pass-through-only directory still works; any directory swapped for a link after the check, below the root, the root itself or one above it, is refused), opens the file non-blocking (a FIFO swapped in cannot hold the bridge) and checks the opened descriptor. The cost is that a path reached through some other symlink than the root's own spelling is refused; that is the safe side. |
| 3 | Thumbnails | Cut on demand (pure Go, CatmullRom, at most 2 at once), cached with the images; JPEG stays JPEG, the rest is PNG. Full images are capped at 12 MiB of base64 (`too_large`; the view says so and keeps the thumbnail). |
| 4 | Images tab data | `transcript.images` lists the whole session from the offset index (an item flagged as having images is read back once); it is not the watch's 200-item window. It is paged with a cursor (`before`, `limit`, `has_more`): the client follows it to the end however many pages that takes, nothing is cut off; a cursor that does not move on (one given before, or none with `has_more`) fails the list, which the tab shows, instead of passing a part for the whole. Hash dedupe and the count badge are the game's. |
| 5 | Grouping rule in the conversation | Consecutive **image-only** items (a `user` or `assistant` item with images and no text) of the **same role** share one strip. A text part, any other visible row (tool, subagent, thinking, compaction, notice) and a change of role break the run; rows the view hides do not (unless "show system" shows them). Within one message or tool row all images are one strip, text between images makes another. Grouping across tool rows (a run of `Read`s) stays out: a tool row breaks a run. Since #70 a tool row's strip shows with the row collapsed (and, bare, with tool calls hidden); it is still that row's own strip and still breaks a run. |
| 6 | The plan-limits plate over the reading overlay | The overlay is a screen like the M3 panels: the plate steps out into its edge tab (Shift+L peeks) instead of hanging over the overlay's header. |
| 7 | Desk prints | A desk's front strip, IN tray (white-bordered prints) on the worker's left, OUT tray (coloured) on the right, at most 5 + 5. Loaded only for the two nearest visible desks and disposed when they drop out (textures, bitmaps, object URLs); their data is a transcript watch with a limit of 1 plus `transcript.images`. |

## 2026-09-30 — #57: agents' questions, from the transcript

**Why a question never showed (the finding asked for by the issue).** Reproduced end to end in a throwaway session
(`h3d-askrepro-*`, Claude Code 2.1.285, herdr 0.9.0, 120 × 40, the operator's own Claude config) with a private bridge
and `herdr-world dump`: the same long AskUserQuestion (the issue's two options) in two panes, one Claude started as the
bridge hires (`agent start … -- … --name <label>`), one without `--name`. herdr said **`idle`** for the named one (the
`dump` has `worker.status idle`, no `question`, so no card flag and no banner), **`blocked`** for the other (then
`questionLoop` read its screen and the game's banner had the question, cut to its last wrapped line). A named
Sonnet 5.5 session said `done`. The screens and their `agent list` status are
`internal/server/testdata/screens/claude-ask-named.*` (idle) and `claude-ask-unnamed.*` (blocked). The cause: `--name`
makes Claude Code draw a rule with the session's name (`──── asker ─`) at the bottom of the screen, below the dialog,
and herdr's Claude manifest matches its blocked form only in the text after the **last** horizontal rule
(`after_last_horizontal_rule`): there it finds nothing, and the idle title (`✳ …`) wins. Every hire passes `--name`,
so every hired Claude worker's question was idle to herdr and the screen-first path (herdr `blocked` → the bridge reads
the screen → `worker.question` → the transcript view's banner) never started. The grounding's 46 captures all said
`blocked` because they were taken without `--name`; the long wrapping text is not the cause (both long captures, 100 × 30
and 200 × 50, are `blocked` too: `claude-ask-long-*.status.json`). Not fixed in herdr here (upstream: its manifest rule);
the transcript-first ask below does not depend on herdr's status at all. Also seen: Haiku 4.5 with MCP servers
configured does not write the pending `AskUserQuestion` line until it has its result (with `--strict-mcp-config` or
Sonnet it does), so a test agent must be Sonnet, or Haiku with `--strict-mcp-config`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where a question comes from | **The transcript, first.** The usage tracker already reads every worker's transcript (one tail, from the file's start); `transcript.AskTracker` gets the same lines (`usage.Session.SetLines`) and keeps only the pending call. Claude: the last `AskUserQuestion` `tool_use` of the main conversation (sidechains are subagents' and ignored) with no `tool_result` of its id (an error result too), no genuine prompt after it, no interruption marker. Codex: `function_call` `request_user_input` with no `function_call_output` of its `call_id`, no `turn_aborted`, no new user message. The parentUuid chain is not followed: a rewind or a new branch always starts with a new prompt, which ends the ask. Known gap: a call left dangling by a crash stays an ask until the next prompt; answering it then finds no dialog on the screen (`input_failed`), never presses keys into something else. |
| 2 | Attention | **Derived in the client** (`status === 'blocked' \|\| ask`), not a bridge `attention` field: `status` stays what herdr says (the elevator, the logs and the M5 path keep their meaning), and the ask carries everything a client needs, `since` included ("waiting 3m"). The game's store makes it `needs_you` (card, counts, N, crew line), on an awake floor only. |
| 3 | Answering | **Keys through the pane** (M5's lane), `worker.answer_ask`, verified on the screen before and after each step with the dialogs' own parsers (`askscreen.go`), against captured screens and live (Claude: single, two questions with a multi-select + Other, previews, Esc + prompt; Codex: three questions, Other with notes). A PreToolUse hook or the Codex app-server stay out (#57's out of scope). One herdr call per digit on a multi-select question: a batched `send-keys 1 4` was dropped live. The answer is `resolved` only when the transcript has the result. |
| 4 | Stale and safety | The answer names its `tool_use_id`; another or none is `stale`, checked when the command arrives, when its turn on the lane comes, and before every key (the ask answered in the terminal meanwhile stops it, no more keys). A dialog not where expected is `input_failed`, the dialog still there after the last key too (never a sent answer that did not take). A dialog is the ask's when it shows one of its questions with its options (Claude's review: its header tabs and each question's text in its place, since another ask can share the headers); Dismiss presses Esc on nothing else (another question on the screen is `stale`). Codex notes open with text other than the answer's `other` are `input_failed`, nothing pressed (Enter would submit that text; clearing them with Tab/Esc is sourced, not verified live). What the dialog held before is reconciled: rows ticked that the answer lacks, Other included, are unticked, and an Other row holding other text is emptied with ctrl+e, ctrl+u first (verified live: typing goes in at the start of what is there; a single-select Other row's digit submits what it holds, so the arrows go there). Errors name steps and questions by number only: they reach the audit log and the bridge's log. A prompt to a worker with an ask is refused (`invalid_state`): its dialog would take the text. No new herdr control shape: digits, arrows, Tab, Enter, Esc and ctrl+letters were allowed already. |
| 5 | "Chat about this" | Not a button of its own: **Dismiss** (Esc) with an optional prompt sent once the dialog is gone does "I want to talk about it first" in one action, on both agents (Claude records a rejected call, Codex interrupts the turn). |
| 6 | Codex | Answered the same way (no multi-select there); no sub-issue needed. |
| 7 | The screen question | Kept (permission and trust prompts have no transcript form), and its parser now reads the ask dialogs whole (long questions joined, clean labels, a Yes / No question is a `choice`). When a worker has both, the ask wins everywhere. |
| 8 | The card | Pinned above the prompt box (the prompt off while it is up), in the blocked banner's sun: tabs for several questions, options as rows led by their key cap (round: pick one; square: pick several), "(Recommended)" split off the label into a quiet badge, an Other field, Send off until every question has an answer. Pending → sending → gone once resolved; the timeline card then shows `● You answered: <question> → <answer>` from the transcript's result, however it was answered. Terminal mode says "Claude is asking a question. T to answer in the transcript view." T works until you type into the terminal (then it is a letter again and the line says Ctrl+Esc): the dialog's own Other field may be what you are typing into. |

## 2026-09-30 — #64: a pasted screenshot in every prompt field

The composer (#13, #51) goes into every field that prompts an agent, with no second paste path: the P overlay, Start Run's Extra instructions and First prompt, the transcript view's prompt box; a paste at a laptop's terminal uses the composer's draft without its view. Each field keeps its own submit keys and its own draft rules.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does Start Run's Ticket take an image? | **No, text only.** The ticket is parsed (`#7`, an issue URL or free text), its words make the run's label (`ORCH|fix-the`) and its 4 KiB cap is the bridge's; a marker in it would spoil the label and the parse, and the same image in Extra reaches the orchestrator at the same place in its prompt. Its hint does not promise one. The composer would have fitted (a `Draft` and `resolve()`), so this is a choice, not a cost. |
| 2 | Extra and First prompt | Extra is a composer over a `Draft` (label `run-extra`). Its serialised text (uploaded images as `[Image #n: path]`, a bare `[Image #n]` while one is still uploading) is `form.extra`, so `run.prepare` shows the markers in the generated prompt as soon as an image is up. The First prompt is a second composer (label `run-prompt`) and stays the editable preview: a generated prompt is put into it with `Draft.replaceAll(text, extraDraft)`, which turns each marker whose path is an uploaded image of Extra into an already-uploaded chip (no second upload, no history step, other markers stay text), so the slip shows a picture where the orchestrator will read one. Pasting straight into it works and makes the prompt "edited" like typing does; an edited prompt goes as `run.start.prompt`, an unedited one is composed by the bridge from `extra`. **The First prompt is read-only while the generated text names an Extra image by a bare `[Image #n]`** (its upload is still on its way, or failed; `previewLocked`): an edit then would freeze the pathless marker into a prompt that overrides the bridge's composition, and the image would upload and never reach the orchestrator. The field says why (its note: read-only until the image has uploaded, retry or remove it in Extra); when the image is up the next preview carries the path, the marker becomes a chip and the field opens. A prompt already edited never locks (its own images are chips and resolve by themselves). **Only a real image of Extra locks** (`previewLocked(preview, extraSnapshot)`): a bare marker counts when the Extra draft has an image with that number and the preview holds more bare markers of that number than the Extra's own prose (its text segments) does, so marker-shaped words the user typed ("keep the label [Image #1] in the docs", no image attached) leave the First prompt editable, and prose that collides with a real chip's number neither hides nor doubles it. |
| 3 | What Start sends, and when | Start resolves both drafts (Extra always; the First prompt only when edited): images still uploading are waited for (both fields read-only, the status says so), a failed one or a text over its cap (Extra 8 KiB, prompt 32 KiB, **with the paths in it**) stops the start with nothing sent and the fields as they are. A failed image also puts a reason on the Start button (`blocker`) as soon as it fails. The drafts belong to one open of the dialog (Start Run keeps no draft between opens, as before) and go with a successful start. |
| 4 | P overlay | The note is a composer on the paper bubble, `Draft` per worker in a map (memory), so the unsent draft keeps its images and uploads while the overlay is closed; the per-worker edit counter is the draft's `revision`, and a send clears with `clearIf(revision)` so a newer edit survives (as the hire does). It is read-only while it sends, as the hire is. Enter sends, Shift+Enter adds a line; **Ctrl/⌘+Enter also sends** now (the composer's `enter` policy, as in the hire dialog and, already, the transcript box), where it did nothing in the textarea. Upload label `prompt-<worker>`. |
| 5 | Transcript prompt box | The textarea is the composer's editor (class `tx-input` kept). One `Draft` for the life of the view (uploads are cancelled with it when you stand up, as the typed text always was). A prompt now stays in the box, read-only, until the bridge answers, and stays there if refused; before, the box was emptied at once and the text came back on a refusal, which cannot work for images (their uploads are released by a clear). A blocked worker's box is `setDisabled` (a new composer state next to `setBusy`: read-only, `aria-disabled`, focus kept away, draft kept) and its placeholder follows the question. The styling uses only `--tx-*` custom properties and `em` so the theming and zoom of #59 carry over; the composer's chips are re-sized in `em` inside `.tx`. Label `transcript-<worker>`. |
| 6 | Terminal paste | `ui/seat.ts` reads `clipboardData` files (the composer's `filesOf`: file items first, the file list only if there were none) as well as `text/plain`; files win over the text a copied file carries (its name). A worker **with an agent**: the images go up through a `Draft` (its pre-check, numbering and 2 at a time), then **one** `worker.prompt` with the markers side by side (`[Image #1: p1][Image #2: p2]`) is queued behind what was already queued. It is **all or nothing**: every file is pre-checked before the first goes up (so a refused file uploads nothing), and a failed upload sends nothing. A pending upload holds nothing else: keys typed meanwhile go out first (no lock on the terminal for up to 2 minutes of upload). A **plain shell** (no `agent_kind`) says `<name> runs a plain shell, not an agent: an image can’t be pasted there. Nothing was typed.` and uploads nothing. The seat bar's note is a few words wide (it ends in an ellipsis), so every error is a **toast as well**, and the note carries the whole text as its tooltip. Standing up does not cancel a paste on its way (as a queued paste never was). Label `terminal-<worker>`. |
| 7 | Failure, everywhere | Sent only when every image is uploaded. Uploading (pending) waits and then sends; a failure (over 25 MiB or not an image: refused in the field before any upload with the composer's message; `upload_busy`, `dropzone_unavailable`, a timeout: the chip says `Failed: …` with its Retry) sends nothing and says which image and why, in the field and, where the field has one, in its status line. The text of the prompt is kept in every field. **A refused file holds the send** (R1, fix round 2): a file refused before any upload is not a chip, so nothing in the draft says it existed; left as a notice only, the text would go alone and clear the draft. The P overlay, Start Run (Extra and First prompt) and the transcript box therefore mount their drafts with `holdRefused`: the refusal (the composer's message, several are joined) is the **draft's** state, shown under the field as an alert (`.cmp-refusal`) with a dismiss button (a real `<button>` after the editor, named "Dismiss this message and send without the refused file", so Tab and Enter reach it, and the editor gets focus back). `Draft.resolve()` answers `refused` while it is held, and each host also says so before it starts to send (`heldRefusalText`: `Nothing was sent: <why> Dismiss that message (the × under the box) to send without it. Your prompt is kept.`; Start Run's Start button carries it as its `blocker` with the field's name and scrolls the refusal into view). **Only the user's dismissal lifts it**: not typing, a later paste, a removed chip or a retry; the next send after it goes with the text and the files that were accepted. It stays with its draft (a worker's note keeps it across close and reopen), is gone with a cleared draft, and does not touch plain-text paste or typing. **A send's acknowledgement never dismisses it**: a refusal held when the answer comes is newer than the send (`resolve()` does not resolve while one is held), so `Draft.clearIf(revision)` empties the text and images the send covered and leaves the refusal held (a stale ack of a closed and reopened overlay, or of a swapped draft, cannot let the next prompt go without the refused file or the user's dismissal). The hire dialog keeps its behaviour (the option is off there: its notice names the file and the rest goes); the terminal paste pre-checks every file and sends nothing on its own. |
| 8 | Caps | The bridge's own, checked on the final text with the paths: `worker.prompt` 16 KiB (`WORKER_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES`), Extra 8 KiB, an edited run prompt 32 KiB. The issue names 16 KiB for the edited run prompt; the bridge takes 32 KiB there (it is a hire's first prompt), so that is the cap used. A marker costs about 80 bytes: 20 images (the composer's limit) are about 1.6 KiB of a field's cap, shared with its text. |
| 9 | Not done | Persisting pending images across a reload (as for the hire), a recent-drops list, dragging chips, pasting an image into the Ticket, Board comments (not an agent prompt). Checked only in Chromium. |

## 2026-09-30 — transcript themes, hide tool calls, font zoom (#59)

The issue's decisions 1–5 hold (a preset sets the terminal's font, background, text and cursor, the 16 ANSI colours
stay; a preset changes both fonts; Quiet is the default, close to the navy screen; the checkbox hides `tool` rows only;
the mono / prose split), with #2 below (the two views are themed apart). Made while building it:

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Terminal's prose font | **Archivo, not Bricolage Grotesque** (user, 30 Sep 2026). Bricolage has no italic and agents' replies use emphasis: the browser's slanted upright would stand in for it. Archivo (`@fontsource-variable/archivo` 5.3.0, OFL) has a true italic and the same printed grotesque feel against the pixel mono. |
| 2 | One choice or two | **Two** (user, 30 Sep 2026): the transcript and the terminal each pick one of the same three presets, and each has its own zoom. The transcript's preset sets its mono and prose fonts, prose size and colours, and every other monospace in the DOM (the limits widget, the boards, the HUD's numbers, the Images tab); the terminal's sets the canvas's font, background, text and cursor, nothing else. Stored apart: `h3d.theme.transcript`, `h3d.theme.terminal`, `h3d.zoom.transcript`, `h3d.zoom.terminal` (both Quiet at 100% by default). The keys and Ctrl+wheel zoom the view that is up (the Images tab has none). Controls: the transcript's in its top strip; the terminal's in the seat bar (the terminal mode's own chrome: three swatches in its screen colours and A− / level / A+, always in view). In terminal mode every key is the pane's (Tab too), so the seat bar's controls take the mouse and the zoom keys there, and Ctrl+, (settings, in many apps; it sent a pane nothing) puts the keyboard on them from any view, with or without a transcript; Esc there goes back to the view. Still exactly three preset constants. |
| 3 | Loading | Quiet's fonts come with the app (`main.ts`); Terminal's and Paper's load the first time either view picks them, from a stylesheet Vite splits per preset (literal `import()`s in `themeLoader.ts`). The stylesheets are our own Latin-only `@font-face`s pointing at the packages' Latin files: the packages' CSS declares every subset, and a Cyrillic or Greek character would then fetch another file in one preset and fall back in the next. The pickers' swatches are colours only: naming each preset in its own fonts would fetch all six. A stylesheet only declares faces and a file is fetched when text uses it: the terminal in Paper fetches Space Mono, never Newsreader. |
| 4 | Box drawing | Terminal canvas: box drawing, block elements and braille are **drawn as shapes** per cell (`glyphs.ts`), font-independent, since no preset's font has braille and JetBrains Mono's and Space Mono's Fontsource files have no boxes (checked in their files), and a system fallback differs per OS and in its advance. DOM (tool output, code): Departure Mono has boxes; for Quiet and Paper, **H3D Box**, the 160 box and block glyphs of DejaVu Sans Mono 2.37 (subset and renamed as its licence asks, 2.5 KB, inlined), sized to each mono's advance so a table's corners meet on every OS. |
| 5 | Ligatures | The canvas draws **each glyph in its own cell** (canvas 2D has no switch for JetBrains Mono's `calt`); the DOM sets `font-variant-ligatures: none` on every mono element. |
| 6 | Zoom keys | Ctrl + = / + zoom in, Ctrl + − out, Ctrl + 0 resets, and Ctrl+wheel over the view, **only while seated**, in the transcript and in terminal mode alike: `keys.ts` sent none of these four chords to a pane before (only ctrl+letters). Ctrl+_ (Ctrl+Shift+− on a US layout) is readline's, zsh's, fish's and Claude Code's undo and is not taken. Cmd (a Mac's browser zoom) is untouched; unseated, nothing is prevented. |
| 7 | What zooms | Each view on its own: the transcript's text (prose and mono together, their sizes relative to the preset's base: 100 % is 13.5 px, Paper's serif 15 px) and the seated terminal. **Not the transcript's top strip**: it holds the zoom's own buttons, which must stay in reach at 200 %. The HUD, the limits widget and the Images tab keep their sizes. |
| 8 | Terminal zoom | Above 100 % the cells grow past the fit and the screen shows the pane's bottom-left (an agent's prompt is at the bottom), moved just enough to keep the cursor in view; below 100 % the grid shrinks inside the letterbox. Pure (`viewport`), so the view never jumps between paints of the same screen. |
| 9 | Departure Mono's 11 px grid | In the transcript its size is a whole multiple of 11 px (11 up to 140 %, 22 from 150 %): the steps skip the sizes that blur. The canvas does not snap: it is a texture sampled in 3D, stretched to the monitor, so no size is pixel-exact there; the glyphs are fitted to their cells like every other font's. |
| 10 | Hidden tool calls | A tool call that fails while tool calls are hidden is not lost: a red chip in the strip names it (or counts them) until clicked, which shows those rows and goes to the last; while one runs, the chip names it and its time, so a quiet view does not look stuck. An agent's question (a tool call with an ask) and subagents stay. |
| 11 | A light screen | Paper keeps the 16 ANSI colours (decision 1), so the painter keeps the preset's text at 3:1 against an agent's own backgrounds (a diff's red and green lines) and, on a light screen, any colour against it (ANSI white on cream). |
| 12 | The About card | Stays on the system's monospace (`MONO`): cards are atlases drawn once, not worth redrawing on a pick. |

## 2026-09-30 — #70: a tool call's images show without opening its row

What the agent looked at (a `Read` of a PNG, a screenshot in a tool result, Codex `view_image`) was only visible after
opening the tool row, while the same picture in a message showed inline. Now a tool row with images shows them under its
header, collapsed.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the strip goes | **In the tool row, under the header button, outside `.tx-tool-body`**, built with the row (`toolRow`), the same `Thumbs.strip` (lightbox, lazy fetch by IntersectionObserver, cards for what is not available) as a message's. The body stays lazy (filled on first open) and no longer carries the images, so nothing is repeated when it opens. The strip hangs on the body's own 2 px rule, indented like it, so header, images and (opened) text read as one call; opening the row puts the text below the images, which do not move. A tool with images and no text says "No text output: the image is above." when opened, never "No output.". The group is named for a screen reader: `Read: <input_summary> — 1 image`. |
| 2 | Clicks and keys | A thumbnail is its own `<button>` outside the header, so a click or Enter / Space on it opens the lightbox and never toggles the row; the header still toggles the text. The lightbox is the strip's (2 / 2 on a Read of two). |
| 3 | Hide tools (#59) | **The images stay, as a bare strip**: hiding tool calls hides no screenshot. A tool row with images carries `tx-toolimg`; the hidden-tools CSS hides the other tool rows whole and, on these, everything but the strip (no header, no body, the rule and indent dropped, flush like a message's images). No second copy of the strip elsewhere: the row is the same row, so a resync, `scrollToItem` and the Images tab's "jump to message" find it as before. |
| 4 | The "new" counter and the chip | **A hidden tool call's images count as new**: they are on screen (`shows()`: a tool row counts while tool calls are hidden only when it has images). An update of an unread row now recounts the chip too (a hidden `Read` that finishes with its images becomes new content). **The failed / running chip keeps its rule**: a failed `Read` with images is still a failed tool call whose failure you cannot see (its header is hidden), so the chip names it, and clicking it shows the row whole (`tx-reveal`). A running call has no images yet: the chip says it runs. |
| 5 | Not available, not an image | The "Image not available" card shows collapsed too, as a **one-line** variant inside a tool strip (icon, title and path in a row, wrapping only for a long path) so the row stays short; a `binary` chip is one line already. |
| 6 | Re-render and scroll | A running call that finishes with images gets its strip when its update draws the row again. A thumbnail the feed already has (`ImageFeed.thumbNow`) is drawn at once when a row is drawn again (an update, a resync), with no shimmer, fade or second request, everywhere (messages too). The thumbnail's box has its size before the bytes (width and `aspect-ratio` from the `ImageRef`'s dimensions, as before), so nothing jumps when they arrive. |
| 7 | Sizes and runs | The same sizes as a message's strip (420 px for a lone image, 240 px each for several), so a phone screenshot makes a tall row; that is what the issue asks for (the picture is the point) and the header stays one line. A run of `Read`s stays separate strips (row 5 of #56): in the demo timeline a Read of two images and the next assistant images read fine as two groups. |
| 8 | Subagents | Their nested timelines render through the same `toolRow`, so their tool rows follow the same rule (checked in vitest). |

## 2026-09-30 — M17 (#50): day and night, the office clock

The game follows the viewer's local time (the browser's clock and zone), game only: no bridge or protocol change.
Decisions (orchestrator and user) and what was measured while building it:

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sunrise and sunset (open question) | **A latitude setting** (option b). With a latitude, the sun's altitude comes from the latitude, the solar declination of the day of the year and the hour angle, with **solar noon at 12:00 local standard time** (13:00 on the clock while the zone keeps summer time: the zone's offset against the smaller of its January and July offsets, from `Intl`). Daylight is a smoothstep of the altitude from −6° (civil twilight) to +7°, so the length of the day and of dawn follow the season and the latitude, and a polar day or night clamps. **No longitude** (a follow-up if ever): Brussels' sun sets about 45 min earlier than the real one. Without a latitude, the **fixed curve**: dawn 06:30–08:00, dusk 18:30–20:00 clock time, and a sun arc (up 07:15, down 19:15, 57° at 13:15) for the light's angle. Same output either way (`world/daylight.ts`, pure). |
| 2 | Where the zone and latitude are set | Client settings, in the **Settings** window since #91 (first in About, beside the limits switch): a timezone field (an IANA name, aliases such as `UTC` and `Europe/Kiev` accepted, validated by the `Intl.DateTimeFormat` constructor; numeric offsets refused: the setting asks for a place, whose summer time then applies; empty: the browser's zone, shown as "In effect") and a latitude field (−90…90, empty: the fixed curve). Commit on Enter or leaving the field, applied live; invalid input is refused under the field and not stored (`ui/timePrefs.ts`, `h3d.time.zone` / `h3d.time.lat`, every storage access guarded). `?tz=` and `?lat=` override them for a page load. |
| 3 | Pinning the hour | `?time=HH:MM` (today in the effective zone) and `?time=YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM`; a pinned time is frozen (the clock shows it, the light does not move) and starts there, no easing. Every e2e spec opens at `2026-09-30T12:00` unless it pins its own (`e2e/game.ts`), `make shot` included; the real-clock path is tested with `page.clock.setFixedTime` and `timezoneId`. `__h3d.daylight()` and `__h3d.clock()` let a test read the light and the clock without OCR. |
| 4 | Cadence | Read just past every minute (the clock turns on time), at once when a setting changes or the tab comes back; eased over about 3 s (exponential, landing on the target so a settled sky costs nothing). First load and a pinned time start at the target. |
| 5 | The baked street (open question) | **Runtime tint** of the baked street and facade by their material's colour, never a re-bake and no night bake. What glows is kept out of the tint: the lamp globes and the neighbours' lit windows are a baked mesh of their own, the lobby's room another (its own lights keep it bright: a milder warm tint), the fire-escape doors multiply from their day colour. The panes have their own node material, one uniform: by day their painted state colours as before; at night a running floor's panes glow warm and a sleeping floor's go nearly black, so the building shows which floors are alive. The street lamps come on with additive pools of light under them (one mesh, its opacity). |
| 6 | Office lights (open question) | A **constant light set**: adding, removing or hiding a light (or toggling `castShadow`) changes the lights' cache key and rebuilds render objects; intensity and colour, 0 included, are uniforms. The office surfaces are toon materials, which a RectAreaLight does not light, so the lamps are ****two** non-shadow spot lights** under the ceiling shining down on the desk area (a point light that high lit the 6.5 m ceiling more than the desks), full at night, 30 % by day, off on an asleep floor; no light per monitor, no new shadow caster. The ceiling panels, monitors and screens were already unlit colour and glow as before. **Measured** (`e2e/m17-perf.spec.ts`, perf-floor60: 60 workers, shadows on, WebGL2 on llvmpipe, 1280×720, looking across the desks and out of the windows; median serialized frame of 3 day/night pairs, two batches): origin/main 121.6 / 122.8 ms (its noon both times); **two lamps** noon 132.2 / 129.0 ms, 23:00 131.8 / 128.6 ms; three lamps noon 133.1 / 133.0 ms, 23:00 134.0 / 131.4 ms. Night costs what day costs (the same light set, the same 111 draws; origin/main 105: the lamps' glow mesh, the lobby's room, the pools and the clock). M17 as a whole is about +7 ms (+6 %) on the fallback; a third lamp adds about 3 ms more for a look two already give, so it stays at two. |
| 7 | Asleep and the time of day | They compose, neither setter overwrites the other (`WalkView.apply` reads both; the office's lamps read both): an asleep floor is darker than a running one at every hour (vitest over 24 hours, and the canvas luma in e2e: noon 190 vs 80, night 87 vs 21, running vs asleep). Day + asleep is the old asleep look; night + asleep nearly black. Noon awake is a little brighter than before (hemisphere 1.6, sun 2.1, from 1.5 and 1.9), the sun's noon direction unchanged in both scenes; the morning and evening sun swing around it. |
| 8 | The clock | A prop in `world/layout.ts` on the **board partition's north face** (the desks' side, 1.78–2.3 m, above the monitors, in view from every desk and from the mezzanine), on every floor. A digital face on a canvas (like the radio's display): HH:MM big, the date (`Wed 30 Sep`, English names whatever the locale) and a small sun or moon, in the effective zone, 24-hour, drawn only when that changes; backlit (basic material), so it reads at night. No analogue face. |
| 9 | Look-only props | `Prop.milestone: null` means the prop is only looked at: the bar shows its label and a line it is given (`Hud.setPropInfo(id, text)`; the clock's `14:05 · Wed 30 Sep · Europe/Brussels`) and no key, instead of "Placeholder until Mx". Not a special case of the clock's id. |
| 10 | Out of scope | Seasons beyond the latitude model, longitude, the receptionist's greeting by time of day, night-time crowd behaviour. |

## 2026-10-01 — #83: wrap up, then send home

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where does the verdict come from? | The agent's **transcript**, never a command, a token or the screen: the line it ends its final message with, `H3D-WRAPUP <nonce> CLEAN` or `… BLOCKED: <reason>`, read by `transcript.WrapUpTracker` from the same tail as usage and asks. Only lines from the armed offset, only after the bridge's own prompt (the first user prompt from there that ends with the nonce's footer; every later prompt is a person's, even one quoting the marker), only the last non-empty line of a turn's final assistant message, matched strictly against the raw line (only a trailing `\r` dropped: an indented, quoted, listed, fenced or space-padded marker is no result; a fence closes only as in CommonMark, on a run of its own character at least as long with nothing after it, indented up to 3 spaces; one after a list marker line, at or past its content indent, closes only at its own indent; when in doubt it is still open); the same marker anywhere else in that message is no result. A fresh nonce per arm kills stale and quoted lines. Codex and Claude only (`invalid_state` otherwise). |
| 2 | The offset | Taken by the usage tracker itself, after its read of that round (`Session.Delivered()`), before the prompt is sent: so the prompt is after it and no line from it on was read without the tracker. Restored after a restart, the tracker is attached before the session's first read (lines before the offset skipped); a tail already past the offset is void and needs you. |
| 3 | When it decides | The turn ended in the transcript and no line came since, herdr says `idle` / `done`, both for 2 s, no `ask`. `blocked` and asks wait. |
| 4 | What disarms | BLOCKED, no result, two markers, a time-out (`--wrapup-timeout`, 30 min), a lost transcript, a CLEAN whose re-check or send home failed: **needs you**, stored, its nonce dead. A person's `worker.prompt` / `worker.keys` while armed, or a prompt or interruption typed in herdr after the bridge's (seen in the transcript): disarmed, nothing needs you; also after a CLEAN while its tree check runs (until `beginFire`), and any other transcript line in that window makes it decide again. Answering an ask does not disarm (it is the wrap-up's own question). A reply while it needs you leaves it needing you: only "Wrap up again" (a new `worker.wrapup`) re-arms. |
| 5 | Teardown | `runFire`, the same code path as Send home, with the armed choice and `fireOp.strict`: a tree that changed while the agent exited fails it (`not_clean`, the worker stays) instead of sending it home with the worktree kept. Four clean-and-pushed checks before a removal: the arm, after CLEAN, and runFire's pair. Plain Send home keeps its `note` behaviour. |
| 6 | The dialog | A second action, "Wrap up, then send home", in the send-home dialog; delete preselected whenever `can_delete` (both actions); Enter with a delete opens an inline confirm, Cancel focused (a click, or Tab + Enter, confirms). The cleanup prompt is a plain `<textarea>`, not the #51 composer: the ticket wants Enter **and** Ctrl/Shift+Enter to add lines and only the button to send, which the composer's policies do not offer, and a cleanup task needs no screenshot. |
| 7 | On the desk | The worker's card carries a cardboard `WRAPPING UP` tape with a drawn box while armed; needing you, the card is red and its task line is the reason. The final toast follows `worker.removed` `sent_home` of a worker that was armed. |

## 2026-10-01 — #86: shadow acne from the moving sun

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Why vertical stripes since M17 | The sun used to stand in one direction that happened to spare the walls. With a latitude it sweeps the sky, so every wall spends the day at a grazing angle or with its back to the sun, and the shadow map striped the faces turned away from it (sofa, whiteboard, the board partition, the city's dollhouse walls): a constant depth `bias` only, no `normalBias`, and three's toon model (`ToonLightingModel`, the node path **both** backends run: `?backend=webgl2` is `WebGPURenderer` with the WebGL2 backend) steps `N·L * 0.5 + 0.5` to 0.7 / 1.0 without clamping at 0, so a back face still took 70 % of the sun **and its shadow term**. The e2e suite pins noon with no latitude (the fixed sun), which is why it never showed. |
| 2 | The fix | `render/toonSun.ts` replaces the toon material's lighting model once, for every `MeshToonMaterial` (it overrides `MeshToonNodeMaterial.prototype.setupLightingModel`; `ToonLightingModel` is not exported): the same 0.7 / 1.0 steps, but the light the step multiplies fades from the **unshadowed** light (`lightNode.baseColorNode`, before `ShadowNode` multiplied it) to the shadowed one over the first 0.08 of N·L. A back face is flat 0.7 and takes no shadow (the shadow lookup is still emitted, its result is multiplied by 0); a facing face is shadowed as before. Plus `shadow.normalBias` on both suns, `4 × half / 2048` (two texels of the orthographic frustum: about 0.024 in the office, scaled with the city's), the constant `bias` kept. Back faces stay flat rather than grey next to a lit wall: the whiteboard and the wall around it agree. A partition seen from behind is now the flat 0.7 tone (it used to be shadowed dark, striped at the edges). |
| 3 | Left as it was | The floor's shadows of desks, workers and mullions (they receive on a facing face; the edge moves a pixel or two with the normal bias); the shadow map size and the office frustum (no stripes remain at grazing angles with the bias). |
| 4 | Test | `e2e/m17-shadow-acne.spec.ts`: lat 51, 09:00 to 17:00 on 1 Oct, the issue's poses; `stripeScore` (`e2e/pixels.ts`, luma turning back on itself along the rows of a plain rectangle) over the whiteboard's margin, the partition, the sofa's back and the city's wall. The old code scores 10 to 150 where the new scores 0; the spec fails on it at 13:00, 15:00 and 17:00. |

## 2026-10-01 — #66: the main checkout herdr opens for a worktree hire

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Show it, hide it, or avoid it? | **Hide it** (the issue's option 2). `herdr worktree create` opens a workspace for the repository's main checkout when none is open on the floor; it holds a shell, no agent, and the player expects one minion per hire. Option 1 needs a new prop, option 3 needs herdr. |
| 2 | When is a workspace hidden? | A **stateless rule** on herdr's current state (`world.HiddenMainCheckouts`): a repository's main checkout (`Worktree` set, not linked, `RepoRoot` set), no agent, and a linked worktree workspace of the repository open on the same floor. Nothing is stored, so it survives bridge restarts; an agent starting in it, or the last worktree going, makes it a worker again by itself (`worker.upsert`). A shell the user opened in a main checkout is hidden by the same rule while worktrees of it are open. |
| 3 | Capacity | A worktree hire is **one** worker. `mainCheckoutOpen`, `hireOp.extra` and the "counts two" check are gone; the hidden workspace is not in `f.order`, so `counts`, `deskCount` and the caps never see it. |
| 4 | Cleanup | Sending home the last worktree of a bridge-hired worker (a `hire_branch` record exists), or rolling a worktree hire back, closes the hidden main checkout with `workspace close` once no linked worktree of the repository is open, only while it still has no agent. `workspace close` kills every process in the workspace, so the hub's last observation is not trusted for that: immediately before each close it re-reads herdr (`session.snapshot`, an allowed read, `Options.Fresh`) and skips the close (logged and audited) if an agent runs in the workspace, a linked worktree of the repository is open again, the workspace is gone, or the state cannot be read. Best effort: a failure is logged and audited, the send home still succeeds, and the workspace then shows as a worker. The workspace stays hidden while the operation runs (`holdRepos`), so it never flashes between the worktree going and the close. |
| 5 | `can_fire: false` | Stays for a main checkout that is visible (one with an agent) while worktrees of it are open. |

## 2026-10-01 — a laptop of a split pane is fullscreen (#74)

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What showed a split pane's laptop narrow? | Not the renderer: `terminal.watch` takes the pane's own size from `session.snapshot` and the observer does not reflow, so a pane in a 60-column split is 60 columns (the agent renders at that width). A single-pane worker is the tab's full width. |
| 2 | Ask or always? | **Always fullscreen**, decided after the research below: the game sends `fullscreen: true` by default and the bridge zooms the pane's tab with herdr's pane zoom (`pane.zoom` / `herdr pane zoom <pane> --on\|--off`) while a laptop sits at it, and puts it back on standing up. The issue's idea 4 had "a seat bar button, never automatic"; the seat bar keeps a visible, undoable fullscreen chip (and the preference `h3d.term.fullscreen`), but nothing waits to be asked. |
| 3 | What herdr 0.9.0 does (verified in throwaway sessions, report `ground-zoom-report.md` of the run) | Zoom is a per-**tab** boolean; the visible zoomed pane is the tab's focused one. `--on` is idempotent but always focuses the target (it moves the human's TUI focus); `--off` is idempotent. Splitting a pane or closing a sibling clears the zoom. A zoom survives workspace and tab switches, client detach and a server restart. `session.snapshot` keeps reporting the **split** rectangles of a zoomed tab; its layout has `zoomed`, `focused_pane_id` and the tab's `area`, which the bridge's `PaneSize` now reads. |
| 4 | The size of a zoomed pane | The tab's `area`. With a TUI attached the real terminal is about 3 columns and 2 rows smaller (area 114 × 49 gave 111 × 47: borders, scrollbar); the observer pads the difference and observer frames carry the requested size, not the real one, so there is nothing to correct the guess with. Padding is kept over subtracting: a guess that is too small would cut off the bottom rows, where the prompt is. |
| 5 | The headless limit | With **no client attached** a zoom resizes no terminal (open upstream issue herdrdev/herdr#1709): the bridge can flip the flag but cannot make the PTY wider. The snapshot has no field for the real columns either. The bridge calls it `headless` when the tab's area is herdr's headless 120 × 40 (a TUI window is never exactly that: the sidebar takes columns) and the seat bar says "Fullscreen unavailable" instead of promising a wide screen. The zoom is still applied: a TUI attaching later finds it. |
| 6 | Who owns a zoom? | The bridge remembers per (session, tab) that **it** zoomed the tab, counted by the fullscreen watches of that tab over all connections. It puts the tab back when the last one ends (unwatch, a re-watch without fullscreen, a dropped connection, a worker removed or its pane changed, the bridge stopping) and the tab is still zoomed. A tab that was already zoomed before the watch, or that the human unzoomed since, is never touched, and nothing is called for an asleep session. A re-watch keeps the zoom. `terminal.zoom off` is the player's explicit act and does unzoom a tab the human zoomed. Review rules (round 2): a watch counts as an owner only while it wants the tab fullscreen (`true`, or `terminal.zoom on`; a follower or one that turned it off is not counted, so it cannot keep the last real owner from restoring); the pane's state is read inside the write lock, before deciding to zoom, and again before a restore, which also checks the pane is still in the tab the bridge zoomed (a pane the human moved is left alone); a `terminal.zoom` is bound to its watch and does nothing once the watch ended. |
| 7 | Why never re-apply? | A human who unzooms or moves the focus while a laptop sits there meant it, and a bridge that zooms again would fight them every poll. The bridge reports the change (`terminal.state`, the zoom state and the new size, polled from the snapshot every 2 s while a watch that sent `fullscreen` lives, and the observer follows the new size) and the seat bar offers the re-apply. That includes a re-watch: the game re-watches for a new fps (reading the transcript) or after a frame gap, and such a watch takes over the decision of the one it replaces instead of zooming again, so only `terminal.zoom on` re-applies. |
| 7b | `fullscreen` is a tri-state | `terminal.watch.fullscreen` is a `*bool`: absent (an old client: no zoom, no polling, no `terminal.state`, exactly as before #74), `true` (zoom and own it) and `false` (no zoom, no ownership, but the watch follows the tab so the chip and the size stay true when the human zooms, moves the focus or resizes). The game sends `false` for the seated watch when the preference is off, and nothing for the ambient laptops. `terminal.zoom` stays the explicit act: it reports a herdr failure as `input_failed` (only the zoom at watch time is fail-open). |
| 8 | Why not move the pane to its own tab instead? | `pane move` changes the pane's id across workspaces and refuses a zoomed tab; it rearranges what the human built and cannot be undone by standing up. Zoom is a flag that is put back with the same command. Resizing a split (`pane resize`) steals the siblings' space. |
| 9 | Stop the splits at the source | The orchestrator's run prompt (`RunPrompt`, also what `run.prepare` returns) has one fixed line: every agent it hires goes in its own workspace or tab, never in a split pane of its own tab or another agent's tab, because a laptop shows a pane at its own size (a fullscreen terminal is wanted). It is there for every ticket kind. |
| 10 | The write path | Two new shapes in `controlShapes` (`herdr --session <s> pane zoom <pane> --on` and `--off`, nothing else: never `--toggle`, never without a mode), each write its own audit row `pane.zoom` (`session`, `pane`, `mode`, `why`), apart from the command's row. |
| 11 | When the zoom does not help | If the screen is still much narrower than the monitor (zoom off, or headless), the terminal's A+ / A− default fits the width instead of the height only (the letterbox is at least not tiny text), and the seat bar says why: "Split pane 60×40". |

## 2026-10-01 — #15 M13: the whiteboard (part A) and recent drops

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Commands or HTTP for the board's bytes? | **HTTP** on the bridge's socket for everything with bytes (the board, `save` with scene and render, image files, `render.png`, a drop's bytes, an agent's image pin), proxied by the helper like `/logo` and `/upload` (#51's decision row 1): a WebSocket message is capped at 256 KiB, a scene at 16 MiB. The ticket's "commands" `whiteboard.get` / `.clear` are WebSocket commands (no bytes; `get` answers the state, the content is `GET /whiteboard/{floor}`); `whiteboard.save{scene, files[], render}` is `POST /whiteboard/{floor}/save`, multipart `scene` + `render`, after the files went up with `PUT …/files/{sha256}`. `whiteboard.updated` is a sequenced event like any other. |
| 2 | One save, or the scene and the render separately? | **One request**: the scene and its PNG are one commit, so a render never belongs to another version than the one it says (`render_version`). The files go first and are content-addressed, so an interrupted save leaves at most an unreferenced file (collected after an hour). |
| 3 | Concurrency | A floor revision compare-and-swap: a save names its `base`, a stale one is `409 whiteboard_conflict` with the current version and nothing is stored. The client then merges (fetch, `restoreElements` on the remote alone, `reconcileElements` into the live scene with `CaptureUpdateAction.NEVER`) and saves again. Per-element last-writer by Excalidraw's own rules (higher `version`, then lower `versionNonce`), not text merging (research `excalidraw-data.md` §7). Tombstones are stored, or a delete would come back from another client's copy. |
| 4 | What is stored of appState | Only Excalidraw's own `export`+`server` keys (`viewBackgroundColor`, `gridSize`, `gridStep`, `gridModeEnabled`), checked, on both sides. Never selection, zoom, tool, menus, collaborators. |
| 5 | File ids | The sha256 (hex) of **exactly the stored bytes**, checked by the bridge (`422` otherwise). Excalidraw hashes a pasted file before it scales it to 1440 px (`generateIdForFile` gets the original), so `generateIdForFile` gives the original's sha256 (Excalidraw's own dedupe of a re-paste still works) and, before each save, the editor re-hashes each file's bytes and re-files a mismatch under its hash (`newElementWith`, `NEVER`); a type the bridge does not store (SVG, BMP, AVIF…) or a file over 8 MiB / 1600 px is redrawn as PNG (JPEG stays JPEG) first. The same picture pasted or pinned twice is one file. |
| 6 | Where the boards live | `--whiteboards` (`HERDR_WORLD_WHITEBOARDS`), default `$XDG_DATA_HOME/herdr-3d/whiteboards` (else `~/.local/share/…`): files, not SQLite (scenes are large JSON, files are bytes; the version history is a directory of 20 files). `head.json` is written last and a commit only adds files until then (the version, its files list, its own render `renders/<n>.png`), so a crash leaves the previous version current with its own render; what the new head no longer names is deleted after it. A floor name is a herdr session name, one path component (`TestFloorNamesAreSessionNames`); the store's files are opened `O_NOFOLLOW`. *(Superseded in part by #58 stage 2, row 1: a board is a floor id's, a session name or a virtual floor's `v-<12 hex>`.)* |
| 7 | History and collection | The last 20 versions; a file is deleted when no kept version uses it and it was last written over an hour ago (it may be on its way into a save). Each kept version's files are listed in `v/<n>.files.json`, so collecting reads no scene (they were in `head.json` first, which grew with them past the 1 MiB it is read with: 900 images over 18 versions bricked the floor). Clear is a new version of tombstones (an open editor deletes what it shows), with no render; older tombstones are dropped then. |
| 8 | Caps | Scene 16 MiB / 20000 elements, file 8 MiB, a floor's files 256 MiB, render 16 MiB and 2400 px a side, note 4 KiB / 200 lines; the helper lets a save through up to 32 MiB + 128 KiB and a file up to 8 MiB + 4 KiB. Elements are checked for shape (id, known type, version, finite coordinates within ±10⁷, valid index, hex file id), not for every Excalidraw invariant. |
| 9 | Agents' pins in Go | `whiteboard.Store.Pin` writes complete 0.18.1 elements (every field; image `status: "saved"`, text in Excalifont 20 with `autoResize`) with a real fractional index after the highest one, tombstones included (`AppendIndex`: `generateKeyBetween(last, null)`, checked against 6008 vectors that `indexVectors.test.ts` recomputes with the fractional-indexing Excalidraw ships). A note's width is a guess: the editor measures it with the font loaded (`restoreElements` with `refreshDimensions` after a tiny `exportToCanvas` loads the face) before it renders. `goPins.test.ts` feeds the Go-made scene through Excalidraw's `restoreElements` and requires the pins unchanged. A repeated `request_id` pins once. |
