A sleeping floor's screen says Asleep. |
| 4 | Opening | **E at the screen** (it grows out of the screen's rectangle as projected on your view), **O** on an office floor wherever you stand (out of the centre), and the top bar's **Overview** button next to Issues / PRs (office floors only): over 1500 px wide with its word and an O key cap, then an icon (its title says O), and at 1280 px and under not at all, so the floor's name and counts keep their room (the M3b rule); the floor's hint line says "O overview" at every width. In the city or the lobby O toasts "Walk onto an office floor to see its overview." (the hire's wording). O was free at floor level. |
| 5 | Full screen | A fixed **`inset: 0` layer above everything**, the top bar included, no gutter (the whiteboard's pattern, M13 row 16), **never the Fullscreen API**: the shell is a Chrome `--app` window already, and fullscreen Chrome takes Esc for itself, which Esc closing the page needs. On open the walker is released and stopped, the background is `inert`, keys are taken in the capture phase, `App.dialogOpen` is true (so `onKey` and the frame loop's `blocked` see it), and `CornerWatch` counts `.ov[open]` as a screen (the limits plate and the inbox chip step aside). Esc runs the grow in reverse, back into the wall screen when it was opened there and the screen is still in view. The grow is a clip-path (FLIP-style without scaling the text). |
| 6 | Live | The page is redrawn in place on every world change while open (keyed rows: the same element, its place kept): a status change flashes the row in its new colour once, a new now line flips in. **The order never follows a status**: orchestrators by desk, each with its freelancers by round, role (code, review, ux, ground) and desk; ghost rows; Unlinked; everyone else by desk. |
| 7 | The tree's edge cases | A freelancer here whose orchestrator is on another floor goes under a muted ghost row "Orchestrator <name> · Floor <x>" (its click goes there: `goToWorker` of the orchestrator); one on another floor whose orchestrator is here stays nested with a floor tag (`Floor <n>` in the same building, else the session); one with no orchestrator, or one that is gone, is in an **Unlinked** group. Collapse state is per orchestrator id (and `ghost:<id>`, `unlinked`) in `localStorage` (`h3d.overview.collapsed`, the collapsed ones only, at most 200; guarded like `inboxPrefs.ts`), every group expanded by default. A collapse folds the group's rows up under it (height, padding and opacity, with a slight spring lift, the last row first; out of the tree and the tab order at once), an expansion draws the connectors in; calm (reduced motion, battery saver): at once. |
| 8 | Colours | **The shared status palette** (`STATUS_CSS`, hud.css `--working` …): the overview defines no hue of its own. Working stays **blue** (the top bar's), green is done: no game-wide recolour. The ink is the top bar's navy taken deeper (`#0b1022`): every status light is ≥ 3:1 on it and on a hovered row, its text ≥ 4.5:1, white on every chip (`STATUS_CHIP_CSS`) ≥ 4.5:1 (vitest). Asleep is an unlit lamp (a ring), its row's text a tone down by its own tokens (never an opacity over text: each stays ≥ 4.5:1, vitest); needs you pulses a ring; working blinks with a glow. The board's accent is the game's sun yellow (focus, ticket keys, the floor's name). |
| 9 | Type | The floor's name in condensed Archivo (its width axis, self-hosted from the shipped Fontsource package), ids, times and counts in Departure Mono (the Terminal preset's): both already in the credits. Counts are split-flap tiles; rows read like departures: the light, the name, the ticket, then a lighter now line, `since` right-aligned. |
| 10 | A row's actions | Click or Enter: `App.goToWorker(id, 'transcript')` (the inbox's go-to: the page closes at once, no shrink, you sit at the laptop with the conversation on screen); O brings the page back over it (the transcript's reading keys take O, not its prompt box or any text field), still seated: Esc closes it back onto the conversation. A ticket (`#123 · <title>`, the bridge's title; the game never builds a provider URL) opens the in-game issue window (`boardsPanel.openItem('issues', …)`) **over the page**: the background is live while that modal is up and it owns the keys (Esc closes it, not the page). Keys: ↑/↓ and j/k move, →/← expand and collapse (← on a freelancer goes to its orchestrator), Space toggles, `t` the ticket, Shift+→ / Shift+← all, Esc closes. |
| 11 | The lines | **Task** for one-off workers (no run); **Now** for every worker with a turn read (the digest's `now_source` present; the task goes out from the first prompt, before any turn): a finished freelancer's report summary, else the digest's recap, else the worker's terminal title; with the live #98 activity chip beside it. A line whose source is `llm` gets a small "AI" marker (none exists yet). Since is `status_since` (`working 4m`). |
| 12 | The digest (bridge) | Per worker, on the usage tracker's line tap (`transcript.DigestTracker`, Claude and Codex): the first genuine prompt and a ring of the last 5 assistant steps (texts, tool name and input summary; never tool output or thinking), reset on `/clear` and on another agent session. Phase 1 sends only the fallbacks (`protocol.md` "Floor overview digest"): task from Claude's `ai-title`, else the prompt's first sentence (Codex: the prompt); now from a recap still current (no prompt, turn start or step after it: event-based, no timer), else `now_source: "title"` with no text, so the hot `worker.status` path stays the title's. The task goes out as soon as it is known (the first prompt is enough), the now line (`now_source`) only from the first assistant step. Changes go out in `worker.upsert`, rarely. `llm` is reserved for phase 2. |
| 13 | Ticket titles (bridge) | `issue` and `work_item` carry `title` and `url` from the floor's board cache; a miss is read once with the provider and remembered (hit or miss) for the bridge's life; a poll that changes a card refreshes its workers (`protocol.md` "Ticket titles"). In memory, not SQLite: a restart reads each missing item once more. |
| 14 | Empty floor | "Nobody's here yet" with a Hire button (the hire dialog at the first free desk); the wall says how to hire. |
| 15 | Not in phase 1 | `internal/digest` and any LLM call, its Settings section, `digest.test`, `HERDR_WORLD_DIGEST_ENABLED`, its feature state in the snapshot (phase 2); an overview of all floors or the city; acting on workers from the page. |

## 2026-10-03 — #118 (phase 2): floor overview summaries

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where | **`internal/digest`**, on `ai.LLM` only (`TestDigestUsesOnlyAIInterfaces`: no vendor client, no `net/*`, nothing of the bridge, not `transcript` nor `activity`): the prompt, the scrub, the cleaning, the keys, and the `Service` (switch, test gate, one-call slot, daily cap, cache). The hub (`internal/server/digest.go`) decides who and when and hands it plain `Input`s built from the tracker's digest. |
| 2 | What runs, for whom | A **task** line for one-off workers (no `role`) from the first prompt, once per prompt hash (again only after `/clear` or another agent session: the tracker's reset); a **now** line for every Claude and Codex worker from its last 5 steps, with a context line where it helps (`Ticket #n: title` for a run's worker, `Task: <task line>` for a one-off). Orchestrators get the ticket too: their now line reads better with it. |
| 3 | When | The now line after a turn ends (`TurnDone` / `TurnComplete`, counted by the tracker as `Ended`), **2 s** settled (the follow-up questions' wait), at most once **60 s** per worker: a turn end within the gap waits for it and then summarizes the steps of then. The hub arms one timer for the earliest line that waits (not the usage rounds: a pump from a client stepping onto a floor or a call ending must not depend on the next round having work). |
| 4 | Only viewed floors | The activity rule, `floorViewed`: a client's `board.watch` (sent for the floor you stand on, board or not) or a laptop or transcript it watches there. The wall screen and the page are on the floor you stand on, so they count without a new signal. A line due on a floor nobody is on waits, and costs nothing until someone walks onto it. |
| 5 | Cost limits | **One call in flight** for the feature (its own slot, `ErrBusy`; `digest.test` waits for it), so the AI integration's two (`ai/service.go`) always leave one for the follow-up questions and pack up. A **daily cap**, default **300**, settable 1–10,000 (`daily_cap`), counted per call whatever its outcome, saved apart from the settings like the activity's count. A **cache** of lines by the SHA-256 of the kind and the text as sent (`digest_cache`, the newest 2,000, SQLite): a restart re-reads every transcript, and every line it already wrote costs nothing. Only the hash and the line are stored. |
| 6 | Off by default, the gate | The followups/packup pattern: its own switch, off; enabling needs the LLM `ready` (`incomplete`) and a passing **`digest.test`** of the LLM in use within 30 minutes (`test_required`; the pass is the LLM's fingerprint). The test writes a sample task and now line; it passes when both come back with a line. `HERDR_WORLD_DIGEST_ENABLED` / `--digest-enabled` wins, read-only in the panel, and still needs the LLM `ready`. State `off \| incomplete \| ready` in the snapshot's `digest`, `digest.update` on a change. A reached cap stays `ready` (cached lines still apply); the panel says so. |
| 7 | Model lines and fallbacks | A model's line replaces the fallback (source `llm`) only while the feature is `ready`: switched off, or the LLM paused, every worker's digest is composed again at once and phase 1's lines are back exactly. A failed call or an answer without a line keeps the previous model line, else the fallback. A Claude recap **newer** than the model's now line shows instead. A model's now line goes with a reset. |
| 8 | Scrub and prompt | Every piece sent goes through `digest.Scrub`: a copy of the activity's token patterns (GitHub tokens, AWS key ids, JWTs, a URL's password; `TestDigestScrubCoversActivity` keeps them equal, since the pin forbids importing `activity`) and `ai.Scrub` (keys, bearer tokens). Followups and packup send less and do not; this sends transcript text. The instruction is the system turn and calls the transcript data, never instructions; the data alone is the user turn between `<transcript>` delimiters (a closing one inside is defused). `max_tokens` 80, temperature 0.2; the answer is cut to its first line, stripped of fences, markdown, labels, bullets, quotes and the full stop, scrubbed, clipped to 80 / 100 runes. |
| 9 | Audit and logs | One `digest.summarize` row per call (client `bridge`): worker, kind, `sent_bytes`, `answer_bytes`, model and the endpoint's host; an error as its kind only. No text, ever. The log has the failure's kind only (a provider's message may echo the transcript). Cache hits are not calls: no row. |
| 10 | The game | A "Floor overview summaries" section after "Classify unknown commands" (the AI features in the order they came), on the follow-up section's classes: what it needs (the LLM pill), exactly what is sent and where above the switch, today's calls against the cap (the activity's ledger and hairline), the cap, Test with both sample lines. The page's "AI" mark (phase 1) now shows; the wall draws the same outlined "AI" before a line the LLM wrote part of. |
| 11 | Review round 1 | **Scrub, then clip** (R1): every piece, a tool's name included, is scrubbed whole, then clipped, then scrubbed again: clipping a step's text to its last 600 bytes first could cut a credential's label off and send the credential. **A reset is an identity** (R3), never guessed from the counts: the usage tracker gives each digest tracker of a worker an epoch (a new one for another agent session) and the digest counts its resets (`Resets`, a `/clear`); the hub starts a new generation (hub-wide, so a worker's entry made again cannot match an old one) when either changes, drops the model's task and now line, and ignores a completion of the old generation. A `/clear` and the same prompt within one poll, or another session with equal counts, now reset. **The cap defers** (R4): past it the pump sends only cached lines and leaves the others due, armed to wake at the next local midnight; a cap raised or the toggle cycled pumps at once; a call that met the cap anyway (`ErrCapped`, like `ErrBusy` and not ready) gives its task or now line back. Only a call made counts its input as tried. **The cap's copy** (U1): new calls stop until tomorrow, the lines written stay and an input seen before reuses its line (not "fallbacks until tomorrow"). The failure log keeps the kind only (R2). |

## 2026-10-05 — #138: the bridge translates a prompt's completions per harness

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the rules live | `internal/harness/translate.go`: a pure `Translate(kind, text, cmds, resolveFile)` and a **harness-keyed table** (`harnessRules`: Codex only; a harness with no row gets its text untouched, so Claude text passes through byte for byte). The server reaches it only through `server.PromptTranslator` (`Options.Prompts`, wired by the app with the #136 catalog); `TestPromptRulesAreInTheHarnessTable` keeps `server`/`store`/`proto` off `internal/harness` and the harness names out of `translate.go` and `ask.go`. `internal/harness/prompts.go` adds the I/O (catalog, checkout stat) around the pure function. |
| 2 | What Codex gets | `/name` becomes `$name` only when `name` is a **skill** of the Codex catalog (a built-in never; a name that is also a built-in never) and the `/` starts a word (start of text, after whitespace or an opening quote or bracket). The whole name token is looked up (letters, digits, `_ - . :`), so `/zzprobe-two` is never read as `/zzprobe`; trailing `.` `:` `-` are tried off the token (`use /review.`). `/usr/bin/env`, `http://x/y`, `a/b`, `/name/more`, `//name`, an unknown `/foo` stay. `@path` loses its `@` only when the `@` starts a word and `path` is a file or directory **inside the worker's checkout** (`harness.InCheckout`: lexically under it, then again after `EvalSymlinks` of both, as `internal/imgcache`'s `within`/`checkPath`; `..` and a symlink out do not resolve; relative or absolute). `@someone`, `a@b.com`, a missing path stay. A trailing `.,;:!?)]}'"` is prose, tried off the token. An `[Image #n: path]` marker (#64) is skipped whole. A rewrite never makes the text longer. |
| 3 | Paths with spaces | **Composer rule: a file with spaces in its name is written `@"path with spaces/file.txt"`; one without, `@path`.** Checked by hand (claude 2.1.289, codex-cli 0.160.0): Claude attaches `@plain.txt` and `@"path with spaces/file.txt"`, but neither `@path\ with\ spaces/file.txt` nor the unquoted form (no-tool controls: `NO-ATTACH`). So Claude's text goes through untouched, and whoever composes a chip must already write it quoted. Codex reads a plain path from prose (`Read path with spaces/file.txt …` read the file; a typed `@path` reaches the model literally, with no attachment), so the translator takes the quoted mention whole and writes the plain `path with spaces/file.txt`. An unquoted `@a b.txt` is `@a` and a word: it is resolved as `a` (usually nothing) and stays. |
| 4 | Spike: do Codex built-ins run when pasted? | **Yes**, when leading: `herdr agent prompt` of `/compact`, `/review`, `/status` and `/new` ran them (compaction started; the review preset picker; the status card; the new-conversation picker), and a typed `/status` ran too; `/status` in the middle of prose goes to the model as text. So the built-ins stay in the catalog and **no typed fallback** is added for Codex. `/status` and `/new` answer `agent_prompt_stalled` although they ran (no `working` state follows a UI command): the retry (one Enter) is harmless there; it is not made smarter. |
| 5 | Spike: is a `$skill` picked up mid-sentence? | **Yes**: `please run $zzprobe now`, a leading `$zzprobe now`, `use $zzprobe.` and `$zzprobe-two` each injected the skill (`skills.selected_skill_instructions` in the rollout); `$zzprobe-two` did not inject `zzprobe`. A leading `/zzprobe` is `Unrecognized command`: the rewrite is needed. |
| 6 | A prompt ending in a `$skill` | Codex's skill popup stays open after a prompt that **ends** in a skill token (`$zzprobe`, `use $zzprobe.`) and takes the submitting Enter: `agent_prompt_stalled`, the token left in the composer; `esc` then `enter` submits it. The Codex row's `retryKeys` answers `esc enter` for a text whose last word is `$name` (plus prose punctuation), a lone `enter` otherwise (`harness.RetryKeys`, `PromptTranslator.RetryKeys`); `h.prompt` and the Codex branch of `worker.answer_ask` use it. `esc` is sent only on a stall, never before the first submit, and **not when the worker started meanwhile**: before the retry keys go, the worker's state is read again, and when it is working, blocked or asking, or its start counter moved since the prompt began (`promptState.started`), nothing is pressed and the prompt counts as started (live Codex aborts the turn on Esc, `docs/research/transcript-activity-jsonl.md`). `retryEnter` (worker.prompt) and `submitAfterAsk` (worker.answer_ask) both do it. |
| 7 | Which routes translate | **worker.prompt** and **worker.answer_ask's `prompt`** (what a person typed in the composer): translated on the worker's input lane, just before the text is sent, direct and queued (`h.prompt`) alike. **Not translated** (the bridge wrote them): `worker.answer_followup` (built from the answers' fields), `worker.wrap_up`, a hire's first prompt, run prompts. `h.prompt` itself never translates, so those routes cannot start to by accident. |
| 8 | Limit and fail-open | The text is translated before the 16 KiB limit and `checkTyped` are applied to it, for a harness that rewrites (**one shared bound before translating, `maxPromptSource` = 4 × 16 KiB = 64 KiB, for both routes**: no rewrite shortens a text past a quarter, the worst being `@"a"` to `a`, pinned by `TestTranslateNeverShrinksPastAQuarter`, so a text whose translation fits is never refused and a larger one is never translated; `worker.answer_ask` translates and checks its `prompt` before it touches the dialog, so a refusal leaves the question as it was); a harness that does not is checked at once, as before. Lookups run off the hub goroutine (the lane) under a 5 s bound (`translateWait`), only for what the text needs (no catalog without a `/`, none for Claude); when the catalog or the stat fails, or the worker is gone, the text is sent **as typed** and the bridge logs the kind of the failure, never the text. |
| 9 | The transcript view | A Codex worker's transcript is read from its rollout, which holds what Codex received: the view shows `$skill-name` and the bare path, not what was typed (accepted by the owner). The game's pending row for a prompt, though, must still go when its item comes: the `worker.prompt` ack carries `sent` (the text the bridge sent, only when it differs from the typed one), the row keeps it, and a rollout `user` item matches the typed **or** the sent text; an item that comes before the ack is matched again when the ack lands (queued prompts alike). Keeping the typed text needs a stored original that survives re-parsing (the transcript log is rebuilt from the file on a page, an item and a restart), matched back to the rollout's user line: a store, a new option on `transcript.Log` and both log-opening paths. Not done in this ticket; the Claude view is unaffected (its text is untouched). |

## 2026-10-05 — #147: the command palette takes Ctrl+P from the seated terminal

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The chord | **⌘P on a Mac, Ctrl+P elsewhere** (`mod` + P, scope `any`, `typing`), from every view: a window capture-phase listener beside the help key's, so it also fires seated, on the whiteboard, in a field and over a dialog. It is always `preventDefault`ed: a page can cancel the print dialog (what a page cannot cancel is ⌘/Ctrl+W, T, N, Q), so the table's "browser-reserved chords" list loses P. No Ctrl+Shift+P alias: Firefox keeps it for a private window. |
| 2 | Ctrl+P seated | **Taken from the terminal**, as VS Code's integrated terminal does. The cost: readline's previous-history (and Claude Code's, fish's and zsh's Ctrl+P) is gone in a seated pane; **↑ still does it**. The alternative, leaving Ctrl+P to the pane and opening the palette only after Esc, is what the `?` help key does and what the ticket wanted not to repeat: a palette you must stand up to reach is not a palette. On a Mac nothing is lost: ⌘ was never the pane's. Ctrl+P on a Mac is still the pane's. `laptop/keys.ts` `seatedKey` returns the `palette` action for the chord (nothing is sent), the table has a readonly `seat.palette` row, and `shortcuts.test.ts` keeps the two in step. |
| 3 | Floor-scoped commands | Hire, Issues, Pull requests, Start a run and Overview work on `hereSession()`. Off an office floor (the city, a lobby, the street) the palette does not toast "Walk onto an office floor": Enter shows the floor list under "Hire on which floor?" and the pick rides there and runs it. On a floor it runs at once for that floor. |
| 4 | Floor order | Floors that need you first, then the rest, each by "Building › floor" without regard to case. A query filters and never reorders, and the order is fixed when the list opens or the query changes: counts update live and rows do not jump (the rule of `world/overview.ts`). |
| 5 | Shared code | The listbox keys and the active-row paint left `ui/composer/completion.ts` for `ui/listbox.ts`, used by the composer's popup and the palette's plain `<input>`. The #140 ranker moved from `ui/composer/rank.ts` to `world/rank.ts` and now also starts a word after a space and `›`. |
| 6 | Over the image previews and a card in the air | **It opens**: the issue's "every view" has no exception, and the previews' own Esc is not what the chord asked about. The palette is the topmost modal; the previews' (and the whiteboard's) window capture-phase handlers skip any key from inside `dialog.palette` (`inPalette`), so Esc closes only the palette and the preview stays up. A card mid-drag is put back with no drop (`BoardsPanel.cancelDrag`) as the palette opens: a drop landing under a modal would surprise. (The `?` help key keeps its own exception for these.) |
| 7 | The plan-limits plate | **Its edge tab over the palette**, as over any screen dialog (the user's call): `dialog.palette[open]` joins `SCREEN`, the palette is `topScreen()`, and `tabFit()` measures it alone, so even over the hire sheet (no gutter of its own) the tab shows, inside the topmost modal and clickable. The palette is 640 px at most and never nearer than 56 px to the right edge; it needs no top offset any more. Shift+L is not a peek key there: it types an L. |


## 2026-10-06 — #154 (phase 1): the Codex terminal beside a second app-server client

Findings, evidence and the fixture: [`docs/research/codex-shared-tui.md`](research/codex-shared-tui.md)
(`make codex-shared-tui`, `internal/codexshare`). Codex 0.160.1, Herdr 0.9.0. The fixture uses mock inference; one
bounded real-model smoke test (findings §9) ran the phase-2 launch, a per-worker server on the real `~/.codex`, through
Herdr only, and never connected to the user's shared daemon.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does the terminal still work with a shared server? | **Yes**: the native TUI with `--remote unix://…` keeps typing, slash commands, skills, Ctrl+C, approvals, exit and `codex resume ID --remote` working, and Herdr's start/prompt/read/wait/send-keys and working → idle/done detection with it. A second client shares the thread, and its async-question replies are rendered and resolved natively (per question, on delivery). The real model behaved as the mock did: working → idle on every turn, across a tool call and a 25 s command, `agent read`, Ctrl+C (during the model phase; interrupting a running command is unverified) and `resume --remote`. Its two new breaks are in the launch and resume arguments, not the terminal (rows 2 and 6). |
| 2 | Server ownership | **One app-server per Codex worker, started from the worker's own pane** after fish and direnv have run, then the TUI in that pane (`setsid -f fish -c 'codex app-server --listen unix://SOCK'`). The CODEX_HOME daemon is never used: it runs every thread's tools and MCP children in the environment of whoever started it, and plain `codex` already auto-starts it (`daemon_auto_start`). Starting from the same pane is also what routes Herdr's session-id hook, which runs in the server, to the right pane. A new worker cwd must be trusted before the TUI starts: Codex's "Trust this folder?" dialog shows even with `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`, and `agent start` then fails with `agent_not_ready`. Hire keeps its trust handling, or the server gets an in-memory override for that one cwd (`codex app-server -c 'projects={"<cwd>"={trust_level="trusted"}}' …`, which leaves `config.toml` alone). A launcher never sends Esc or Ctrl+C to a dialog it did not expect: on this one they open Codex's Agent command center, which lists every thread of the server's CODEX_HOME. |
| 3 | Lifetime | The bridge owns each worker's server: it records socket and pid and stops the server on send-home, fire and floor sleep (it outlives its pane and `herdr session stop`). It reports a dead server, which Herdr shows as `working`. |
| 4 | Subscriptions | `thread/resume {threadId}` without overrides, only while watching; `thread/unsubscribe` after. The thread id comes from Herdr's `agent_session`; ephemeral side threads are skipped. Herdr clears that value when the TUI exits and, after a resume, reports it again only with the first turn, so the bridge keeps the id it last saw and never reads a missing one as a new thread. |
| 5 | Approvals | The bridge stays passive and never answers a server request: the first answer wins. |
| 6 | Resume | Herdr's own resume drops `--remote` and the workspace env. herdr-3d resumes shared-mode Codex workers itself: server first, then `codex resume ID --remote … -C …` with hire's model args **minus `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`**. A remote resume refuses any permission override ("Permission overrides are not supported when resuming a remote task"), Codex exits, and `agent start` only reports `timeout` after its full timeout. The thread keeps the permissions it was started with; `-m` and `-c model_reasoning_effort=…` are accepted. A restarted server needs the cwd's trust again: the in-memory override is per server (row 2). How to keep Herdr's native resume off such a pane is phase 2's first check. |
| 7 | Questions | Reply with the native envelope: `turn/steer` (with `expectedTurnId`) during the asking turn, `turn/start` after it. The terminal can no longer answer once that turn has ended, and Herdr shows a pending question as `blocked`. The server does not deduplicate, so the bridge must. |
| 8 | Terminal-free mode | Documentation only: a separate, persisted Settings opt-in with its gaps (no Herdr agent control, no TUI commands, the bridge answering approvals). The terminal stays the default. |

## 2026-10-06 — #155 (phase 2): Codex workers and the bridge on one local app-server

Built on #154's decisions (rows 1–8 above). Protocol: [Shared Codex server](protocol.md#shared-codex-server). Code:
`internal/codexapp` (adapter, launcher, process ownership, checks), `internal/server/codexserver.go` and
`codexfloor.go`, four new `herdr.Control` shapes. Evidence (raw captures recorded during #154/#158, not published; regenerate with
`make codex-shared-tui EVIDENCE=<dir>`; conclusions in `docs/research/codex-shared-tui.md`):
`TestCodexShareResumeSuppression`, `TestCodexShareSharedLaunch`, `TestCodexShareSharedApprovals` (mock model) and
`TestCodexShareRealModelSmoke` (real model). Codex 0.160.1, Herdr 0.9.0.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Step 0: how to keep Herdr's own resume off a shared pane (D5) | **The TUI's own exit before the session stops.** `herdr pane release-agent <pane> --source herdr:codex --agent codex`, with the TUI running, changed nothing: the pane kept its `agent_session`, `session.json` kept the id and Herdr resumed the pane its own way on the next start. Leaving the session unreported is not possible: Herdr's SessionStart hook reports it by itself. After the TUI's exit the pane has no `agent_session`, `session.json` does not name the thread and nothing is resumed (`TestCodexShareResumeSuppression`: `rel`/`ex`/`ctl`). So the bridge's sleep quits each shared TUI (`/quit`, Enter: never Esc or Ctrl-C) and keeps the thread id itself. Not a different design from #154's: the documented fallback, minus the exit on wake, which stays as the safety net (a Codex Herdr resumed without `--remote` is quit, then relaunched). |
| 2 | The quit-to-stop race | Herdr forgets the pane's session **a moment after** the TUI quits; a `session stop` before that saves the id, and Herdr would then run a plain `codex resume` on wake, which starts the user's CODEX_HOME daemon (B2). The sleep waits (up to the send home's exit timeout) until the bridge sees no agent and no session on the pane, and notes it when it does not. The real-TUI suite caught this; the real-model smoke refuses to restart the session if `session.json` still names the thread. |
| 3 | Opt-in and modes (D8) | One bridge-wide switch, `codex_server.set`, off by default, persisted; Settings → "Codex workers". It applies to **new hires**. Each Codex hire made with it on records its launch (`codex_launch`: shared, or legacy and why), and keeps that mode: switching never touches a running worker. A Codex hire made with the switch off writes **no** record and runs byte-for-byte as before (legacy launch, Herdr's own resume, the sleep backfill). |
| 4 | Launcher (D1, B2, B3, B10) | From the worker's pane after its shell is up: the bridge writes `<--codex-run>/<marker hex>.fish` (0600 in a 0700 directory) and types `setsid -f fish <launcher> …` (`pane run`). The launcher marks the server's processes with a per-launch random token (`H3D_CODEX_SERVER`), records its pid and runs `codex app-server -c 'projects={"<dir>"={trust_level="trusted"}}' --listen unix://<socket>`: the directory trusted in memory (no "Trust this folder?" in a fresh directory with the real model; `config.toml` unchanged). Then the socket must answer with a supported server and the TUI is `agent start … -- --remote unix://<socket> -C <dir> -m … -c model_reasoning_effort=… --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`. The only shell-typed variable is the launcher path, under `CodexRun` and of plain characters; `-C` takes plain paths only. |
| 5 | Failures stay usable (acceptance 5) | Before the TUI: an unsupported `codex --version` (outside 0.160.x) or `userAgent`, a blocked or denied `.envrc` (`direnv status --json`), a path the launch cannot pass, a server that does not answer in 20 s: whatever started is stopped and the worker starts **the usual way**, the reason in the hire's ack (`codex.note`), its toast and the laptop's seat bar. A TUI that does not attach stops the server and falls back when the pane is at its shell, else the hire fails and rolls back. Codex's "Hooks need review" dialog (B8) is left to the player (answering it trusts hooks for every thread of the CODEX_HOME); the hire's first prompt is then not sent and says so. |
| 6 | Ownership and lifetime (D2, B5, B6) | The bridge owns the server: send home stops it after the agent exited, the sleep stops it after the TUI quit, a failed hire stops it. "Stopping" signals only this user's processes whose environment carries the launch's token (SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 2 s), then removes the launcher, socket (and the socket Codex may link it to), pid file and log. A dead server (pid gone, or another process under that pid: the start time is recorded) is `server: "down"` on the worker and on the seat bar, checked every 3 s; sleeping and waking the floor starts it again. |
| 7 | Resume (D5, B4, B9) | herdr-3d resumes shared workers on wake, alongside Herdr's own resumes: the pane at its shell, the server started again (a fresh token, the trust again), then `agent start <name> … -- resume <thread> --remote … -C … -m … -c model_reasoning_effort=…` without the bypass flag. Real model: 4.1 s, the history shown, the next turn ran and Herdr reported the same thread. The worker's `resume` is `fallback` ("relaunched"). |
| 8 | Thread identity (D3) | The thread is Herdr's `agent_session` for the pane, kept in the bridge's record: an empty value (the TUI exited, or a resume before its first turn) changes nothing, a new one is taken (a `/new` in the terminal), one that is another worker's thread is refused. The bridge never picks a thread among the loaded ones. Two workers in one session: two servers, two threads, never swapped (`TestCodexShareSharedLaunch`). |
| 9 | Worker-stop vs client-detach | **Worker stop** (send home, sleep) ends the server and everything it runs. **Client detach** (the transcript closed, the bridge reconnecting or gone) is `thread/unsubscribe` and closing the bridge's own connection: the terminal's subscription and the turn in flight go on (`TestCodexShareSharedLaunch`: a terminal turn after the bridge left). |
| 10 | The bridge's join and approvals (D3, D4) | `codexapp.Watcher` joins while the worker's transcript is open: `initialize` as `herdr_3d` (experimental API on, the `userAgent` version checked), `thread/resume {threadId}` with nothing else, `thread/unsubscribe` on leaving, reconnecting with a bounded backoff (0.25 s doubling to 5 s, 8 attempts in a row). It has no way to answer a server request: approvals and blocking questions are observed (`serverRequest/resolved`) and left to the terminal (`TestCodexShareSharedApprovals`: the modal stayed open until the terminal's `y`). Joining made no model request (mock) and started no turn (real model). The join also brings `thread/goal/cleared`, `account/updated` and `thread/tokenUsage/updated` notifications: the server's state for a new client, not changes the join made. This phase stops at the join and its events; questions and answers are phases 3–4. Rollouts stay under `~/.codex/sessions` (D6): the transcript tail is unchanged. |
| 11 | Orchestrator-created workers (D7) | Unchanged in this ticket: they start through the Herdr CLI and stay embedded. The procedure is the same two calls the bridge makes (`pane run <pane> '<server start>'`, then `agent start … -- --remote unix://<socket> -C <dir> …`); a shared helper on PATH that also checks direnv is the way the write-code skill adopts it. |
| 12 | Review round 1: send home never Ctrl-C's a shared worker (R1) | A shared worker whose terminal does not quit after `/quit` and Enter (up to three more) is **left as it is**: the send home fails ("no Ctrl-C to its shared Codex terminal … quit it there and send it home again"), nothing is closed and its server keeps running. Legacy workers keep the two Ctrl-C. Rejected: stopping the server instead, since a TUI whose server is gone does not exit but waits to reconnect (B6); reading the screen for a state where Ctrl-C is safe, since the only such state (an idle composer) is one `/quit` already handles. |
| 13 | Review round 1: wake without a thread (R2) | A shared worker that slept before Herdr reported its thread (Codex's hooks review, or no turn yet) is relaunched on wake like the others: its server, then a **new** TUI attached to it (`agent start … -- --remote … -C …`, hire's arguments). No thread is resumed or guessed. |
| 14 | Review round 1: wake without its server (R4) | When the server does not start on wake (an unsupported version, no answer, no run directory) or the terminal does not attach and the pane is at its shell, the worker resumes its thread on Codex's **embedded** server with hire's arguments: `agent start … -- resume <thread> -m … -c model_reasoning_effort=… --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` (new shape `resume-codex-embedded`; any `-c` keeps a plain Codex off the CODEX_HOME daemon, B2; the bypass flag is refused only by a *remote* resume, B9). Mock model, real TUI (`TestCodexShareSharedLaunch`, "wake without its server"): the shape resumed the thread with its history, the next turn ran, Herdr reported the same thread, no `app-server-daemon` was started, and Codex's own startup warning confirms B2: "Running without the shared background server: command-line configuration overrides (-c …) requires embedded mode". Without a thread it starts the way a legacy hire does. An embedded Codex may show "Trust this folder?" (no in-memory override there): it is answered as a hire answers it. **The worker stays shared**, with the reason as its note and `server: "stopped"` (the seat bar: "No own Codex server this time … the next wake tries its own again"): flipping it to legacy would hand its next wake to Herdr's own argument-free `codex resume`, which joins the daemon. Its next sleep quits the TUI as for any shared worker, and its next wake tries its server again. |
| 15 | Review round 1: a failed join is kept (R3) | A join that failed (its retries spent, or refused for good: ephemeral, another thread, an unsupported server) stays failed for that thread on that server (the launch's token): not tried again on the next tick. Closing and reopening the worker's transcript, another thread, or a new server (a wake, a relaunch) try again. Events are tied to the join that sent them, so a join that was left can no longer mark its successor detached. |

## 2026-10-06 — #156 (phase 3): Codex async questions and the bridge's answers

Built on #154 (findings §1, §2) and #155 (rows 8–10, 15). Protocol: [Codex async questions](protocol.md#codex-async-questions).
Code: `internal/codexapp/questions.go` (the reconciler), `reply.go` (the envelope, moved from the fixture, and its
parser), `watch.go` (the join's history, `Steer`/`StartTurn`), `internal/server/codexquestions.go`. Evidence:
`TestCodexShareAsyncAnswers` (captures not published; findings §11; mock model, real TUI). Codex 0.160.1.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Seeing a question while the transcript is closed (D3) | **The bridge joins every shared worker's thread while its server runs and its thread is known**, transcript open or not (amends #155 row 10). Joining is passive and cheap: no model request, no turn, no setting changed (`Lifecycle/join-settings`, and the real model in #155). The join is also the only way to *answer* and the only source of the running turn the steer needs, and a prompt to a worker herdr shows `blocked` needs it attached the moment the prompt comes (row 10). Rejected: reading the rollout's `event_msg/item_completed` (`internal/transcript/codex.go` keeps hiding it), a second source with its own deduplication that enables nothing the join cannot; and joining on demand when a question is seen, which leaves the prompt refused for the seconds the join takes. |
| 2 | Failed joins (amends #155 row 15) | A join refused for good (ephemeral thread, another thread, an unsupported server) is kept as before: tried again when the transcript is opened, the thread changes or the server is new. One that only spent its retries (8, backoff to 5 s) is also tried again after 60 s (`CodexRejoin`): with the join no longer tied to the transcript, nothing else would ever retry a worker whose transcript is never opened. |
| 3 | Batch identity | A batch is an `agentMessage` with `delivery: "async"` and `questions`; its id is the function `call_id`, equal live, in `thread/resume` and `thread/read` history and after a cold resume (§2 [R]; `AsyncAnswers/30-d-hydrated`). The bridge never assumes it: live and history that disagree stay explicit. `twin`: two ids, same turn, same questions, each seen only one way (both kept and answerable); `conflict`: one id with different questions (the first kept, not answerable). Neither was observed. |
| 4 | Recovery | Every (re)join reconciles the history with what the bridge knew: the turns `thread/resume` returns, plus one page of up to 50 older turns (`thread/turns/list`, `itemsView: full`) when the response has a `turnsBackwardsCursor` (a paginated thread, `Lifecycle/16-cold-resume`). Older batches are not recovered (bounded joins). Batches first, then envelopes, so an answer never waits on its batch; an envelope for a batch not seen yet is kept (64 at most). |
| 5 | States | Per question: `pending`, `sending` (on the input lane, request in flight), `sent` (the server accepted it), `answered` (a user message whose whole text is the reply envelope naming `(call_id, index)`, live or in the history: the one rule for the bridge's and the terminal's answers), `dismissed` (the bridge only). The tool's `{"accepted":true}`, the turn ending and other user messages change nothing (§1 [R], `TestAsyncQuestionSurvivesTheAcknowledgement`). Kept per worker and thread in the hub, not in the join: a reconnect, sleep and wake keep them; a new thread (`/new`) and send home drop them. A bridge restart keeps only what the history shows (`answered`). At most 32 batches per thread. |
| 6 | Who answered | The bridge's sends carry `clientUserMessageId: "herdr_3d-<24 hex>"`; Codex echoes it as the user message's `clientId`, live and in the history (`AsyncAnswers/09-*`, `30-d-hydrated`: `by: bridge` from the history alone). `answered_by` is `bridge` for that prefix and `other` for anything else (the terminal's uuid, a client that names none). That is a proven pairing and it survives a restart; nothing else is inferred. |
| 7 | The answer command | `worker.answer_async {id, thread, batch, answers}`: checked when it arrives, queued on the worker's input lane (the one `worker.prompt` and `worker.keys` use), and checked again there in one hub step that also marks the questions `sending`: any other submission for them made before that step is `stale` from then on, whatever comes of the first (a generation captured when it arrives, `Questions.Admit`/`BeginAdmitted`; `TestAsyncAnswerSerializedOnTheLane`, `TestAsyncAnswerQueuedTwiceIsNotARetry`). The bridge must be attached (`invalid_state` otherwise: never queued blind). A question with options takes one of them exactly; one without takes any non-blank text. Whether the terminal accepts free text on a question that has options is unverified, so the bridge is strict. |
| 8 | Steer or start, and the completion race | A running turn (as the join last saw it): `turn/steer` with `expectedTurnId`; none: `turn/start` with the input only. The server refuses a steer that cannot land with nothing delivered: "no active turn to steer" and "expected active turn id … but found …" (`-32600`, `AsyncAnswers/12-*`, `13-*`). Any such refusal (a JSON-RPC error response) falls back to `turn/start` **once**. A `turn/start` while a turn runs is accepted into that turn and returns its id (`14-*`, `15-*`), so the fallback never opens a second delivery path. No response (a lost connection, a timeout) is `sent` with `unconfirmed`, never re-sent: the server does not deduplicate (§2). A refused `turn/start` is `input_failed`, the question `pending` with the reason. |
| 9 | The race phase 1 left open: steer accepted, then the turn ends first | **Dropped.** A steered input becomes a user message only when Codex delivers it with the next model request (`07-*` none before, `09-*` at delivery). Interrupted before that request, the turn ends `interrupted`, no user message is recorded, nothing reaches the model and the next turn does not carry it (`AsyncAnswers/18-*` to `27-*`); the terminal's badge goes with the turn. Encoded as `sent` + **`undelivered`** (set when the turn the answer reached ends without its user message, live or in the history): never re-sent by itself, and the user may answer it again (`28-*`: the second send is answered): only a submission made after it went undelivered; a duplicate queued before is `stale` (row 7), not a retry. This is what a fresh join already shows (`pending`: no envelope in the history). |
| 10 | Prompts stay usable (B7) | herdr refuses `agent prompt` while Codex's async question shows the worker `blocked`. A `worker.prompt` to a shared worker that is blocked, attached, with a running turn that asked a question still not answered, and **no server request open**, is steered into that turn (as Codex's own composer queues a message during a turn), with the same one fallback; ack `queued: true, via: "app_server"`. An open approval or blocking question is a real block: refused as before. Such a worker's screen is not read as a `question`. The legacy blocking ask is unchanged: `request_user_input_async` is not `request_user_input` (`TestAskTrackerIgnoresCodexAsyncQuestions`), and `internal/transcript/ask.go` and `internal/server/ask.go` are untouched. A terminal-only built-in still has to be typed into the terminal, so it stays refused. |
| 11 | What phase 4 (#157) consumes | `worker.codex.thread`, `.turn`, `.questions` (batches with `seen`, `twin`, `conflict`, `at`; questions with `state`, `answer`, `answered_by`, `unconfirmed`, `undelivered`, `error`, `truncated`), `worker.answer_async` (ack `sent` / `via` / `fallback` / `unconfirmed`) and `worker.dismiss_async`; the TypeScript types are in `game/src/net/protocol.ts`. The game's cards, and how a blocked worker with only async questions counts, are #157's. |

## 2026-10-06 — #157 (phase 4): answerable Codex async question cards in the transcript

Built on #156 (row 11: what phase 4 consumes) and the findings of §1 and §11 (docs/research/codex-shared-tui.md).
Protocol: [Codex async questions](protocol.md#codex-async-questions) (`async_blocked`). Code:
`internal/server/codexserver.go` (`codexWorkerView`), `game/src/world/codexQuestions.ts` (the rules, pure),
`game/src/laptop/asyncCard.ts` and `asyncDrafts.ts` (the card and its drafts), `game/tools/fixture-codex-questions.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Telling an async question's `blocked` from a real one | **The bridge says so: `worker.codex.async_blocked`**, its `asyncBlocked` predicate (attached, the running turn asked a question Codex still holds, no server request open), the one a prompt already takes the app-server on (#156 row 10). The game cannot derive it: open server requests (approvals, blocking questions) are not on the wire. It is part of the worker's `codex` view, so `refreshCodex` re-emits the worker on every flip (`TestAsyncBlockedFlagFollowsThePredicate`: a question, an approval opened and resolved, the envelope, the turn's end, the join lost). It does not read `status`; the game combines them. No other bridge behaviour changed. |
| 2 | How such a worker counts | **Working.** `effectiveStatus` (`status: "blocked"` with `async_blocked`) is the one rule: the store's display (so the counts, N's queue, the inbox, notifications, the HUD, the desk card and the crowd), the P overlay (`promptBlocker` lets the prompt go and `willQueue` says it is queued, as the bridge acks it), the crew lifecycle, and the seated transcript's status (no screen-question banner, the live cue keeps saying what it does). A real block (the flag false) is exactly as before. |
| 3 | The "has a question" cue | **How many questions want an answer**: `pending` (a refused one with `error` included) and `sent` + `undelivered` (dropped: only the user can send it again). Not `sending`, plain `sent` or `unconfirmed` (a reply of ours is on its way, or its fate is unknown and it is never sent again), not `answered`, not `dismissed`; a twin pair once. The desk card says "Codex asks N questions · answer at the laptop (E)" with the first question, its status chip still WORKING; the look-at bar has "asks N questions" in the working blue. |
| 4 | An inbox row? | **No.** The inbox is "needs you or done", and an async question does not need you: Codex works on and reads an answer whenever it comes (until its turn ends; after it, only the bridge can answer, §1). A row would either count as needs-you (wrong) or need a third kind of row and notification for something non-blocking. The cue is on the desk card and the look-at bar instead. |
| 5 | Activity while herdr says blocked | **Known limit, left as is**: the bridge composes `activity` only for `working` (activity.go), so the desk monitor shows the terminal's last lines instead of the activity card while an async question is open. The transcript's own cue is derived in the game from the effective status, so it keeps updating there. Changing the bridge's activity rule was out of #157's scope (one flag, nothing else). |
| 6 | The card | **Non-blocking**, modelled on the follow-up card: the prompt box stays on, the terminal is a click or Ctrl+Esc away. The ask card's family (stacked questions, key caps, the foot) in the working blue (`--tx-sun` re-pointed on `.tx-aq`): solid like the ask (Codex's own questions, not inferred), not the sun of "needs you". One batch open, the others folded to a line, oldest first; ← → walk every question across batches. A question with options takes one of them (the bridge is strict, #156 row 7), one without a text box. Picking or Enter never sends; Send or Ctrl/⌘+Enter sends the answered questions of the open batch, once while it is on its way (a per-batch in-flight set until the ack or the command's end: a dropped connection settles it as `disconnected`). That set is the card's only guard. **The bridge is the duplicate guard** (#156 row 7: it marks the questions `sending` on the input lane, and any other answer submitted before is `stale`), and the card draws what it is given: the store's latest `codex` once a frame (`codexSeen` skips an unchanged one), so it may never see `sending` or `sent` (a hidden tab draws no frames while its socket goes on, review round 2). A guard waiting for a step it may not be shown would keep a legitimate retry (not delivered again, `pending` after a restart) disabled for good; without one, a send acked before its step is drawn (about a frame) can go again, and its `stale` is said calmly ("Already sent, answered or hidden — nothing more went to Codex"), the answer kept, no error (`asyncCard.retry.test.ts`: real store, controller and view). A blocking ask hides it, and the screen question's digits, the ask's and the follow-up's keys go first. |
| 7 | What the card says | **Only what the bridge has seen** (§11): "Sent — Codex will read it with its next step" (accepted is not read), "Sent, not confirmed" (no resend offered), "Not delivered — the turn ended before Codex read it" (the answer kept, Send again), "Answered" / "Answered elsewhere" (`other` is any client but herdr-3d, not necessarily the terminal), a refusal in words with the answer kept. Hide calls `worker.dismiss_async`: "Hide these questions on this card. Codex still has them: nothing is sent to it", never cancel; a hidden one is listed again, read-only, on demand ("N hidden"), and dismissals are not kept in the game (after a bridge restart they show again, legitimately). A `conflict` batch, or any while the bridge is not joined, is read-only: "Answer in the terminal while it shows them". So is a question whose suggested answer came shortened (`truncated`, an option within a character of the bridge's 1000-byte cut): the bridge checks a reply against the whole option, which the game never saw. A batch answered while you looked stays until you clear it ("Clear"); one answered before is not shown. |
| 8 | Drafts | **Above the view** (`AsyncDrafts`, held by `TranscriptMode`; the terminal toggle and a re-seat remount the view): `Drafts('codex-async')` keyed by worker, thread, batch and index, so a refusal, a drop, an upsert, a reconnect's snapshot, standing up and the toggle keep them; a draft goes when its question is seen answered or the thread changes, never on a missing `codex`. Batches are keyed by thread + batch, so a replayed snapshot is the same card. |
| 9 | Fixture | The fixture server makes a new simulated world per WebSocket connection, so `simcodex=ask` seeds Pixel (demo-m5) on every connection: a reconnect's snapshot replays the batch, which is what the e2e checks (one card, the draft kept). `sim.codex` asks batches (twin, conflict), changes the launch, opens an approval (a real block) and arms a failure (`input_failed`, `stale`, `unconfirmed`, `undelivered`); `sim.codex_answer` is the terminal's answer. |
| 10 | The follow-up card and the async questions' card at once | **They take turns.** An idle shared Codex worker keeps the async questions its ended turn asked (the turn's end answers none) and can get a follow-up (#90) as well; on a phone's screen (under 300 px) two cards left about 3 px of question room each. With both up, one is open and the other a tab away: a tab strip ("Open questions", "Codex asked", each with its count) over them, the card that was up first stays open (both at once: the follow-up), a click or Enter on a tab opens its card with the keyboard on it, ← → on the tabs switch. The card behind is only not drawn, so its picks and text stay. The timeline's digits, ← → and Ctrl/⌘+Enter go to the open one. On a very short screen the open tab names the card, so its own top line goes and its questions keep a lone card's room; on a laptop's screen it keeps its top line. Everywhere, not just on phones: one rule, and two full cards squeezed the conversation out on a laptop too. **Where a card goes when there is no room beside the conversation is measured, not a height** (review round 2: seated on a phone, then resized to a 303 px screen, the strip, the tabs, a card and the prompt box overflowed it; the timeline's padded scroller painted over the tabs and the prompt box went off the screen). The view sets `data-tight` while a pinned card is up and, laid out without it, the conversation keeps less than a line, the prompt box goes off the screen or the card's questions get under 64 px; always on a very short screen (≤ 300 px, the phone's layout as before). Tight, the card takes the room of the timeline, of the strip and of half the prompt box, and scrolls as one between its sticky top line and foot. No height could say it: the strip wraps to two rows on a narrower screen, the zoom grows the card and not the strip, the tabs come with a second card. It is measured on the layout without it, so the answer never depends on itself, and only when the screen's size or a card changes (a `ResizeObserver` on the view, whose size is its screen's). A new size puts the focused answer back in sight. The timeline also clips to its box (`overflow: clip`). 1280×720 and 1920×1080 keep the card beside the conversation; 960×600 with both cards (19 px of conversation left) is tight. |

## 2026-10-07 — #158 (phase 5): terminal/transcript parity validated through the bridge, opt-in rollout

The epic's last phase. Evidence, fixture and the requirement ledger: findings §12
([`docs/research/codex-shared-tui.md`](research/codex-shared-tui.md#12-phase-5-full-stack-validation-158)),
`make codex-shared-bridge`, `internal/codexshare/bridge*_integration_test.go`. Rollout and the resulting architecture:
architecture.md "Shared Codex workers". Codex 0.160.1, Herdr 0.9.0; mock model, one bounded real-model run
(`gpt-6.1-sol`, low effort, two turns, 34,218 tokens), and the UX review's real-game run (three turns, same model).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What counts as evidence | **The full stack**: herdr-world itself (`app.Serve`) aimed at a throwaway Herdr session, driven over its WebSocket as the game drives it, against the real Codex TUI. Assertions on the bridge's acks, events and `worker.codex`, the TUI's screen and the mock's classified requests; "one model-visible answer" counted in the model's whole input, never in acks. Phase 1–3's protocol-client suites stay as they are. |
| 2 | Where the suite lives | `internal/codexshare`, integration-tagged test files that import `internal/app` and `internal/proto`. Test imports are in no package's graph, so `codexshare` is still imported by nothing and every `layer_test.go` tripwire holds; `internal/app`'s `make integration` (real cheap models) is left alone. Rejected: a suite in `internal/app` importing `codexshare`, which would break the package rule. |
| 3 | Fixture deviations | Workspaces without `.envrc` (the preflight reads the bridge's own allow list); the bridge's own processes get the fixture's `CODEX_HOME`; a floor wake starts the fixture's own session server in place of `systemd-run`'s login environment, so no woken pane can reach `~/.codex`; the hooks review answered as the player would. Each is said in findings §12. |
| 4 | The default | **Stays off.** The switch is the rollout: opt-in, new hires only. Nothing in #158 flips it. |
| 5 | Rollback | Switching off changes **later hires only**: a running shared worker keeps its server, join and answers (verified), and its mode for life. Back to the usual launch for one worker = send it home, hire again. Switching on again changes only later hires. |
| 6 | Unsupported Codex | The existing note is actionable as it is (`… unsupported Codex version: "codex-cli 0.161.0" (supported: codex-cli 0.160.x); started the usual way`): it names the version found, the supported line and the outcome, on the ack, the toast and the seat bar. Not changed. |
| 7 | Found: a terminal whose server died could not be put to sleep | Such a TUI only waits to reconnect and ignores `/quit` ("ctrl+c quit" in its footer), so the sleep left it, herdr resumed the pane its own way and the wake came back without a server (`resume: failed`, `agent_name_taken`). **Fixed**: one Ctrl-C after `/quit` failed, **only** when the worker's recorded server is gone (pid gone or reused); send home treats such a worker as a legacy one. Nothing to interrupt is left once the server is gone, and the #155 R1 rule (no Ctrl-C into a live shared terminal: B10's command center) is unchanged. Unit tests in `codexserver_test.go`; the full-stack scenario now recovers by sleep and wake. |
| 8 | Both views answering one question | **A known limit, documented.** Answered in the terminal and from the transcript before delivery, both reach the model (the server does not deduplicate; the bridge cannot see the terminal's queued answer), and the bridge shows the one delivered last. Every other duplicate (two clients, a resend while sending or sent, after answered or dismissed) is refused `stale` and never reaches the model. |
| 9 | Approvals and images on a shared worker | Recorded, not changed: hire's bypass flag means a hired worker is never asked for an approval (an escalation is refused by Codex); images reach Codex as their path in the text on both paths, as for every Codex worker. |
| 10 | Delivered vs answered, for users | `sent` is the server's acceptance; `answered` is a reply envelope for that question seen in the thread (live or history), with `answered_by` only from the bridge's own `clientId`. Stated in architecture.md and unchanged in the protocol. |
| 11 | Found in review: a duplicate answer after a bridge restart (R1) | The bridge sent an answer with `turn/steer` (`sent`), herdr-world restarted before the turn's next model request delivered it, and the player retried. A steered input is not a user message in the history until delivery, so the restarted bridge saw the question `pending` and took the retry: two envelopes, two answers the model sees. **Fixed by recording the bridge's sends** (`codex_async_send` in the bridge's store, one row per question: worker, thread, batch, index, the `clientUserMessageId`, the turn it was steered into or reached, confirmed / unconfirmed). Written on the hub before the request goes out, updated with the response; on every join `Questions.Restore` only turns a **pending** question into `sent` (its turn still running: a retry is `stale`), `sent` + `undelivered` (its turn ended without it: one resend, #156 row 9), or `sent` + `unconfirmed` (no response came). A `turn/start` never confirmed was not delivered (its user message would be in the history) and stays pending. *(Wrong, row 17: one taken into a turn the terminal started is not in the history until that turn's next model request; it is now `sent` + `unconfirmed`.)* Rows go when the question is answered (live or in the history), the send is refused, its batch is gone, the thread changes or the worker is sent home. The reconciler's rules are unchanged: only the envelope in a user message answers, `answered_by` from the client id's prefix. Rejected: re-sending nothing after any restart (a stuck question needs the terminal), or reading Codex's queued steers (none of `thread/resume`, `thread/read` or `thread/turns/list` shows a queued steer: `BridgeRestartSent/1-raw-history-active-*`). |
| 12 | A dismissed `sent` question across a restart | **Its row is kept** (unlike the brief's suggestion to drop it on dismissal): dismissals are the bridge's only and a restart forgets them (#156 row 5), and the dismissed answer may still be queued in the turn. After a restart the question shows `sent` (or `undelivered`), not `pending`, so it cannot be answered twice. |
| 13 | Found while fixing R1: `thread/resume` gives the newest turn under legacy ids | To a client that was not subscribed (a restarted bridge), Codex 0.160.1's `thread/resume` gives the thread's **newest turn, running or interrupted**, as its legacy history builder makes it: items `item-1`, `item-2`, …; the async question there is not under its `call_id`. `thread/read` and `thread/turns/list` give the `call_id` (`BridgeRestartSent/*-raw-history-*`). Before the fix a restarted bridge showed the batch as `item-2`, `pending`, beside the live batch's records, so a retry was admitted under an id Codex does not know. **Fixed in the join** (`codexapp.Watcher`): the turns `thread/resume` gives with `item-N` ids are read again with `thread/turns/list` (newest first, as many as needed, at most one page) and put in their place; a failure keeps resume's turns, and the bridge treats that history as unresolved (row 15). Findings §2's "ids are the same on every path" is corrected. |
| 14 | The real game against a real TUI and model | Closed by the UX review's run (findings §12.5a [R-real]): the browser game, the real herdr-world, Codex 0.160.1 and three `gpt-6.1-sol` low turns, a private CODEX_HOME. The model read the card's answer during the asking turn and after an idle `turn/start`; the terminal's answer showed "Answered elsewhere". **Advisory, not changed:** at 390 × 844 the card's Clear button (59.56 × 26.25 px) and the folded batch button (28.25 px tall) are below a 44 px touch target (`game/src/laptop/transcript.css:2114`, `game/src/laptop/asyncCard.ts:740`). |
| 15 | Review round 2: when reading the legacy ids fails (R1 reopened) | A join whose `thread/turns/list` failed, or had nothing for the newest turn, kept resume's `item-2`; the restore found no `call_3` and **deleted its record**, so a later join that read `call_3` made it `pending` and admitted the same answer again (two envelopes, one turn). **A record goes only on positive evidence**: the question answered (live or in the history), the answer refused, the thread changed, the worker sent home or no longer shared, or its batch unknown to a **resolved** history. A history is unresolved while a batch is under an `item-N` id; then a record whose batch it does not show is kept, on the join and in `pruneSends` (a live change) alike. *(Superseded in part by row 16: this round read "unresolved" from the batches the view kept (`Questions.Unresolved`), which eviction and dismissal change, and `pruneSends` still dropped a record whose batch the view lacked.)* Such a batch is **refused** (`ErrUnresolved` → `invalid_state`, the message naming the id and the terminal): an envelope naming `item-2` would answer nothing. It stays **visible**, not hidden: Codex holds it, and it keeps `async_blocked` and the prompt route through the app-server (#156 row 10) true. The repair is **retried by joining again** after `CodexRejoin` (60 s, the timer of a join that gave up, #156) while the history stays unresolved: a new `thread/resume` and `thread/turns/list`; each join first drops the last one's `item-N` batches (they are one join's numbering, never answerable, so nothing was sent for them). Bound: one record per question answered, gone by the causes above; no age cap. Rejected: failing the join itself when the read fails (the whole view goes read-only and prompts lose the app-server route for a transient read), and hiding `item-N` batches (a worker shown blocked with no card). What remains is in findings §12.1a: waits, except a record SQLite failed to write, lost with the process. Tests: `TestQuestionsLegacyIDsAreUnresolved`, `TestAsyncAnswerKeptThroughAFailedLegacyRepair`, `TestAsyncAnswerKeptThroughAnUnmatchedLegacyRepair`. |
| 16 | Review round 3: the view is not evidence (R1 reopened) | With 31 older unanswered batches, dismissing the unresolved `item-2` and one more question evicted it (32 batches per thread, dismissed first); the view then looked resolved, `pruneSends` deleted the record of `call_3` while its answer still waited in `t1`, and the canonical rejoin admitted the same answer again. The same follows without any legacy id from a `sent` batch evicted by 32 newer questions. Row 15's claim that an evicted batch cannot be answered later was wrong. **Invariant: a record goes only on positive evidence that its answer can no longer be delivered, or was; absence from the in-memory view is never evidence.** So: (a) an envelope naming the question drops its record **from the envelope's text** (`codexapp.Answers`), live or in a join's history, whatever the view kept; (b) `pruneSends` drops only a record of another thread or of a question the view shows `answered`; (c) "unresolved" is the **join's** (`codexapp.LegacyHistory` of the turns it read, kept on `codexWatch`), so eviction, dismissal and live events cannot clear it; (d) a join drops a record whose batch it does not have only when its history is the whole thread (`Thread.Partial` false: the Watcher read every older turn, from `thread/turns/list`'s `nextCursor`) and resolved; a batch the history has but the hydration evicted keeps its record. **The record outlives eviction, the batch does not** (chosen over protecting such batches from eviction: the view and the snapshot stay bounded at 32, and a dismissed or undelivered answer could otherwise pin batches indefinitely); an evicted batch cannot be answered from the bridge (`stale`) and its record comes back with it when a join (or a live event) shows it again. Eviction never drops a batch whose answer is `sending` (its request in flight; one per worker, the input lane is serial), so a join cannot mistake an in-flight `turn/start` for one never confirmed. Dismissal keeps the record (row 12). Records that never resolve stay until the thread changes, send home, or the worker is no longer shared at startup: one per question answered, no age cap. The unresolved rejoin stays one join per `CodexRejoin` (60 s) per worker. Every path is listed with its evidence in findings §12.1a and driven by `TestAsyncSendRecordInvariant` (21 rows); also `TestAsyncAnswerKeptThroughAnEvictedLegacyBatch` (the reviewer's scenario), `TestAsyncAnswerKeptThroughAnEvictedBatch`, `TestAsyncAnswerKeptThroughADismissedEvictedBatch`, `TestQuestionsKeepABatchWhileItsAnswerIsSending`, `TestWatcherHistoryPartial`. The one duplicate window left is still a record SQLite failed to write. *(Wrong, row 17: a join still dropped a `turn/start` recorded but never confirmed whose envelope the history lacked, which a turn the terminal started at the same instant can hold; closed there.)* |
| 17 | Review round 4: an unknown outcome is not non-delivery (R1 reopened) | The bridge chose `turn/start` from its idle view while the terminal started `t1`; Codex took the input into `t1` and held its envelope until `t1`'s next model request (findings §11 `b-start-while-active`). herdr-world stopped after recording the send (no turn, not confirmed) and before the response: the join's history had `t1` running without the envelope, `Restore` dropped the record (rows 11 and 16: "its user message would be in the history"), and a retry put a second identical envelope into `t1` (reviewer-run, server overlay and Codex 0.160.1 with the mock model). A steer into a turn that ended meanwhile has the same window: refused, it falls back to `turn/start`, recorded under the steered turn, and was restored `undelivered` (answerable). **A send recorded but never answered by the server, whose envelope the history lacks, is restored `sent`, `unconfirmed` (the existing "Sent, not confirmed") and keeps its record**: never sent again by the bridge, the terminal can answer it, and the existing evidence (its envelope live or in a history, thread change, send home, not shared at startup, its batch missing from a whole resolved history) drops it. A steer recorded into the turn still running keeps row 11's rule (`undelivered` once that turn ends without it): a fallback `turn/start`, if any, joined that same running turn. Cost: a send whose request never left the bridge, or that Codex dropped, can no longer be resent from the bridge after a restart (the terminal answers it); chosen over a second model-visible answer. No other change. Tests: `TestAsyncAnswerQueuedIntoAConcurrentTurnSurvivesARestart` (both paths), `TestAsyncSendRecordInvariant` (22 rows; "a turn/start never confirmed" moves to the kept rows), `codexapp.TestQuestionsRestoreASendAcrossARestart`, and on real Codex `TestCodexShareAsyncStartIntoARunningTurn` (the reviewer's probe, ported: the model sees the answer once). The one duplicate window left is a record SQLite failed to write (findings §12.1a). |

## 2026-10-07 — #183: Codex async answers read as question and answer in the conversation

Follow-up to #157 under #153. Code: `internal/codexreply` (the envelope), `internal/transcript/codex.go` (`codexAnswers`), `proto.TranscriptItem.Answers`, `game/src/laptop/transcriptRender.ts` (`answerRow`), `transcript.css` (`.tx-ans*`); tests `internal/codexreply`, `internal/transcript/codex_answers_test.go`, `transcriptRender.test.ts`, `e2e/h183-answers.spec.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the envelope's rule lives | **`internal/codexreply`, standard library only**: `Answer`, `ReplyEnvelope`, `ParseReply`, `AsyncTool` moved out of `codexapp`, which keeps them under the same names (`type Answer = codexreply.Answer`, thin delegates; `TestReplyNamesAreCodexreplys`). The transcript needs the same rule and must not link the app-server client (`codexapp` imports the WebSocket library and launches processes). One rule, so the reconciler and the transcript agree on what an answer is; no duplicate to keep in step. |
| 2 | Recognising it | In `codexMessage`, a user message whose text parts, joined as `codexapp.Item.UserText` joins them (no separator), are one complete validated envelope. Not a regex that strips tags: a prompt that quotes an envelope, text around it, a cut or malformed one, another tool's id, an empty list, one bad entry among good ones, a message with an image: all stay ordinary bubbles, content whole. |
| 3 | The wire shape | `answers: [{question, answer}]` on the `user` item, in order; **`parts` keeps the envelope as sent** (one plain text part: an `[Image #n: path]` inside an answer is not turned into an image). So an older client degrades to today's bubble, `itemKey` / pending-prompt matching and the image-only test are untouched, and the raw view needs nothing more on the wire. Ids (`questionItemId`, call id) are in no `answers` field; they remain only in the raw text, as in the rollout. |
| 4 | The entry | Your bubble (same container as any prompt), a small "Your answer" label, each question as quiet context, the answer in the stronger weight; several answers in order, separated by a hairline. **Plain text** (`textContent`, `white-space: pre-wrap`, `overflow-wrap: anywhere`), not Markdown: what you typed (`**x**`, `<b>`, quotes, Unicode) is what shows, and nothing can inject. The raw reply is a native `<details>` "Raw reply", closed. No "sent / seen / read" wording: the entry says what was answered, not that Codex acted on it (delivery stays on the question card, §157 row 7). |
| 5 | Duplicates and history | No optimistic echo exists for `worker.answer_async` (the card is the status surface; `PendingPrompt` rows come only from `worker.prompt`), so nothing is added twice; the entry is built from the transcript item's text alone, so pagination, reopen, reconnect and a question already gone from `worker.codex.questions` all render it, at the item's own place. |
| 6 | Out of scope | Inter-agent NOTE messages; the digest's "first prompt" for a thread whose first input is an envelope (unchanged). |
| 7 | What "validated" means (fix round 2) | Per entry: a `questionItemId` string that parses as `["request_user_input_async", <non-empty id>, <integer ≥ 0>]` (a null, string or missing index is refused), and `question` and `answer` keys that are **JSON strings** (`json.RawMessage` plus a type check: a missing key and `null` are not empty strings; an empty string is valid, so a blank answer is still an answer). **All or nothing**: one entry that fails makes the whole text not an answer, so the user's own text stays visible in an ordinary bubble and never shows as a blank "Your answer". Unknown extra keys are **ignored** (unchanged from before, and as the TUI's own parser does): the raw reply keeps them. The rule is `codexreply.ParseReply`'s for the reconciler too (`Observe`, `Answers`), so a half-formed envelope also answers no pending question; Codex and the bridge always write all three fields, so no real answer is refused. `isAnswers` (TS) re-checks the string fields and falls back to the plain bubble. |
| 8 | Contrast (fix round 2) | "Your answer" and "Raw reply" use `--tx-text-2` (they were `--tx-text-3`, 3.66:1 on the Quiet user bubble, below 4.5:1); the question is the same colour at a larger size, the answer stays strongest. |

## 2026-10-07 — top bar: the bar says where you are, the Menu holds the commands

The bar had grown to a dozen buttons (Issues, PRs, Overview, Elevator, Hire, Run, Share, Settings, About, sound, battery) and cut the floor's name to "he…" at 1509 px, while the one thing the user wants to see, which floor they are on, was a line of small grey text. Every command already has a key (docs/shortcuts.md) and a row in the command palette. This supersedes the top bar's width rules of M13 row 11 (the share's words going at 1500 px), #118 row 4 (Overview's word, cap and 1280 px hiding) and M6's Hire / Run icon-only rule.
Code: `game/src/ui/hud.ts` and `hud.css` (the "top bar redesign" block).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What stays in the bar | **State, not commands**: Back (on a floor), the floor plate, the name and its building, the counts (numbers only: their words are the titles and the screen-reader text), the usage estimate, a share going out (LIVE tape and Stop: M13 says it must stay on screen), the AI chip, the connection badge, and the Menu button. Layouts A (plate + two lines), B (slim, one line) and C (the plate centred) were tried side by side; **B, the slim one line**, won. |
| 2 | The floor plate | A yellow elevator-indicator sign, **"3/6"**, the loudest thing on the bar, and a button: a click opens the elevator (the plate is indicator and switcher). Off a floor it is the place's glyph and name (Lobby, Outside, Fire escape) in the sun colour on navy; in the city it is absent. The sub line now holds the building (and "asleep", "waking up…"): the floor's number moved to the plate, its text to the plate's accessible name ("Floor 3 of 6. Open the elevator"). **Contrast**: ink `#0b1022` on the sun is 11.7:1 (13.2:1 hovered), and the "/6" is full strength, not an opacity (at 70% it was 4.6:1 at 12 px). |
| 3 | The Menu | A popover (light dismiss, Esc and the top layer from the browser) under the bar, in groups: **This floor** (Hire, Run, Issues, PRs, Overview, Elevator), **City** (Buildings, Team presets), **Game** (Share screen, Settings, About, the sound control, Battery saver), and "All commands…" (Ctrl/⌘+P, the palette). Each row says its key, from the shortcut table (`keyName`), so none can drift. The rows **are the old buttons**, moved: every mount point (`addTopbarButton`, `mountScreenShare`, `mountAudio`), the disabled-with-a-reason rows (Issues / PRs on an older bridge, Run) and each dialog's focus handling work as before. A row that acts closes the menu; the sound control (sliders) does not. Arrows walk the rows; opening focuses the first. |
| 4 | Hire and the sound | **Hire is a row, not a bar button** (the user uses H); **the sound controls stay in the menu** (the user's call), the whole control: master mute and volume, and Music with its popover. Share: starting is a row, the LIVE tape and Stop stay in the bar. |
| 5 | Keyboard | Shift+Tab still goes into the bar: Back, then the plate, then Menu; Enter opens the menu on its first row. Settings and About return focus to the Menu button (their row is not on screen when they close). |
| 6 | Widths | One rule set: under 1100 px Back and the share's words go (their titles stay); under 900 px the counts take their own row. The old per-button breakpoints (1500, 1439, 1280) are gone. |

## 2026-10-07 — #174: reasoning effort and a real completion budget for the floor overview summaries

The overview's TASK lines stayed at "Please investigate." with summaries on, a ready LLM and budget left. Two causes: `max_tokens` was a hardcoded 80, which a reasoning model (`openai/gpt-6-luna` through OpenRouter) spends entirely on thinking, answering HTTP 200 with empty content and `finish_reason: "length"` (the audit said `bad_response`, 0 answer bytes); and a failed task call was never asked again (`taskTried` was set before the call and only a busy, not-ready or capped outcome cleared it). Research on what providers accept: `docs/protocol.md` "Floor overview summaries"; sources there and in the ticket.
Code: `internal/ai/{reasoning,openai,service}.go`, `internal/digest/{digest,service}.go`, `internal/server/digest.go`, `internal/store/digest.go`, `internal/transcript/digest.go`, `game/src/ui/{digestSection,overview}.ts`, `game/src/world/{digestSettings,overview}.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the two settings live | **On the summaries feature, not the shared LLM config**: `digest_settings.reasoning_effort` (`""` = provider default, nothing sent) and `max_tokens` (0 = the default), SQLite. The LLM role's `ai.json` and `extra_body` are untouched, so the follow-up questions (1200), pack up (600) and classification (64) keep their own `MaxTokens` and send no effort; the effort is a field of `ai.Request`, data on the request, never a vendor client in `digest` (`TestDigestUsesOnlyAIInterfaces`, `TestAIIsFeatureAgnostic` hold). The UI says so under the two controls. |
| 2 | The default budget | **1,024** (range 64–16,384), up from 80, shown as "In use". It is the provider's generation budget **including reasoning**, not the line length (task 80, now 100 characters, unchanged). 1,024 is not a guarantee with reasoning left on (OpenRouter documents no universal minimum; OpenAI suggests far more when experimenting): a truncated call says so (#6) and the Test shows what each sample spent. Validated against a fake provider that spends 300 tokens thinking (`internal/digest/provider_test.go`): 80 tokens → truncated, 1,024 → both lines, effort `none` → fine even at 80. No live provider was called. |
