| 3 | How the effort is sent | **One control, top-level `reasoning_effort`**: accepted by OpenRouter, OpenAI chat completions, LiteLLM, vLLM and Ollama, so one shape serves every OpenAI-compatible endpoint the AI integration already allows. OpenRouter's unified `reasoning: {effort}` would be OpenRouter-only. The `extra_body`'s own `reasoning` object is dropped from a request that carries an effort (never two competing controls) and left alone when none is chosen; the summaries' own budget is what is sent, whatever the `extra_body` says, **for the summaries call only**: it opts in with `ai.Request.BudgetWins`; an `extra_body` that names `max_completion_tokens` (native OpenAI's name) keeps its field name and gets the budget (never both fields), one that names `max_tokens` is overridden, and with neither it is `max_tokens` (accepted everywhere and by OpenRouter). Every other caller keeps the earlier rule: an `extra_body`'s budget (`max_completion_tokens` over `max_tokens`) wins over the request's `MaxTokens`, which applies only when the `extra_body` names none. That keeps the connection test's 64-token probe and the follow-up (1200) and pack-up (600) budgets deferring to a budget a user set in the shared `extra_body` for a reasoning model; a global override would truncate `ai.test` and block saving that config. A request that sets no bound (`MaxTokens` 0) leaves the `extra_body`'s own. |
| 4 | Which efforts are offered | **The model's own, from its catalog, read when needed**: OpenRouter's `GET {base}/models` entry (`reasoning.supported_efforts` highest-first → shown lowest-first, `default_effort`, `mandatory` removing `none`; `null` efforts = every gateway effort; no `reasoning` object but `supported_parameters` without `reasoning`/`reasoning_effort` = unsupported). Never an effort the list omits (Luna has no `minimal`). Other endpoints list ids only: `unknown`, the generic `low/medium/high` offered, any gateway effort accepted by the API, and a value the model refuses (HTTP 400 with an effort set) fails as a clear `bad_config`, never silently. A model known to have no reasoning gets no control (disabled, with the reason). A **saved** effort the model does not take is `effort_stale`: shown marked, **not sent**. The catalog entry is cached 30 minutes (2 minutes after a failed read) and read by `digest.get`/`digest.set`/summaries; no call at all without an effort chosen. |
| 5 | What the tested/ready fingerprint covers | The summaries test (`tested`) covers **the LLM's config (base URL, model, key, extra_body), the effort and the budget**: changing any needs a new test before switching on. The input-hash cache of lines is deliberately **not** keyed by them: a line written for an input is still the right line, and re-asking after every settings change would spend the cap. What a config change does invalidate is the failures (#7). |
| 6 | Telling a truncated answer from a malformed one | `ai` gets two kinds, `truncated` (the provider's `finish_reason` is `length`: always when there is no text, and for any text when the request says `NoPartial`, which the summaries do: a half line is never shown) and `empty_answer` (well-formed, no text); both were `bad_response`, now reserved for a malformed answer. Other features keep accepting partial text (their validators reject what is cut). Each HTTP answer's sanitized account (status, finish reasons from a short allowlist (`finishReasons`: the gateway's `stop`, `length`, `tool_calls`, `content_filter`, `error` and the common native ones, lowercased) and `other` for anything else, never the provider's own text, which could echo a key; also run through `ai.Scrub`, prompt/completion/total/reasoning tokens as reported, content absent/empty/nonempty and its length) is handed to the request's `Report` callback and put in the `digest.summarize` audit row and the test's samples. Never text, prompts, reasoning or keys. |
| 7 | Retrying a failed task line | **Bounded, timer-driven**: 4 attempts per prompt hash, after 1, 5 and 15 minutes (`digest.RetryDelay`, a hub option for tests), then it waits. Reset by a new prompt hash, `/clear` or another session (a new generation), a change of `digest.Service.ConfigKey` (LLM config, effort, budget, passing test, switch) and the new `digest.retry` command. A call that could not go (daily cap, off, not ready, busy) is not an attempt. Failed attempts still count toward the daily cap and are not cached; serialization, cancellation, session identity and stale-response protection are unchanged. An answer with no line is now a failed attempt too (it used to end the task for good). The now line is not retried by a timer (it follows the turns; #174 asks for the task); a failure shows and `digest.retry` or the next turn asks again. |
| 8 | Showing it | `WorkerDigest.task_status` / `now_status` (`pending`, `retrying` with `retry_at`, `failed`; reason = the error kind; `daily_cap` while waiting for budget), only while a fallback is shown, never for a model line. The page puts a dashed chip where the AI badge goes ("AI pending", "AI retry 14:32", "AI failed", the sentence on its title and for a screen reader) and a Retry link (R on the focused row) when a retry helps. The wall screen texture is not annotated (not interactive, small). |
| 9 | The better fallback | `taskFallback`: the first sentence when it says something; when it has two content words or fewer and no identifier (path, file, number, ticket) it is joined with what follows up to the first sentence that does, greetings dropped, a leading "please" removed, at most 80 characters. "Please investigate. The transcript view no longer shows diffs. Please create a ticket." → "Investigate. The transcript view no longer shows diffs." |
| 8a | Retry on the page, keys and room | The row's "Open transcript" hint is a label only: `pointer-events: none`, hidden while a Retry is hovered or focused, and not shown at all at 760px and below (a phone has no hover), so Retry's whole area is the topmost element at every width. The overview's capture-phase key handler leaves Enter and Space alone when the event comes from `.ov-retry` (the browser clicks the button, once); R and Enter on the row are unchanged. At 480px and below a status chip and Retry wrap under the line they are about, so a long fallback keeps the whole width. |
| 10 | NOW timing | **Unchanged** (a separate product decision): after a turn ends + 2 s settle, at most once per worker per 60 s, floor viewed. The settings now say it (and the task's once-per-prompt, and the retry schedule). |

## 2026-10-07 — #172: the Ubuntu preset replaces Terminal and Paper

#59's three presets became two: **Ubuntu** (new) and **Quiet** (the default, unchanged). The Terminal (green on black,
Departure Mono and Archivo) and Paper (Space Mono and Newsreader) presets are gone from both pickers. #59's decisions
about the two views (apart, each with its own zoom), ligatures, zoom keys and hidden tools hold; those that name Terminal
or Paper are history.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Colours | The terminal's own purple, not a brand swatch: `#300A24` (RGB 48, 10, 36, Ubuntu's terminal default, a community reference) for the screen and the canvas, `#FFFFFF` text, Ubuntu orange `#E95420` for the accent (the `blue` token), dark aubergine `#2C001E` for the code ground and for text on the orange, mid aubergine `#5E2750` for the raised controls. Canonical's brand aubergines (`#77216F`, `#772953`) are not used. |
| 2 | Contrast, measured | Every text token is 4.5:1 or better on every surface it sits on (`ui/themes.test.ts` computes them). White on the orange fill is 3.65:1, so filled controls carry the dark aubergine (5.11:1). The orange itself as text is 4.82:1 on the screen but 4.35:1 on `bg-2` and 3.06:1 on `bg-3`, so the accent as *text* (links, running tools, hunk headers) is its own token `blue-text` (`#FF9B76`, 5.4:1 or better everywhere); `blue` stays for fills, rings and borders (3:1 or better on every surface). Waiting is a yellow sun (`#FFC83D`), apart from the orange accent; success, error, added and deleted keep their green and red. The ticket's "orange on `#300A24` is about 3.6:1" is white on orange; the orange on the purple is 4.82:1. |
| 3 | Fonts | The **classic** Ubuntu (0.83) and Ubuntu Mono (0.80), not Ubuntu Sans, as Fontsource's unmodified files (`@fontsource/ubuntu` and `@fontsource/ubuntu-mono` 5.3.0, pinned like the others): 400 and 700, upright and italic, Latin and Latin Extended, written as our own `@font-face`s in `fonts/ubuntu.css` (loaded on first pick, `font-display: swap`). Sizes: Ubuntu 13.5 px, Ubuntu Mono **14 px** at 100% (the ticket's 12 px start read as small print beside the prose, Ubuntu Mono's advance being 0.5 em with a small x-height; 14 gives the prose's x-height and a whole 7 px cell, Quiet's being 6.9), to the half pixel at every zoom (Departure Mono's pixel grid went with it). |
| 4 | Licence | The Ubuntu Font Licence 1.0, **not the OFL** (#59 rejected Ubuntu over this; #172 chooses it and ships the notices). `FONT_LICENSES.txt` carries the full UFL text of each package, and now a `Copyright:` line for every font (the packages' LICENSE has none): "Copyright 2011 Canonical Ltd." (the font files' own name table). The credits panel and `fontCredits.ts` take the licence per entry. The files are unmodified; only H3D Box is a derivative (renamed). |
| 5 | Glyph coverage | Measured on the files: Ubuntu Mono has 40 of the 128 box-drawing glyphs, 4 block elements, no braille, arrows, geometric shapes or check marks, and Fontsource's subsets none of the boxes. The DOM stack is `"Ubuntu Mono", "H3D Box Ubuntu", "H3D Symbols Ubuntu", "H3D Symbols Ubuntu 2", "H3D Symbols Ubuntu Consolas", SYSTEM_MONO`. **H3D Box Ubuntu** is the existing DejaVu subset with its own `size-adjust`, measured: Ubuntu Mono's advance is 500/1000, H3D Box's 1233/2048, so 500 / 602.05 = 83.05%, set to **83.35%**: on platforms that lay text out on whole pixels (headless Chromium on Linux; the e2e measured it) a half-pixel advance is rounded per font, and at 83.05% the box glyphs rounded to 6 px where Ubuntu Mono's own rounded to 7 at 13 px; 0.4% more makes every tie round up for both (Quiet's 99.66% and Paper's 101.65% do not apply). **The symbols** (arrows, math operators, geometric shapes, check marks and dingbats, braille, misc symbols: U+2190-23FF, 25A0-25FF, 2600-297F, 2B00-2BFF) are in neither Ubuntu Mono nor the box face, and the system's monospace advances 0.6 em, so a row of eight arrows was 1.6 cells wider than a row of letters (the first version documented that as a limitation; the acceptance criterion says symbols stay aligned at 70, 100 and 200%, so it is fixed). Three `local()`-only faces (nothing shipped, no licence to carry) take those ranges from the monospace fonts a machine has and are sized to 0.5 em, one family per advance because `size-adjust` is per face: **H3D Symbols Ubuntu** (DejaVu Sans Mono, Menlo: 0.602 em, 83.35% like the box), **H3D Symbols Ubuntu 2** (Noto Sans Mono, SF Mono, Liberation Mono, Nimbus Mono: 0.6 em, **83.5%**) and **H3D Symbols Ubuntu Consolas** (0.55 em, 91.3%, Windows' monospace: arrows and shapes). The 0.6 em faces need 83.5% where the box's 83.35% is enough: a sweep in headless Chromium over 10 to 32 px (an eight-glyph row against eight letters) showed 83.35% letting Nimbus Mono's half-pixel advances round down at odd sizes (8 px short a row) while 83.5% holds at every one, and the DejaVu-advance box face holds from 83.35% to 85%. Measured: every row of the e2e's symbol frame (arrows, bullets, shapes, check marks, braille, math, ❯ and stars) is within 0.5 px of the row of letters at 70, 100, 150 and 200%; a synthesised bold or oblique keeps the advance, so each family is regular only. A font that is not installed, or lacks the glyph, passes it to the next family (checked in Chromium) and in the end to SYSTEM_MONO, so what a machine lacks is only left unaligned. What CSS cannot do: a glyph that only a *proportional* font has (Segoe UI Symbol, Apple Symbols, Noto Sans Symbols 2) keeps that font's own advance, and a glyph no font has is a box. In the sandbox that built this, only arrows, bullets, shapes and math exist (Nimbus Mono) and the check marks, braille, ❯ and stars are boxes (`D050000L`'s dingbats are proportional, so not usable); a desktop with DejaVu or Menlo has them all. The canvas still draws boxes, blocks and braille itself (decision 4 of #59) and takes the same stack for the rest: a symbol there now measures a letter's advance, where the 0.6 em system glyph was squeezed into the cell by `maxWidth` (e2e measures it). |
| 6 | A narrow font on the canvas | The canvas fits each glyph to its cell by stretching it horizontally, and a cell is shaped for a 0.6 em advance: Ubuntu Mono at the usual size would be stretched up to 1.8 times. `paintBuffer` now grows a narrow monospace's em toward the cell's width (up to 1.25 times, never past the cell's height) instead; fonts at 0.6 em (Quiet, any system monospace, so a failed font load too) are unchanged. |
| 7 | Saved choices | `terminal` becomes `ubuntu` and `paper` becomes `quiet`, in `h3d.theme.transcript` and `h3d.theme.terminal` alike, when read, and the stored value is rewritten (Quiet as nothing, the default's form). Zoom, Hide tools and fullscreen are other keys and untouched. Anything else that is not a preset id is still Quiet; storage that throws or is missing keeps the choice in memory. The themed *view* called `terminal` is not a preset and stays. |
| 8 | What stays | Departure Mono and Archivo are **not removed**: the floor overview's wall and page use them (`overview.css`, `overviewScreen.ts`), and their credits stay with those roles. Space Mono and Newsreader had no other user and went: their stylesheet, packages, lockfile entries and credits. |
| 9 | Filled controls (review fix) | `.tx button { color: inherit }` (specificity 0,1,1) out-ranked `.tx-send` and `.tx-jump` (0,1,0), so Send and the jump-to-latest chip drew white on the orange (3.65:1) instead of `--tx-on-accent`. Both are now `.tx .tx-send` and `.tx .tx-jump` (0,2,0), like `.tx .tx-ask-send` before them. Audited: every other rule that sets a colour on an accent, sun, green or red fill is on a span or div (`.tx-banner-tag`, `.tx-choice-n`, `.tx-ask-mark`, `.tx-ask-key`, `.tx-qtab-n`, `.imgs-n`) or a button with a rule of 0,2,0 or more (`.seat-tab[aria-selected]`, `.tx .tx-ask-send`), so none lost the cascade. Quiet's own `#0b1020` on `#6f9bff` (7.04:1) is unchanged, and both themes are asserted in `e2e/h172-ubuntu.spec.ts` (enabled, hovered and focused Send; the chip; desktop and 390 px). Disabled Send stays at its 45% opacity, the on-accent ink faded with its fill: 2.07:1 as shown in Ubuntu and 2.48:1 in Quiet, legible as a label but under the 4.5:1 a disabled control is exempt from; left as it was. |

## 2026-10-07 — #188: the all-floors overview and the active-only filter

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Floor order | Buildings in `sort` order (then name), each building's floors **top floor first**, then the unassigned lot by name: the Buildings panel's order (`WorldStore.stack`). The brief said "exactly `store.city()` order, top floor first, as `city()` returns them"; `city()` in fact returns a building's floors **ground first** (`store.test.ts` "stacks building floors ground-first"), so `buildCityOverview` takes `city()` and reverses each building's floors: the outcome the brief and the issue name, and the view the user already reads floors in. `city()` itself is untouched (the 3D city, N's queue and the palette use it). |
| 2 | Each worker once | A sibling `buildCityOverview` (`world/overview.ts`); `buildOverview` and the floor page's tree are unchanged byte for byte. Under each floor its tree one level deeper, with one rule: a freelancer sits under its orchestrator, on the **orchestrator's floor**, tagged with its own floor when that is another (`Floor 1`, or the session outside the building, the floor page's `floorLabel`); **no ghost rows**; a floor's **Unlinked** group (key `unlinked:<session>`, per floor, under the freelancer's own floor) holds only the freelancers whose orchestrator is truly gone, absent, or not an orchestrator. The floor page keeps its ghosts and its pulled-in freelancers: between them the floor pages list a cross-floor run twice, the all-floors page once (vitest on a run spanning three floors). |
| 3 | The filter | **Active only / All**, key `f` and a two-position switch in the header, on both pages. Active = `working` + `needs_you` + `waiting`; idle (done included) and asleep are not. A pure post-pass on the tree (`filterOverview`): an inactive orchestrator, Unlinked group or floor stays while anything under it is active, everything else goes and is counted. **One setting per view** (`h3d.overview.filter.city` / `.floor`, `active` or `all`, guarded like the collapse state: missing or throwing storage works for this page only). Defaults: **active only** on the all-floors page (its job is "how many are still running"), **all** on a floor's page (as before #188). |
| 4 | What the header says | The counts are never filtered: the whole system on the all-floors page (the awake floors' counts summed, then every worker of an asleep floor as `asleep`, `cityCountItems`), the floor's own on a floor's page. Beside the switch, what it hides: `12 idle hidden · 3 floors asleep` (asleep workers under a floor that still shows, an active freelancer keeping their orchestrator's asleep floor up: `1 asleep hidden`; a floor with nobody on it and no idle or asleep worker to explain it: `1 quiet floor hidden`; a floor's page: `N idle hidden`, `N asleep hidden`), a button that switches to All: whenever the filter hides anyone, the line says so. A filter that hides everyone: "All quiet" with Show everyone. |
| 5 | Motion | A row the filter hides folds away **where it was** (the collapse's fold: height, padding and opacity with the slight spring, the last row first), anchored after the nearest row above it still on the board, which generalises #118's fold (it anchored under the collapsed group, so a hidden floor had nowhere to fold). A row that shows again comes back like an expansion. Calm (reduced motion, battery saver): at once. The order is the tree's, never a status's. |
| 6 | Opening | **O in the city, a lobby or on the street** opens the all-floors page (the toast is gone); **O on an office floor** opens that floor's, unchanged. The top bar's Menu has an Overview row in its **City** section (accessible name "All-floors overview", O cap), shown wherever the floor's own Overview is not (the city, a lobby, the street), so the Menu never has two; it is a Menu row like the floor's (the top bar keeps only state, `decisions.md` "top bar"). The command palette has "All-floors overview" (a palette-only row: its key is O off a floor). The all-floors page always grows out of the centre and closes into it, never the wall screen, which stays per floor (`render/overviewScreen.ts` untouched). |
| 7 | Between the views | A floor's page: **`a`** and an **All floors** header button switch to every floor in place (no second grow, the rows cascade in). **Esc on the all-floors page closes it**, back to where you stand (the city, the lobby, the floor), also when you came from a floor's page with `a`: Esc means "back to the game", and a second Esc to leave a stack of pages would be a trap. Enter / click on a floor row: `goToFloor` (the palette's: dialogs go, a seat is left, the elevator takes you), the page closes at once; on a worker row: `goToWorker(id, 'transcript')` as before, which walks to another floor first; `t` the ticket, pinned to its own floor (`openItem(…, { pin: true })`): a live update neither closes it (the city, a lobby) nor retargets it to the floor you stand on, until you close it. |
| 8 | Floor rows and collapse | A floor row is a band over its rows (they read as on a floor's page, no extra indent): the session in the header's condensed face, `Floor 2 · Work` (nothing more for a lot floor: its session is its name), the top bar's counts as chips (`countItems`; a zero goes quiet), and a drawn crescent "Asleep" when it sleeps. `VisibleRow.depth` is 0–2; a collapsed floor hides its whole tree, its collapsed orchestrators' state kept. Collapse keys: `floor:<session>` beside the orchestrator ids (shared with the floor pages); **asleep floors start collapsed**, and one the viewer opens keeps an `open:floor:<session>` marker in the same list (folding it again removes it). Shift+→ / Shift+← act on floors and orchestrators together. |
| 9 | Empty | A city with no worker: "Nobody's here yet" under either filter (not bare floor rows), no Hire (a hire needs a floor; the floor page keeps its Hire). |
| 10 | AI lines elsewhere | Option (a): floors nobody stands on show the phase 1 fallbacks (prompt, recap, title); the page already marks the model's lines AI. No bridge or protocol change, no extra LLM cost; an "all floors are viewed" signal is left for later. |
| 11 | Keys in the table | `F` and `A` are listed (read-only, "Dialogs and fields") in the shortcut table and `docs/shortcuts.md`; the O row says it opens every floor's overview off an office floor. The seated O (the floor's page over the conversation) is unchanged. |

## 2026-10-07 — #184: Kaneo as a floor's issue tracker

A floor can take its issues from a project of a Kaneo instance instead of GitHub. The bridge-wide connection lives in
`internal/kaneoconf` (settings, the key's file, the test that gates a save, the generation); the server, the store and
the protocol never import the Kaneo client (`internal/kaneo`), which `app` wires in behind `kaneoconf.Connector` and
the board factory (`TestBoardLayerIsProviderNeutral`, `TestKaneoSettingsIsProviderNeutral`).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Default workspace semantics | The global default workspace is a **prefill** for a floor not linked yet. A floor's save stores its own explicit `(workspace_id, project_id)`; a later change of the default never rebinds it. Names are stored beside the ids for show only; ids are authoritative. |
| 2 | Save gating | The bridge, not the client, insists: `kaneo.set` saves only an instance and key that passed `kaneo.test` (the key by its SHA-256, in memory) within 30 minutes, and a default workspace that test listed. A test changes nothing saved; a failed save changes nothing. The panel's own gating (an edit undoes the test) mirrors it. |
| 3 | A key for another instance | A blank key means the saved key **only for the saved API base**; any other address without a typed key is `key_required` before a request goes out, so a saved key never travels to a different host, a test included. Same instance written as origin or `/api` base is the same instance. |
| 4 | Where the key lives | Its own `0600` file (`kaneo.json` next to the database, `--kaneo-file`), atomic like `ai.json`, apart from the AI integration (a Kaneo key is not an AI key; clearing one never touches the other). No key hint is shown (only `key_set`): the ticket asks for safe metadata, and the last four characters are not needed to recognise one Kaneo instance's key. |
| 5 | Generation | A counter persisted in the file that moves when the instance or the key changes, or on disconnect. Every Kaneo floor's board source names it, so the board, its SQLite cache and any poll in flight of an older generation are dropped (`finishPoll` re-runs `boardOf` before applying a result). A default-only change keeps it (the boards stay). |
| 6 | Runtime 401 | Kaneo's key verifier answers an exhausted quota with 401, so a 401 is never "the key is invalid". A poll's 401 pauses **every** Kaneo floor (one key, one quota) for 5 minutes through the provider's rate gate, marks the connection `paused` (a `kaneo.update`), and `board.refresh` does not poll meanwhile. It resumes on the cooldown's end, on a passing `kaneo.test` of the saved connection, or on a new connection. A 403 is `forbidden`, shown as a permission problem, with no pause. A command's 401 or 403 is reported as such and changes no state. |
| 7 | Floor saves that need Kaneo | `floor.settings.set` moved to the asynchronous command path: a save that stores a new link or switches to Kaneo asks Kaneo for the project (`GET /project/<id>`: it must exist, belong to the workspace and not be archived) off the hub goroutine, then applies the args again to the floor's current settings. It is `stale` when the floor's tracker or link changed meanwhile (compared with the link the save **started from**, not with the args applied again: an older save answered last never undoes a newer one) or when the Kaneo connection's generation is not the one that checked the project (another instance or key saved, or cleared). Only the tracker and the link are compared, so a concurrent save of the floor's other settings does not make it stale. Saves that do not touch the link stay synchronous and call nothing. |
| 8 | Tracker and code host apart | `BoardSource` gained `Tracker` and the Kaneo fields (instance, generation, workspace, project; never the key). The factory picks the issue tracker by `Tracker` and the code host by the repo: a Kaneo floor without a repo has an issues board and no pull request board; with one, GitHub's pull requests. The inactive tracker's settings are kept in `floor_repo`. A Kaneo floor's pull requests read GitHub's rate gate, not Kaneo's. |
| 9 | Virtual columns | Kaneo's `planned` and `archived` are columns with `kind` (always present, `planned` first, `archived` last). Archive is not completion: only `done` columns (Kaneo's final ones; there can be several) make a card `closed`. The game draws the virtual ones as dashed trays with a note ("archived, not done") and never gives them the done chip. |
| 10 | Close | Off for Kaneo (`capabilities.close: false`, `issue.close` `unsupported`): no duplicate or not-planned reasons, no silently chosen final column, and never `DELETE /task`. Completion is a move to a final column. |
| 11 | Missing timestamps | Kaneo gives none: `updated_at: 0`, `closed_at: null`. The game shows no time and no "closed recently" (the seven-day window is GitHub's). |
| 12 | Truncated boards | A bounded read that stopped early is `truncated`, said on the board. A placed move on it would anchor to cards the bridge did not see: the game sends a status-only move there (the provider refuses `after` on a truncated board). |
| 13 | Run references | A Kaneo task is `{provider:"kaneo", id}` everywhere: `run_launch` gained `item_provider`, `item_id`, `item_key`; `runs.Links` carries `Item` beside `Issue` (freelancers inherit it). `tracker.RunLinker` stays GitHub-only; a non-`RunLinker` item is resolved with `Tracker.Get` into an `item` run ticket. A Kaneo floor's numeric tickets are never read as GitHub issues. |
| 14 | Prompts | An agent cannot read a Kaneo task (no `gh`, and the key never reaches an agent): the orchestrator's prompt and "Ask a worker…" inline the key, title, URL and description (6000 / 4000 bytes, then a note), and say not to look it up with `gh`. "Ask a worker…" on a Kaneo task is therefore offered only once the board's `issue.get` of it answered: while it loads, or after it failed (Try again is beside it), the button says why it waits and asks nothing, so an ask never goes without the description. The issue-status line ("move the ticket status…") is replaced, for a Kaneo task, by "leave its status on the Kaneo board to people": the orchestrator has no way to move it. The write-code skill (outside this repo, `~/.agents/skills/write-code/SKILL.md`) still says a referred ticket should be set to Doing/QA; it names no `gh` command, and the prompt's own line overrides it for Kaneo. GitHub's prompts are byte for byte as before. *(Amended 2026-10-17 by #102: the assumption was wrong. That the bridge never passes its key to an agent says nothing about the tools and credentials an agent already has (Kaneo has a CLI and an MCP server that take a task id). A Kaneo task is now named by its canonical URL, which carries the instance, workspace, project and task id (`Kaneo task <key>` when there is none, never a bare number): the orchestrator's prompt is `/write-code <url>` (`$write-code` for Codex) with the team, placement, floor and extra lines and **no** status line, and "Ask a worker…" sends `askPrompt(key, url)` like an issue's and is offered while the description loads or after it failed. No title, description, truncation note, "don't use gh" or "leave its status to people". Retrieval is the agent's: no inline fallback, no credential forwarding, no access preflight. `Tracker.Get` still validates the item and gives the key, title and URL of the run's link; `RunTicket.Body` and the 6000 / 4000-byte bounds are gone. GitHub's and free-text prompts are unchanged.)* |
| 15 | "Open in Kaneo" from the helper | The helper's built-in allowlist stays `github.com`. The bridge names its saved instance's web **host** (https, no port) on its own `/healthz` (`X-H3D-Open-Hosts`), which the helper already polls every 5 s through its ssh tunnel; the helper adds it while it is named and drops it when not. Trust: the bridge you ssh to is already trusted (it holds the keys and runs the agents), and no page can set the header (the helper does not proxy `/healthz`). The URL checks are unchanged (https, no credentials, no port). A self-hosted instance on a non-443 port cannot be opened from the helper (the check refuses ports); a later change could add exact origins. Propagation: within one check (≤ 5 s) of a save or a disconnect, no helper restart. |
| 16 | Rate friendliness | Outbound calls run off the hub goroutine, 15 s each, two at a time at most; workspace and project lists are cached 30 s per generation; the floor's project list waits 200 ms after a workspace change and only the newest answer lands (`LatestOnly`), so rapid switching sends few calls and never restores an older project. The lists belong to the connection's generation: a `kaneo.update` with another generation (a new instance or key, a clear, a first connection) drops them and every answer on its way, and an open floor form asks the new connection at once (one opened before Kaneo was connected loads when it is); an answer whose `generation` is not the shown connection's is dropped. Nothing is called until a floor is switched to Kaneo or Test is pressed. |
| 17 | The wiring | `app.Kaneo` opens the connection with `app.KaneoConnector` (internal/kaneo behind `kaneoconf.Connector`: `ParseURL`, the connection test, the workspace and project lists, `Project`) and `app.KaneoFloors` (the floors' trackers); the world command and the end-to-end tests use the same. The connector makes a client per call of the instance and key it is given (a draft under test is not the saved connection; a client is a few fields, and every client shares the default transport's pool). The provider's error kinds pass through unchanged; a call that never reached Kaneo is also `kaneoconf.ErrUnreachable`. `Project`, not `ProjectIn`: `VerifyProject` tells gone (`not_found`, a 400 on the id read included) from another workspace's and archived (`bad_request`) itself. |
| 18 | Floors' trackers | Built on the hub goroutine from `Credentials(generation)`, with no call to Kaneo: one client per saved generation, one tracker per (workspace, project) shared by the floors that link it and kept while the generation lives, so its last board (what a move is computed and checked against) survives the factory's calls; a new generation drops them all. The board identity is the tracker's `Key()` plus `\|g<generation>`, never the key. A board read is bounded per call (15 s, like a test's) and by the provider's 12-call budget; there is no deadline over the whole read, as for GitHub's feed. |
| 19 | A Kaneo floor's pull requests | The poll reads the code host first; its failure used to be charged to the tracker's gate, so a GitHub limit (or `gh` refusing) on a Kaneo floor's pull requests backed off, or paused, Kaneo. It now goes to the host's own gate and never pauses the connection, and the pull request board carries GitHub's rate reading (Kaneo has none). |
| 20 | Comment length | Kaneo counts 10,000 UTF-16 code units; the provider refuses a longer comment with `bad_request` and Kaneo's limit in the message before any call. The bridge's own cap (64 KiB, every provider) and the game's comment box are unchanged: the refusal is shown like any other. |
| 21 | Tests | `internal/kaneo/kaneotest` serves the provider's fixtures over HTTP (a wrong key is a 401, a DELETE fails the test). `internal/server/kaneo_wired_test.go` runs the hub with `app.Kaneo` and `app.Boards` against it (a faked `gh` for the pull requests): settings, a floor's link, the board, every write, the 401 pause and its re-test, a truncated board, a repo, a run's ticket and its launch across a restart, and the key in nothing a client read, the log, the audit log or the board caches. No automated test calls a live instance. |
| 22 | Run ticket titles | The title, URL and key one `Tracker.Get` read of a run's item off the board (#118) belong to the board source it was read from (`BoardSet.Key`: for Kaneo the instance, the key's generation, the project): the cache is keyed by it, a floor's entries go with its board (`dropBoard`), and a read that lands after the floor's source changed is dropped. A new instance, key or project reads the item again once, from the new one; a worker never keeps a title of a connection that is gone. |
| 23 | A run started while its item is read | `run.start` of a `work_item` reads the item off the hub goroutine (`Tracker.Get`). The floor's board source (`BoardSet.Key`) is taken when the read starts and compared on the hub when it ends: when the floor switched tracker, Kaneo connection or project meanwhile, nothing is hired or recorded and the answer is `stale` (the item is of a board the floor no longer has). |

### #184: the Kaneo provider package

The provider half of the entry above, `internal/kaneo`, built against the instance's deployed OpenAPI and the pinned
upstream source (`docs/research/kaneo-api.md`), tested only against `httptest` fakes (no live call). The settings, the
floors, the wire and the wiring in `app` are the table above. Code: `internal/kaneo/`,
`internal/provider/provider.go` (`ErrUnauthorized`, `ErrForbidden`), `internal/tracker/tracker.go` (`Column.Kind`).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What instance URL is accepted | The origin or its `/api` base, with or without a trailing slash; scheme and host lowercased, a default port dropped, `/api` never doubled (`ParseURL` → `Endpoint{APIBase, WebOrigin}`). Refused: anything but http(s), a user or password, a query or fragment (even an empty `?` or `#`), spaces, any other path (a pasted dashboard link is not an instance; sub-path installs are not supported), and plain http off loopback (`localhost`, 127/8, `::1`). |
| 2 | Errors | One kind per status, as the contract pins: 401 `ErrUnauthorized` (its message says the key may be wrong, expired, revoked **or out of quota for now**: Kaneo answers an exhausted key with 401), 403 `ErrForbidden`, 404 `ErrNotFound`, 409 `ErrStale`, 400/422 `ErrBadInput`, 429 `*RateLimitError` (`ResetAt` from `Retry-After`, seconds or a date; zero when absent, which is what Kaneo sends), 3xx (never followed: the key would go along), 5xx, network and timeouts `ErrFailed`; a timeout or cancel also answers `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded / Canceled)`. Kaneo's own words (plain text, or JSON `message`) are kept, one line, 160 bytes, with the key and any `Bearer …` replaced by `[key]`; a URL error keeps only its cause. Each call has its own timeout (15 s default) under the caller's ctx; an answer is capped at 16 MiB (a board page can carry 100 descriptions of 64 KiB). |
| 3 | A 400 on a read by id | Kaneo answers an unknown task or project with **400** ("Unknown task, or its workspace could not be determined"), not 404. A read whose only argument is the id (`GET /task/{id}`, `/project/{id}`, `/column/{id}`, `/task/tasks/{id}`, `/comment/{id}`) reads it as `ErrNotFound`; writes keep 400 → `ErrBadInput`. |
| 4 | The connection test | `GET /auth/organization/list` (it is what proves the key; timed for the latency), then `GET /user/me` for the owner's name, optional (its failure is not the test's, a cancel is). Only GETs. An empty list is a success with no workspaces. An entry without an id is skipped; one without a name shows its id. |
| 5 | Projects | `GET /project?workspaceId=` (`&includeArchived=true` only when asked); archived ones and any that names another workspace are dropped on our side too. `ProjectIn(ws, project)` reads `GET /project/{id}` and answers `ErrNotFound` when it is of another workspace (or unknown): what a floor's save checks. |
| 6 | Ids, keys, links | A card's id is Kaneo's task id (opaque); its key `<project slug>-<number>` (the slug alone for a task without a number); its URL `<web origin>/dashboard/workspace/<ws>/project/<project>/task/<task>` (by ids: no rename breaks it). The fetch identity is `Tracker.Key()` = `kaneo:<API base>\|<workspace>\|<project>`, never the key; the app appends the connection's generation (`\|g<n>`, row 18 above). |
| 7 | Columns | **Planned first, the workflow columns in position order, Archived last, the two virtual ones always present** (also empty), with `Kind` `planned`/`archived`: a card can be moved into an empty bucket, and the column set does not change with the cards. Archived is not completion: only a column with `isFinal` is `Done`, and a card is `closed` only there. A workflow column whose slug is `planned` or `archived` replaces the virtual one; a task whose status only the board pages name (a column made since the column list was read) gets that column, before Archived. Column ids are slugs (what a task's status holds), never the column list's database ids. |
| 8 | How much a board read does | `limit=100`; task pages followed through `totalPages` up to **5** (500 tasks), each task page's related pages (labels, links, columns) through `relatedTotalPages` up to **3**, and at most **12 calls** a read, the column list included. Past any of them, when `pagination.total` changes during the read, or when the read ends with a number of distinct tasks other than `pagination.total` (the board moved under it: a reorder between pages repeats one task and skips another with the total unchanged), the board is `Truncated`; the next poll reads it whole. Labels are merged by id across related pages (Kaneo repeats the page's tasks on each), a task is a card once. A small project is 2 calls a poll; the worst is 12, so a watched Kaneo floor (60 s) spends at most 12 of the key's default 100 calls a minute. No rate headers exist: `Board.Rate` is nil. Final-column tasks are all read (no seven-day window: Kaneo gives no completion time). |
| 9 | Card fields Kaneo lacks | `UpdatedAt` 0 and `ClosedAt` nil always (the schema has neither; `createdAt` is not relabeled), `Author` "" (a task's `userId` is its assignee; no creator is exposed), so `Detail.Author` is empty too. `Position` is the card's rank in its column in Kaneo's order (position, then id; none last); the raw positions stay inside for the reorder. Priority `urgent/high/medium/low`, `no-priority` and anything else none; label colours `#rrggbb` (`#abc` widened, anything else grey); the one assignee with its avatar. |
| 10 | Get | `GET /task/{id}?view=detail`, then the check that it is of this project (and workspace, when the detail says) else `ErrNotFound`, before anything else is read. A deferred description (`descriptionDeferred`) is read through `GET /task/{id}/description` page by page carrying the version; a change under the read (409) starts it over once, a second is `ErrStale`; past 64 pages it is an error, never a silently cut body. The card is the last board's (labels, avatar, rank; a board is read when there is none yet) with the detail's title, status, priority and assignee (the rank dropped when the column changed); a task off the board is made from the detail alone. Comments: `GET /comment/{id}`, all of them (Kaneo has no paging), so `CommentsTruncated` is false. |
| 11 | Comment | A blank body or one over **10,000 UTF-16 code units** (what Kaneo's validator counts: a 4-byte rune is 2) is `ErrBadInput` before any call. The task's scope is checked with a fresh detail read, then `POST /comment/{id}` `{content}`. The answer is an activity row without an author, so the comment list is read again for it; when that read fails the comment is still returned (it landed), without its author. |
| 12 | SetStatus | The column must be a column of the last board (one is read when there is none). Without an anchor: the scope from a fresh detail read, then `PUT /task/status/{id}` `{status: slug}`. With one: **one** `POST /task/reorder`: the destination column numbered 0..n-1 in its new order, only the cards whose position changes sent plus the moved one with its status, and `expectedTasks` every task of the column it leaves and the one it enters as the last board has them (raw positions, null kept), so Kaneo answers 409 (`ErrStale`) when the board changed since; Kaneo itself refuses a task of another project (404). A moved card or an anchor that is not where the last board says is `ErrStale` without a call; a truncated board cannot be ordered (`ErrUnsupported`: Kaneo would refuse every snapshot). The answer of either write updates the last board, so a second drag in a row is computed from Kaneo's state. |
| 13 | Close, DELETE | `Close` is `ErrUnsupported` (`Info.Capabilities.Close` false): completion is a move to a final column. No code path sends a DELETE (`TestNoDelete`; the fake fails any test that would). |
| 14 | Concurrency | Board reads are one at a time; the last board is behind a mutex, and the writes and Get read and update it under it. Two moves computed from the same board both go: the second is refused as stale by Kaneo. |
| 15 | Boundaries | `internal/kaneo` imports the standard library, `provider` and `tracker` only (`TestKaneoIsAProviderLeaf`); only `app` imports it (`TestBoardLayerIsProviderNeutral` checks both that `server`, `store` and `proto` do not and that `app` does). |
| 16 | Unreachable | A round trip that failed (DNS, refused, TLS) is `provider.ErrFailed` with `ErrUnreachable` and the network error as its cause, so the settings tell "could not be reached" from "Kaneo failed"; its message is unchanged and never the cause's. |

## 2026-10-07 — #187: the laptop you sit at is not taken from you by the world

A background change of the world (another worker finishing, leaving or arriving, a floor's desk count, the building's
outline or floor order) used to stand you up when it rebuilt the floor's scene: `Laptops.attach` took a new
`OfficeView` for another floor. Reading, typing a prompt or watching a screen is not interrupted by the world any more.
Code: `game/src/laptop/index.ts` (`attach`, `rebind`, `rebase`, `syncAway`), `laptop/seatAway.ts`, `app.ts`
(`syncWalk`); tests `laptop/seatAway.test.ts`, `e2e/h187-keep-seat.spec.ts` (the repro of #187 and the rest below).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What a seat is tied to | **The worker and its floor's session, not the `OfficeView` instance.** A rebuilt scene of the same session keeps the seat: `rebind` sets the lean and the laptop's lid on the new scene, the seated monitor (full, frozen behind the conversation) on its new meshes, and fits the camera at once, no glide. The transcript, its draft, image chips, scroll, focus and selection live in the overlay, are never touched and need no rebinding. You choosing another floor (or none) ends the seat. |
| 2 | The world moving the walker | A storey change or a nudge out of a new chair in `App.syncWalk` is not a step of yours: it calls `Laptops.rebase(walker)` so the seat takes the new feet (and refits the camera) instead of reading it as navigation. Your own movement while seated still stands you up. |
| 3 | The worker's desk changes | The seat follows it (`rebind`), instead of "moved desks, so you got up". |
| 4 | The worker is gone, the floor asleep or the pane gone | **You stay.** This reverses "the worker leaving stands you up" (M5): `whyAway` (pure) says why, from the store each frame (so a woken floor or a new pane recovers by itself). The view and its draft stay; the seat bar says it as `offline` ("Nibble is no longer on the floor. Nothing you type is sent."), no toast; the seat's terminal and transcript watches are not asked for until it is back; keys, pastes and every command of the conversation (`worker.prompt`, …) are refused with `unavailable` (the prompt box keeps its text and shows the reason like any refused send). A refused terminal watch (`terminal.watch` failing for the seat) is the same: the bar says why and it is asked for again after 5 s. |
| 5 | What still stands you up | Only you: Esc, a panel, dialog or plan (not the pack-up check or the overview), walking or a teleport. The world never does, not even by removing the floor you sit on (`floor.removed`): there is no exception. |
| 6 | A desk the scene no longer has | The seat keeps `anchor`, the desk slot's world matrix as last seen (`Laptops.seatMatrix`): when a rebuilt scene has no such desk (its worker left and the floor shrank), the glass, the camera pose and the conversation laid over it use the anchor, so the overlay is never hidden and the composer keeps its focus. |
| 7 | The floor you sit on is removed | `App.syncWalk` does not call `leaveFloor` while you are seated: the `OfficeView` stays as it was (nothing rebuilds it, the store has no view of it), the worker is away (row 4), and the seat keeps working. Once you get up yourself (`step` sees `orphaned` and no seat) it runs again, says "… is gone." and leaves to the city, disposing the scene, so nothing is kept. If the floor comes back first (a `floor.upsert` of the session), the scene is applied or rebuilt as for any update. |
| 8 | The seat bar's line | The away (or offline) line is set again whenever the view changes (Terminal ↔ Conversation: `SeatBar.setMode` says "ok"), and wraps to two lines instead of ending in an ellipsis, so the terminal view never says "Your keys go to …" about a worker that is away. |
| 9 | Not done | The composer is not disabled while away (a disabled box would take the keyboard from you while you type); the draft is yours to copy or keep. |

## 2026-10-08 — #186: a hire on Codex's built-in server is a warning, not a failure; Codex 0.161

A Codex hire made with the shared-server switch on that runs on Codex's built-in server instead (an unverified Codex,
a blocked `.envrc`, a server that does not answer, …) is a hire that **succeeded**: the worker is there and works,
without the transcript's async answers. Its toast was red, like a failed hire. Code: `game/src/ui/hiredToast.ts`
(hire and run start), `ui/hud.ts` + `hud.css` (`ToastKind`), `world/codexServer.ts` (`codexNotice`); tests
`ui/hiredToast.test.ts`, `ui/hire.dom.test.ts`, `ui/codexServerSection.dom.test.ts`, `e2e/h155-codex-server.spec.ts`
(the fixture bridge's `simfail=codex_unverified`).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | How a fallback hire is shown | **A third toast kind, `warn`**: ink with a sun-yellow ring and a drawn triangle, between the info sun and the error red. `error` gets a drawn octagon, so the two never differ by colour alone (screen readers hear the words; the toast stays one polite status). It lasts 9 s, the hire dialogs' errors 6.5 s. |
| 2 | Which wins | `hiredToast(lead, ack, failure)`, one rule for `worker.hire` and `run.start`: a task or prompt that did not reach the worker (`prompted: false`) is `error`, also beside a fallback (both are said); a fallback alone is `warn`; else `info`. A refused hire is unchanged (`error`, `failureText`). |
| 3 | The words | Any fallback names its cost: "It runs on Codex's built-in server, so its async questions can't be answered from the transcript." An unverified Codex (by `codex --version` or by the server's `userAgent`) is said plainly: "the shared Codex server is not verified with Codex 0.162.0 yet (verified: 0.160.x or 0.161.x)". The supported set is read from the bridge's note, not kept in the game. The same words show on the seat bar (a wake without its server too). |
| 4 | Found on the way | A toast shown as a screen dialog closes (a hire's) was placed before the limits plate the dialog had folded came back, and sat under it. The toast is placed again on the next frame. |
| 5 | Which Codex is supported | **An explicit set of verified release lines**, `codexapp.SupportedLines` = 0.160, 0.161, used by both gates (`codex --version`, the server's `userAgent`). A line matches exactly and the patch must be numeric: 0.159 and 0.162 (and 0.161.0-alpha) are refused until verified. Rejected: a numeric range, which would call every later release verified. |
| 6 | The fixture's Codex | **Exact versions**, `codexshare.VerifiedCodex` = 0.160.1, 0.161.0; any other skips with "UNVERIFIED CODEX, not a pass", and each test logs the version it ran. A unit test keeps every fixture version inside the gates' lines. Verifying a release = adding it to both, then a run without a version skip. |
| 7 | The stand-in for "unsupported" | codex-cli **0.162.0** (and 0.159.9) in every unit test and in #158's rollout scenario, in place of 0.161.0. |
| 8 | 0.161.0's evidence | Findings §13: both suites on 0.161.0 with no version skip; every bridge, question, exactly-once, restart and lifecycle test passes. One 0.161-only change outside the bridge: no `SessionStart` hook on a **warm** re-attach (a TUI resumed against a server that still has the thread loaded), so Herdr loses the session id. **Accepted as a known upstream limitation; 0.161 is supported.** herdr-3d never re-attaches warm (wake starts a new server; the bridge keeps the recorded thread). `MixedHarness` asserts it per version: strict on 0.160.x, the observed loss on 0.161.x (an upstream fix fails it loudly, and it is then made strict), and a cold resume on a new server strict on both. Rejected: holding 0.161 back for a path the bridge does not use. |
| 9 | A worker hired the usual way, after an upgrade | It keeps its mode for life (#155): it does not migrate when a verified Codex is installed or the switch changes. Shared mode = send it home and hire a new worker with the switch on. Turning the switch off only stops the fallback notice for later hires. |

## 2026-10-08 — #171: code changes show by default in the transcript, Claude's Bash edits included

Claude's Edit and Write diffs were parsed but drawn only inside a tool row you had to open, and Bash-driven edits
(scripts, formatters, redirects) were dropped although Claude Code (2.1.269+) records them as
`toolUseResult.bashEditDiff`. Now every recorded change shows under its call's header, with a switch to fold them.
Code: `internal/transcript/claude.go` (`claudeDiff`, `claudeBashChanges`), `internal/proto/transcript.go`
(`TranscriptDiff.Op` / `NoDiff`, `TranscriptChanges`, `ToolDenied`), `game/src/laptop/transcriptRender.ts`
(`changesBlock`, `changeFile`), `transcript.css`, `ui/themePrefs.ts` (`showChanges`); tests
`internal/transcript/changes_test.go`, `laptop/transcriptChanges.test.ts`, `e2e/h171-changes.spec.ts` (fixture
`demo-h171`: `?ws=%2Ffixture%2Fdemo-h171%3Fsim%3D1`, sit at Nibble on herdr-3d).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Shown by default? | **Yes.** "Code changes" in the transcript's strip (under 1,120px of view, as on a laptop's screen, just its box and a pencil, its name kept for screen readers and in its tooltip, so the strip stays one line), `h3d.transcript.showChanges`, absent ⇒ on (stored `'0'` when off), shared by every view like the other view prefs. Off, each changed file is one line (path, what happened to it, +/− counts) that still opens on a click; on again opens the same history. Each file folds on its own either way; flipping the switch resets those hand choices. The reading position is kept (the row at the top of the view stays put). |
| 2 | Where | Under the call's header, outside its body, like #70's images: the raw input and output stay behind the row. "Hide tools" keeps rows with changes (bare, no header) and their images; the two switches are independent. A failed edit has no change to show, so it stays hidden and the "failed" chip still counts it; a row whose changes show is not counted there. |
| 3 | What a file says | Path, a tag where the source says what happened (`new`, `deleted`, `overwritten`; an edit has none; Codex patches have none, the patch format does not say), +/− counts, line numbers where the source has them (Claude: yes; Codex: none made up). Never "new" for an overwrite: `Write`'s `type` decides, never `originalFile` (null on 74% of edits and on some overwrites). |
| 4 | An overwrite without hunks | Tagged `overwritten` and `no diff`, no counts, and why: "Claude Code keeps none when nothing changed, the old file was too large, or the diff timed out." No removed lines are made up. Documented, never seen locally (synthetic fixture). |
| 5 | How `bashEditDiff`'s flags render | Files with a diff as above (`created` → new, `deleted` → deleted). Changed files without a diff (`changedFiles` less the diffed, `moreFiles`): "N more files changed, no diff recorded", the list behind it, "and K not listed" when `moreFiles` is larger. `unavailable`: "Incomplete: Claude Code could not record all of this command's changes" (its diffs still show), or "Not recorded" with nothing listed. `skipped`: "No diff taken: this command moves the working tree (like git checkout or git stash)" (documented, never seen locally: synthetic fixture). `shared`: "Another command ran in this repository at the same time: some of these changes may be its", only when something is listed: with nothing listed it says nothing about this call (44 real results), so nothing shows. |
| 6 | What "unknown" means | A Bash result without `bashEditDiff` (an older Claude Code, recording off, a failed command, whose result is a string) shows nothing extra: not "no changes". Nothing is derived from the command's text or from a later `git diff` (that would hand one call changes from others). |
| 7 | What "proposed" means | A call still running whose diffs came with its input (a Codex `apply_patch` before its output): "Proposed: not applied yet", dashed. Claude diffs come only from results, so a running Claude Edit/Write shows as running, without a diff: **nothing is synthesized from a tool's input** (rejected: an `old_string`/`new_string` diff, which has no line numbers and would have to be withdrawn on failure). |
| 8 | The other states | From the call's status: applied (`ok`), failed (`error`: "may not have been applied, or only in part"), denied (new status `denied`: "Denied: not applied"; Claude's `toolDenialKind`, else its rejection text), interrupted ("may be only partly applied"; "partially applied" is this and `unavailable`, no source says more). Not applied = dashed. A failed, denied or interrupted Claude edit has no diff: its status says it. |
| 9 | Large changes | The bridge's 64 KiB list cap stays. A diff we cut is tagged `cut` and offers "Show the whole change" (`transcript.item`, a subagent's with its agent), redrawn in place; the source's own gaps (`no_diff`, files without a diff, `unavailable`) offer nothing, since there is nothing more to get. The view draws 2,000 lines of a file at a time: past that, "Show next N lines (M left)" adds the next 2,000 in place (the reading position stays, and how many show survives a re-render of the row), so every line of a whole change is reachable without drawing tens of thousands at once. A file's lines scroll in their own box (26em high, but never taller than the timeline's view less 3em for the file's header: its measured height, `--tx-view-h`, since 26em is more than the view at 200% on a laptop's screen), which takes the focus (Tab, labelled "Changes to <path>", ring inside it): there the timeline's keys (PageUp/PageDown, Space, the arrows, Home/End, j/k) scroll it instead of the timeline. Where the box goes no further that way, and on Home/End, the key moves the timeline toward the box's edge that way while the view does not show it, on either side (a resize can leave the whole box above or below the view): a key's step at a time, never past that edge, so its first and last lines always come into view from the keyboard (rejected: handing the key to the timeline, whose PageDown/End would jump past the file). |
| 10 | Claude's recording setting | Display only: herdr-3d never reads or writes `bashEditDiffEnabled` (user or managed settings only; unset records in auto and bypassPermissions modes). |
| 11 | Coverage limits (Claude Code's, best effort) | No diff for files git ignores, files in submodules, background commands and read-only commands; at most 5 file diffs and 200 changed paths per call; MCP tools and anything outside Edit / Write / Bash are not covered; overlapping commands can contribute changes (`shared`). Requires Claude Code 2.1.269+. |

## 2026-10-08 — #206: syntax colours inside the transcript's diff rows

The transcript drew a diff as plain text on a tinted row; the Claude Code terminal colours the code inside every line
on a solid green or red band. Diff rows are now syntax-coloured with highlight.js (already the transcript's lazy
highlighter, `lib/common`). Code: `game/src/laptop/diffTokens.ts` (language, hunk sides, the line splitter),
`diffHighlight.ts` (scheduling, the worker, painting rows as highlights), `diffHighlight.worker.ts`, `transcriptRender.ts`
(`diffRows`, `diffLines`), `transcript.css`, `ui/themes.ts`; tests `laptop/diffTokens.test.ts`,
`laptop/transcriptChanges.test.ts`, `ui/themes.test.ts`, `e2e/h206-diff-highlight.spec.ts` (fixture timeline
`demo-h206`: `?ws=%2Ffixture%2Fdemo-h171%3Fsim%3D1%26simtx%3Ddemo-h206`, sit at Nibble on herdr-3d).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which highlighter | **highlight.js**, already shipped and themed (the issue's benchmark: Prism faster but a second grammar set; Shiki 5–15× slower). Never `highlightAuto`: the language is the path's extension through highlight.js's own aliases plus a small map (`EXT_LANGUAGE`: tsx, jsx, mjs, cjs, mts, cts, pyi, htm, vue, mdx, jsonl…). No extension (`Makefile`, `.env`) or an unknown one stays plain. |
| 2 | The unit | A hunk's two sides, each tokenized whole (old: context + deleted; new: context + added) and split back onto rows; context rows take the new side's. A template literal or block comment spanning rows colours right. A hunk that starts inside one can still be wrong, as in the terminal. |
| 3 | When | Rows draw plain, exactly as before, then gain colours: hunks up to 12,000 characters are tokenized on the page's thread in ~8 ms background tasks (`scheduler.postTask`, background), larger ones (a created 2,000-line file is one hunk, ~100 ms) in a lazily made Web Worker with its own highlight.js (its own lazy chunk); without one they stay plain. Results are cached (2M characters) for a diff drawn again. `?diffhl=off` turns it off, `?diffhl=trace` measures it. |
| 3a | How rows are painted | With the **CSS Custom Highlight API**, not spans: a row stays the one text node it was drawn as, and each token colour is a highlight (`CSS.highlights`: `tx-hl-comment`, `-keyword`, `-string`, `-number`, `-title`, `-attr`, `-meta`, the `.hljs-*` rules' seven colours, `diffTokens.HL_COLOURS`; `::highlight()` rules in `transcript.css`) holding a `StaticRange` per token. Spans (the first version) restyled and laid out every row they went into: colouring a large diff on screen cost 8–25 extra long tasks of 50–200 ms in the e2e; ranges cost a repaint only. A token in nested scopes takes the innermost one that has a colour (`${}` in a template string is the string's, a number in it the number's), as a span's colour would be inherited. `::highlight()` takes colours only: **comments are not italic** in diff rows (Markdown code blocks keep their spans and italics). No API (jsdom, old browsers): rows stay plain, nothing is tokenized. |
| 3b | Which rows hold ranges | Only those around what a diff's box shows: its view and a view's height above and below, while the box is on screen or within a screen of it (an `IntersectionObserver` with `scrollMargin` on each box, its `scroll` events and a `ResizeObserver`; a binary search over the rows' positions). Chromium checks every range in the page again on any change to the DOM: in a probe on the e2e host 20,000 ranges added ~30 ms to every frame that changed anything, 5,000 next to nothing. Painting every drawn row, as spans did, would hold ~35,000 ranges in the e2e's timeline; the window holds a few hundred (224 at its end). Scrolling a box moves the window in the frame it scrolls in (an animation callback). A row is painted only while it is still the one text node with the text it drew; a diff whose box left the page (a re-render, a live update, a loaded full change, the transcript closed) loses its ranges when anything is drawn or painted next, or within 10 s. |
| 4 | Plain text on a band | A coloured row's untokenized text takes the context's colour (`--tx-text`), as in the terminal: the band says added or deleted, and the `+` / `-` sign keeps the row's tint (`--tx-add-text` / `--tx-del-text`). Done with a highlight too: the cell keeps its tint, and `tx-hl-plain` (priority 0, under the tokens' priority 1) paints the text after the sign. A row of a language nobody knows stays tinted whole, as before. |
| 5 | Band weight | Stronger, to the limit where every token is still 4.5:1 on the band over the code ground (a test checks every theme). The terminal's bands (dark: `rgb(34,92,43)`, `rgb(122,41,54)`) are ~2:1 against the ground but leave its comments ~2:1 on them; we stop earlier. Quiet: added `rgb(20 200 60 / 22%)` (was 47 196 106 / 15%), deleted `rgb(232 65 58 / 30%)` (was 16%), and its comment colour lighter (`#8e99b8`, was `#76819f`, 3.9:1 on the old band already). Ubuntu: added `rgb(20 200 60 / 36%)`, the terminal's green hue (the old one read as grey on the purple), deleted `/ 36%`. Line numbers on a band take its hue (`--tx-text-3` alone falls under 4.5:1). |
| 6 | Row geometry | Unchanged: highlights do not take part in layout (spans in Ubuntu Mono Italic made a row 1px taller and needed `line-height: 1`; gone with them). The e2e compares every row's size with colours off and on, in both presets. |
| 7 | `:has()` and spans | `.settings-switch:has(input:disabled) span` made Chromium restyle every `<span>` in the page on any DOM insertion; with the first version's coloured diff (~20k spans) each painted batch did. Rewritten as `input:disabled ~ span` (same elements), kept: any page with many spans paid it. Rule for later: no bare element selector behind `:has()`. |
| 8 | Long tasks (headless Chromium, llvmpipe) | The highlighter's own work stays below 50 ms (largest slice ~22–39 ms: highlight.js compiling a grammar on first use; a frame's painting ≤ 3 ms). With ranges, colours add no long tasks: on − off per phase (open, scrolling the coloured create, the whole change loaded, "Show next") was −1…+1 in four runs of the spec alone and 0…+2 run beside three other specs, where spans gave +6…+22. A CDP trace of opening the transcript, alone on the host, has no page task over 50 ms either way (largest 46 ms with colours, 46 without); the open phase's one long task in the spec is the host's, there with colours off too. The e2e holds "on" to "off" + 3 long tasks per phase, and the page to fewer than 5,000 ranges. |

## 2026-10-08 — Codex 0.162: supported, with instant interrupt off on shared servers

Codex 0.162.0 was installed and hires with the shared-server switch on fell back to Codex's built-in server ("not
verified with Codex 0.162.0 yet"). Verifying it found one behaviour change inside the bridge's path (findings §14).
Code: `internal/codexapp/watch.go` (`SupportedLines`), `launch.go` (`ServerFeatures`), `internal/codexshare/home.go`
(`VerifiedCodex`), the fixture's `startServer`, `mixed_integration_test.go`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Codex 0.162's `instant_interrupt` (on by default: a steer cancels the model request in flight and is recorded at once) | **Off on every shared server**: the launcher passes `-c features.instant_interrupt=false`, 0.160's and 0.161's default, so the bridge's sent/delivered model (#158) holds unchanged on all three lines. Rejected: reworking that model for an immediate record (larger, touches the exactly-once guarantees, and the feature is "under development" upstream). Cost: a line typed into a shared 0.162 worker while it works waits for the model's current step, as on 0.161. Workers hired the usual way keep Codex's default. |
| 2 | Which Codex is supported | `codexapp.SupportedLines` = 0.160, 0.161, **0.162**; `codexshare.VerifiedCodex` adds **0.162.0**. Evidence: findings §14: the whole suite on 0.162.0 with the override, no version skip; on 0.161.0 the fixture-level tests (the bridge suites cannot be pointed at a Codex other than the user's `codex`), and the source (the override restates 0.161's default). |
| 3 | The stand-in for "unsupported" | codex-cli **0.163.0** (and 0.159.9), in place of 0.162.0 (#186 row 7). |
| 4 | The warm re-attach gap (#186 row 8) | Still on 0.162.0; accepted as on 0.161. `MixedHarness` expects the loss on 0.161.x and 0.162.x, strict on 0.160.x. |
| 5 | A wake refused `agent_pane_busy` | **Not part of this change.** Most bridge runs today failed a wake because herdr counts a zombie of the user's tide prompt in the pane's foreground group (findings §14); it is not a Codex change (0.162 passed it twice). A 15 s retry was tried and did not help, so it was dropped. Its own issue. |
| 6 | The fixture's own servers | Start with the same override, placed after `--listen` so the tests' process lookups (`app-server --listen unix://…`) still match. |

## 2026-10-09 — #212 (phase 3 of #209): the accordion review of a dictated hire

A dictated hire always opens a review (`ui/hireReview.ts`, `HireReviewDialog`); the human's Hire is the only thing that
launches. Phase 4 (the dictation bar) opens it with `App.openHireReview(interpretation, desk?)`; until then it is built
and tested against fixture interpretations (`world/hireReviewFixtures.ts`, `window.__h3d.openHireReview`).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | A second launcher? | No. The review reuses the hire dialog's pieces (exported from `ui/hire.ts`: the chip and model pickers with their readable names (#200) and Settings' hidden models (#194), the worktree control, the capacity block), `hire.prepare` for folder, label, worktree and caps, `hireArgs` / `hireBlocker`, the desk rules (`resolveHireDesk`, which `App.openHire` now uses too) and **`HireCoordinator`**: the desk's hold, background mode, outcomes and the once-only key (`review:<dialog>:<epoch>`). |
| 2 | Fallbacks | **None.** `resolveChoice` / `resolveVisibleChoice` replace an unknown model with the first and a missing effort with `high`; the review never calls them (`world/hireReview.ts` has its own pure functions). A value that is missing or not valid is empty and its section is open; a harness change empties the model; a model change keeps a still-supported effort and clears any other (its word kept for the question). The bridge's floor default (`hire.prepare`'s `choice`) is never an agent choice. |
| 3 | Sections | Harness and model, Effort, Task, Location and other hire options. A valid section starts collapsed with its value in the header; a section that needs a choice starts open and stays open until the human closes it (several can be open; they are independent buttons with `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`). Each says where its value came from: **Dictated**, **Resolved by the interpreter**, **Default** (the floor, name or folder the bridge offers), **Your choice** after an edit, **Not given** / **Needs a new choice** for a missing or invalid one. |
| 4 | Effort | Required and never preselected: the open section asks the interpreter's question and lists the model's levels. `answerEffort(level)` takes a dictated reply like a click (marked Dictated; a level the model does not support is refused and changes nothing). |
| 5 | Task | The complete text in an editable box; the header shows a one-line preview. Sent as the first task exactly as it stands (no images: the dictated hire has none). Empty, starting with `--`, or over the prompt cap blocks Hire. |
| 6 | Location | The floor and desk (the first free one, or the one given; a floor that is asleep or changing, a desk someone sits at or another hire holds, is refused with the dialog's own toasts), label, folder, worktree and the caps, all visible. A desk taken meanwhile blocks Hire and offers the next free one; the hard cap blocks it. |
| 7 | Late answers | A `ticket` (`begin()`: the review's epoch and its edit count) goes with a request to the interpreter; `apply(interpretation, ticket)` changes only the fields the human has not edited since, keeps the harness and model together, and is dropped when the review was cancelled or replaced. `hire.prepare` answers are guarded by their own counter and the epoch. A catalog that loses a model clears it (`refresh()`), never switches it. |
| 8 | Nothing launches early | No `<form>`: Enter in a field does nothing, toggles, picks, follow-ups and Cancel send nothing. Hire is a disabled button while a value is missing, with the reason beside it. The focus on opening is the first section that needs a choice, never Hire (the Enter that interpreted must not press it). |
| 9 | On Hire | The reviewed values are snapshotted into `HireRequest` and handed to the coordinator; the state goes to `hiring`, so a second press is ignored (and the coordinator would return the same operation). Background: the review closes at once and the coordinator toasts and beeps. Foreground: success closes it, goes to the worker and toasts as the hire dialog does; a refusal leaves the review as it was. An unknown outcome (timeout, dropped connection) is never retried by itself. |

## 2026-10-09 — #211 (phase 2 of #209): dictated hire requests become proposals, never hires

The bridge reads one dictated sentence into a hire proposal for the review dialog (phase 3), or into `unsupported` /
`unclear`. The research (`docs/research/dictation-hiring.md`) measured the configured LLM plus catalog validation at 50
of 50; this applies it. Code: `internal/hireintent`, `internal/server/hireintent.go`, `internal/proto/hireintent.go`;
protocol: [Hire interpretation](protocol.md#hire-interpretation).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Shape | `internal/hireintent` takes only `ai`'s LLM interface (`TestHireIntentUsesOnlyAIInterfaces`): `Interpret` (one call, `Parse`, `Resolve`) and `Run` (the role's readiness, the bound). No Jev gate (the research measured it rejecting valid examples), no switch or `*.test` of its own: nothing happens until the game sends a sentence, and an LLM that is not `ready` is a `not_ready` result. Only hiring is understood; `intent` leaves room for other explicit intents, none implemented. |
| 2 | The prompt and the LLM's schema | **The research's revised prompt, verbatim** (`prompt.txt` embedded), and its seven keys unchanged, so every recorded live answer replays as is and the dry-run check is the research's setup. The request is the research harness's: JSON mode, 1,400 tokens, no partial answer, no reasoning effort of its own, `{"command", "pending_hire"?, "hire_options"}`. The parse is strict (exactly those keys, enums for `intent` and `harness`); `model` and `effort` are free text so an unknown one becomes a question, not an error. |
| 3 | Dictated vs resolved | The LLM answers an exact catalog id when it resolves a name, so the spoken words of a valid model are not recoverable without changing the schema (which would void the measurement). Provenance says it instead: `dictated` when the value itself is said as a word, `resolved` when the catalog supplied it for what was said (a label, an alias, a harness from its model), `invalid` keeps the unrecognised words in `said`. |
| 4 | Validation | Every value against the bridge's **current** catalog, on every call: exact ids and readable labels (any case), the harness named or, with none, exactly one harness; a model of the other harness, unknown or contradictory is `invalid` and asked about with the catalog's choices, never substituted. No defaults from floor settings, the last hire, `hire.prepare` or the game's `resolveChoice`. The tuple rule is one: `hireintent.Catalog.Check`, which the bridge's `validHireIn` (worker.hire, settings, presets) now calls; `proto` cannot be imported there (it imports the feature's types), so the server converts `proto.HireOptions` field for field. |
| 5 | Effort | Required, never defaulted: missing asks exactly "What effort level should this agent use?" with the model's levels (common ∪ extra). On top of the prompt's rule, an effort counts only when its word (or "extra high", "maximum") is said **outside the task span**: a task word the LLM mistakes for the effort is dropped, and asking is the safe side. |
| 6 | The task | A **source span** in UTF-16 code units (packup's units) into the sentences, found deterministically: the latest sentence holding the LLM's task text, its last occurrence there (a hire wrapper comes before its task). Not an exact part of what was said: `bad_span`, no proposal. |
| 7 | Follow-ups without state | The bridge keeps nothing. The client sends the pending proposal back; its sources go to the LLM as `pending_hire` (the research's history), its task span and field shapes are checked (`bad_pending`; a valid harness must be `claude` or `codex`), its values resolved again against the current catalog: a harness with no models there is `invalid` (said kept, not carried to the next reply) and the model question offers every model of the catalog, never an empty list nor another harness's model in its place. A field the LLM leaves out keeps the pending value; the task changes only to a part of the reply; a model change drops an effort the new model lacks to `missing`. At most 6 sentences. |
| 8 | Ready means review | `ready_for_review` = every field valid and nothing to ask. Nothing in the result launches; phases 3 and 4 open the review and wait for the human's confirmation. |
| 9 | Failures | Operational errors are the result's `error {code, message}` (the `ai.Kind`, `bad_answer`, `bad_span`, `bad_pending`), the text kept, no proposal; the command fails only on bad args. Messages are fixed text (a provider's may echo the sentence). Bounded at 35 s on the bridge: the AI integration's two 15 s attempts. |
| 10 | Logs and audit | Like `packup.detect`: never a sentence. The log has the intent, readiness, question count and error code; the audit the text's length and the pending sources' count. |
| 11 | Evidence | `go test ./internal/hireintent` replays the 50 bench cases with their recorded live answers (`testdata/bench.json`, the live catalog recorded with them), asserting outcome, exact task, span and every field's provenance; `docs/research/dictation-hiring-bench/dryrun` replays them live through the package, the original texts against the live catalog (a dry-run interpretation check, not a launch test; its result is recorded in the pull request, not committed). The unit-test fixture holds only the selected models (four Claude models and `gpt-6.1-sol`): its four GPT-6-Sol cases name GPT-6.1-Sol, their recorded answers `gpt-6.1-sol`. |

## 2026-10-09 — #210 (phase 1 of #209): Hire in background and the hire coordinator

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The setting | Settings → Display → "Hire in background", right after "Go to a new hire after hiring" (`HirePrefs.hireInBackground`, `ui/hirePrefs.ts`, key `h3d.hire.background`, browser-stored). **Off by default**; only "on" is stored. Off is the existing foreground hire, unchanged. |
| 2 | One path for every hire | `HireCoordinator` (`ui/hireCoordinator.ts`, no DOM) runs a hire from Hire to its outcome: resolve the first task (images upload) and hold the desk (in the order of row 4), send `worker.hire` once, let the desk go. The dialog's foreground and background hires both go through it; the app owns one instance (`App.hires`) for later callers (dictation, #209). |
| 3 | The snapshot | The dialog hands it what was reviewed when Hire was pressed: the args (copied and frozen), the draft (its `resolve()` is called at once, so later edits are not sent) and the background setting, read once. A setting changed on the way does not change how that hire ends. |
| 4 | When the desk is held | **Background:** at Hire, synchronously, before the images upload (the dialog is gone at once, so nothing else would keep another hire off the desk while they go up). **Foreground:** as before #210: the images upload first, then the desk is held for `worker.hire` (Esc while they upload leaves the desk free and its dialog opens again; an image that does not upload never held it). A first task without images holds the desk at once in both. A hire that ends any way lets go of its own hold only. |
| 5 | Background outcome | The dialog closes at once, the player stays and plays on. Success: exactly **"Agent spawned successfully."** and one `spawned` tone (`audio.playUi`, a short G5, under the master mute and volume), never through the inbox, its pulses, desktop notifications or pings, never a go-to. A Codex fallback warning is its own warn toast 3.8 s later (the HUD has one toast slot). |
| 6 | Background failures | An image that did not upload, a refusal, or `prompted: false` (the worker is there, its task is not) is one error toast naming the desk; the draft stays for a retry or a prompt by hand. On `prompted: false` the desk is the new worker's, so its hire dialog does not open: the task as sent (Drop Zone paths in it, uploaded images as chips, not uploaded again) goes into that worker's Prompt (P) note (`HireCoordinatorHost.holdTask` → `PromptOverlay.holdTask`), only when that note is empty, and the toast says "…prompt them yourself (P); the task is in their prompt." (a note already written in is left as it is, and the toast then points nowhere). Never a second `worker.hire`. The foreground keeps its own message (the dialog's text). A timeout or a dropped connection is an outcome-unknown warning: the hire may have happened, so it is never sent again. |
| 7 | Once | A submission has a key (the dialog and its generation): a second Enter while it runs is the same operation, and each operation says its outcome at most once. |
| 8 | Simulator | `simfail=prompt_undelivered` acks the next hire with a first task `prompted: false`; `simclock=manual` holds a hire's ack until `sim.advance` (e2e `h210-hire-background.spec.ts`). |

## 2026-10-09 — #216: the Ubuntu application UI theme

Settings → Appearance picks Quiet or Ubuntu for the whole application shell. The issue's palette, button and field tables,
typography and contrast pairings are the specification; the rows below are what it left to the implementation.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the theme lives | **CSS custom properties under one root attribute.** `ui/uiTheme.css`: `:root` is Quiet, `:root[data-ui-theme='ubuntu']` overrides the same names; `ThemeLoader` writes the attribute, and an inline script in `index.html` writes it first (before any CSS paints) from `h3d.theme.ui`, so a saved Ubuntu never flashes navy.
