A switch is one attribute write: nothing is rebuilt and the 3D scene is not touched. `uiTheme.ts` keeps the ids, the key, the swatches and the copy; `uiTheme.test.ts` pins the Ubuntu values to the issue's table and the measured contrast ratios. |
| 2 | Quiet stays byte-for-byte | Every Quiet token equals the literal it replaced, and the legacy names (`--ink`, `--ink-2`, `--sun`, `--text-on-ink*`, `--paper`) stay as aliases. `tools/ui-quiet-check.mjs` expands the Quiet tokens in each stylesheet, drops the `ubuntu:begin` … `ubuntu:end` blocks and diffs against the commit before the work; what it still prints is deliberate (the `--well` re-declarations removed from `.desk-dlg` and `.changes`, which would have beaten the theme's own; the first `color` / `font-family` the transcript's chrome now sets itself; a fallback written as a token). Before / after screenshots of every surface at 1280 and 390 px (`e2e/ui-theme-shots.spec.ts`, `tools/ui-shot-diff.mjs`; since the #215 merge the before build is origin/main, `--base origin/main`) confirm it on pixels. |
| 3 | Roles, not hues | The old names were overloaded: `--ink` was a panel, a bar and the dark text on a bright fill; `--sun` an action fill, the focus ring, accent text and "warning"; `--needs_you` both a status and an error. They are split into `--surface` / `--chrome` / `--raised` / `--hover` / `--well`, `--action` (+ hover, active, `--on-action`, border) / `--focus` / `--accent-text` / `--warn`, and `--needs_you` / `--error-*`. A state the issue says must not be colour alone (toasts, chips, the picker) keeps its sign or label. |
| 4 | Ubuntu-only rules | At the end of each stylesheet between `/* ubuntu:begin */` and `/* ubuntu:end */`: disabled without opacity (raised fill, muted label, strong border), the busy spinner (`aria-busy`, now set on the primary button of Hire, Start run, Send, presets, the settings panel, Open PR and the board's confirmations; the existing guards already refuse a second submit), 36 / 32 px minimum sizes and 44 px on a coarse pointer, the dark keyline that separates the focus ring from a bright fill, forced-colors rules for state drawn by fill. Quiet gets none of them. |
| 5 | Mono ownership | `--font-mono` is the UI theme's (JetBrains Mono stack in Quiet, Ubuntu Mono and its box / symbol fallbacks in Ubuntu); the transcript preset now sets `--tx-mono` (a literal stack), `--tx-prose` and `--tx-*` only. **Consequence:** with Quiet UI and an Ubuntu transcript the shell's numbers and keycaps are JetBrains Mono, where before they followed the transcript to Ubuntu Mono; all Quiet is unchanged. `--tx-sun` and `--tx-on-sun` no longer read the shell's tokens. |
| 6 | The seated screen's widgets | App chrome inside the transcript (the Conversation / Images bar, seat tabs, zoom and theme controls, composer, Send, jump chip, question answers, banners' buttons, tool toggles) reads `--ctl-*` and the action tokens; messages, code, diffs, tool output and status annotations keep `--tx-*`. The `--ctl-*` Quiet values are the blue and navy those widgets had with the default transcript, so the default screen is unchanged. **Accepted consequences, as the issue's ownership rule asks:** Quiet UI over an Ubuntu transcript shows Quiet's blue controls on the purple transcript (they used to follow its orange), and Ubuntu UI over a Quiet transcript shows orange controls on the navy one. Every Ubuntu rule there is `:root[data-ui-theme='ubuntu'] .tx …` (0,3,0 or more), so `.tx button { color: inherit }` (#111, #172 row 9) cannot out-rank an app button; `e2e/ui-theme.spec.ts` measures Send and the tabs inside the transcript. |
| 7 | Fonts | One lazy stylesheet (`fonts/ubuntu.css`, #172) for all three picks. Ubuntu UI imports it and asks for Ubuntu 400 / 700 and Ubuntu Mono 400 at once, so the shell has its faces with both seated views on Quiet; a terminal-only Ubuntu still fetches Ubuntu Mono and no proportional file (a file loads when text uses its face); all Quiet fetches no Ubuntu file. The real 400 / 700 faces are used for every weight the shell asks for (600 to 850 resolve to 700, `font-synthesis: none` forbids a faked bold) and `font-variation-settings` becomes `normal` (`--fvs-*`): no Ubuntu Sans, no new dependency or licence entry. A failed font load leaves the stack's fallbacks. |
| 8 | The floor overview | It keeps its own layout in Ubuntu but not its own palette: its `--ov-*` colours map to the UI tokens and its display and data faces (Archivo, Departure Mono) become Ubuntu Bold and Ubuntu Mono (`--display-font`, `--data-font`; Quiet keeps them). Its rows keep the status colours of the shared palette. |
| 9 | Board cards | In Ubuntu the DOM cards are `--raised` with light text and a strong border; the data-driven `--paper` / `--pin` (the label tint of the physical corkboard note, set in `panel.ts`) survive as a stripe and the pin dot. The corkboard textures and the 3D notes are untouched. |
| 10 | The whiteboard editor | Excalidraw stays on `theme: 'light'`: its dark theme sets `--theme-filter: invert(93%) hue-rotate(180deg)` on the canvas, which would repaint the drawing and its exports. Its UI is themed through its own custom properties, overridden on `.excalidraw` (not scoped to `.wb`: the dialogs it mounts in an `.excalidraw-modal-container` and the library are children of `body`; tooltips are `.excalidraw-tooltip`), in `whiteboard/editor.css`, which loads with the editor (the start bundle still contains no Excalidraw CSS: `tools/check-bundle.ts`). Drawing data, background, fonts and exports never read these properties. |
| 11 | Toolbars over the world | Surfaces that sit on the 3D world carry an opaque Ubuntu background (`--hint-bg`, the plan labels, the lightbox's top strip): a scrim alone does not give a text a contrast ratio. `e2e/ui-theme.spec.ts` fails a text sample whose background is translucent. |
| 12 | Checks | `e2e/ui-theme.spec.ts`: Appearance semantics and copy; live switching with Settings and Hire open (focus, draft, 3D rebuild count, commands sent); reload before the first frame; invalid, blocked and throwing storage; fonts (lazy, UI-only, terminal-only, real bold); exact palette anchors on real elements; a stray scan of every surface in an all-Ubuntu page for Quiet's colours and fonts; text contrast against the colour that is really behind it on every surface; the eight combinations; zoom and mode switches; 390 px and 200 % zoom; touch sizes; forced colors; reduced motion. Debug hooks: `__h3d.theme().ui`, `__h3d.setUiTheme`, `__h3d.toast`. |
| 13 | What was already wrong in Quiet, left alone | `.hud button { color: inherit }` (0,1,1) out-ranks `.floor-plate`'s own `#0b1022`, so Quiet's floor plate has always drawn the light bar text on the yellow (the comment claiming 11.7:1 is not true of the page); and `--tx-text-3` on `--tx-bg-2` (the strip's counts and durations) is 4.26:1 in Quiet. Quiet must look as it did, so neither is changed; Ubuntu sets the plate's dark label in a rule that wins (0,3,0) and its own muted text is 5.69:1 on the raised purple. |
| 14 | Busy, disabled | One precedence: `[aria-busy='true']` wins over `[aria-disabled='true']` (a button that is working is also not accepting a second press, but must not look disabled: a hire in flight stays orange with a spinner). Every Ubuntu disabled rule says `:not([aria-busy='true'])`; the e2e spec holds a real hire open (the manual simulator clock) and measures it. |
| 15 | Glyphs | Ubuntu's faces have no warning sign (⚠) and a missing glyph is a box: signs are drawn (masks), as the toasts' always were; arrows and the Command key come from the system font, as before. |
| 16 | The prompt overlay's backdrop | It is a modal `<dialog>` like every other, so in Ubuntu its `::backdrop` is the standard scrim, `rgb(44 0 30 / 72%)` (`--scrim-light`); the dialog still sits low, so the worker above it stays in view, dimmed. Quiet keeps its own lighter 22 %. The full-screen viewers (the image lightbox, the composer's image preview, the whiteboard) are the 92 % one. `uiTheme.test.ts` pins all of them. |
| 17 | Colours the table has no row for | Each Ubuntu value is a table token or is kept for a stated reason. **Table values used as a fill:** the seat bar's label tape (`--tape-label-bg`) and the image viewer's INPUT badge (`--tag-fill-neutral`, white before) are the secondary-text tint `#E8D5E2` with the dark label (13.4:1): labels, not buttons, and nothing is white-filled; a volume slider's filled track (`--slider-fill`) is the accent `#FF9B76` (5.42:1 against its `#5E2750` rest track, where the raw orange would be 3.06:1); highlighted code and paths in dialogs (`--code-hl`) are the secondary text `#E8D5E2`, set apart by Ubuntu Mono on the well. **Swapped to the table:** the prompt bubble's and the start slip's failed-upload chip (`--chip-error-bg`, was `#4A1426`) is the raised `#3B1330` with the red border and state, as the composer's own error chip. **Kept off-table:** the Claude provider tape in the limits plate (`--tape-claude #E7A98F`, dark label 9.3:1): it is the provider's identity, like an artwork swatch, and Ubuntu orange is reserved for actions. |
| 18 | The limits tab in a thin gutter | Quiet's snug tab is 22 px in a 27 px gutter beside the seated laptop's glass. Ubuntu's smallest target is 32 × 32, so its snug tab is 32 px wide, flush with the screen's edge, and `limits.ts` asks 34 px of room for it (2 clear of the glass); with less it is away, as when Quiet has no room (Shift+L still peeks the plate). A live switch places it again (a `MutationObserver` on `data-ui-theme`). |
| 19 | The hint above a wrapped look-at bar | The hint sat at a fixed 118 px, room for a two-line look-at bar. A third line (#215's report request line, 111 px at 1280) put it 11 px over the bar, and at 390 px, where the bar wraps further, it covered the bar's first lines, in both themes. A deliberate Quiet change (it fixes Quiet too): The bar's height is now a custom property (`--prompt-h`, a `ResizeObserver` as `--topbar-bottom`) and the hint sits 8 px above it, never lower than its old 118 px, so a desktop bar of the usual height is where it always was. |
| 20 | #215's report request in the theme | Merged after the theme: its new CSS asks for roles with Quiet's values unchanged (`ui-quiet-check --base origin/main`). The inbox row's full-request well is a new token, `--req-well` (Quiet the faint white wash it had, Ubuntu the recessed `#2C001E` with the strong border); the "Show full request" disclosure is a link in the accent colour, its underline thicker on hover; the crew line's "Shift+N: full request" is secondary text. The look-at bar's request line and the expanded inbox row are surfaces of the e2e inventory. |
| 21 | Which controls are dense | **Standard, ≥ 36 px high:** every labelled `.btn`, `.btn--sm` included (Save, Retry, Open PR, Edit, the limits plate's Refresh and Hide meter are actions: 36 px in Ubuntu, Quiet keeps its 30), and every text field and select (the building rename field and a tracker status row's field were 32 px; the palette's field 27). **Dense, ≥ 32 × 32:** icon-only buttons, tabs, chips, menu and context-menu rows, disclosures (`<summary>`), the volume slider, the transcript's link buttons (`.tx-link`: 32 px, which makes a pending row's meta line taller), the limits tab (row 18). A control drawn smaller reaches it with an absolutely placed hit box (the seat bar's 18 px theme and zoom buttons, 32 px). **Exempt:** text links in a line or a list (the issue's link row; WCAG 2.5.8's inline exception) and the whiteboard editor's colour swatches and opacity slider (Excalidraw's own dense palette of artwork colours). A coarse pointer keeps 44 × 44. The larger controls reflow, never shrink text: at 1280 the Ubuntu transcript bar wraps its theme and zoom group to a second row. `e2e/ui-theme.spec.ts` (`targets:`) measures every control of every inventory surface in Ubuntu, the limits plate included. |
| 22 | #212's hire review in the theme | Merged after the theme; its CSS asks for roles with Quiet's values unchanged (`ui-quiet-check --base origin/main`). New tokens, Quiet = the literal replaced: `--attention-border` / `--attention-bg` (a section that needs a choice; Ubuntu the warning `#FFC83D` outline, doubled by an inner line, on the raised fill: its question and its tape say what is missing, never the hue alone) and `--tape-resolved` / `--tape-default` (origin tapes; Ubuntu `#8EC9FF` and `#CDB1C6` with the dark label). In Ubuntu a section header is a menu row (hover fill, pressed keyline) and an open one (`aria-expanded`) is the selected tab: orange with the dark label, its ring 2 px off with a dark keyline; the review's chips keep #212's 44 px on touch and at a phone's width (the 32 px chip rule out-ranked it). The review's Hire in flight is busy, not disabled (`aria-busy` + `aria-disabled`, rows 4 and 14), as the hire dialog's. **Deliberate Quiet change:** at ≤ 560 px a header's origin tape sits under its value; beside the title it covered the title's end in both themes. Surfaces `hire-review`, `hire-review-invalid`, `hire-review-ready` in the e2e inventory. |
| 23 | The seated screen's widget sizes, the popup's mono | Ownership (row 6) covers size too: the transcript's zoom (`--tx-fs`, `--tx-k`, `--tx-mono-fs`) enlarges the conversation, never the prompt box (14px), Send (36px), the jump chip (12px), the banner's choices, the question cards' answer controls (options 14px, their keys 22px, Send and Done 14px, meta 12–13px) or their icons. The completion popup's commands and paths and a file chip's name are the composer's data: `--font-mono` at 14px (Ubuntu Mono with a Quiet transcript too, at any zoom). Ubuntu rules only: **Quiet keeps today's behaviour** (its widgets zoom with the transcript; its popup rows keep the transcript's mono), as row 2 requires. `e2e/ui-theme.spec.ts` zooms 100 → 200 and measures both themes. |
| 24 | The focus ring is never inward | The issue's 3px ring 2px outside the widget, everywhere the fix round looked (the composer, the seated screen, the whiteboard editor); where a widget fills a box that clips, the box makes the room instead of the ring going inside: the timeline's scroller stands 5px inside the screen (its padding 5px less, so the conversation does not move), a diff's scrolling body 5px inside its card, a question card's options 5px inside it, the short screen's sticky card top and foot 6px; a composer chip no longer clips (its upload bar rounds its own corners), a changed file's card lets its header's ring out (the header rounds its corners), and a widget scrolled into view keeps 6px (`scroll-margin`). The editor itself (the whiteboard's canvas container, which fills the board's clipping frame) has the board wear its ring, 2px outside the frame. Likewise the prompt dialog's speech bubble wears its editor's ring (the editor fills the bubble; two rings would sit side by side on its border). **The rest of the UI, the same way:** the top bar menu's rows, the inbox's rows and close button, the palette's hint, an overview row, the Settings volume slider ring 2px outside (they were −3 … 1px); the scrollers that take the focus themselves stand 5px inside the box that clips them (every credits body, i.e. credits, shortcuts, palette and Settings, 5px from its dialog's sides and foot; the plan-limits plate's body, its padding 5px less; the changes panel's scroller), and the scrollers whose rows fill them give 5px (the inbox list, the overview board, the palette's foot 10px). Every widget keeps 6px `scroll-margin`, and a scroller under a sticky bar pads past it (`scroll-padding`: the credits' genre heading 52px, a form's sticky Save bar 72px), so a ring brought into view is neither cut nor covered. In the whiteboard editor a colour swatch's own 1px-off ring (an `::after` border, off on some swatches) becomes the outline 2px off on every swatch, a dialog's content (help, Mermaid) rings when it takes the focus, and the library trigger Excalidraw leaves in the keyboard's path (its picture is hidden, row 10) goes with its checkbox. The focus inventory test now runs on **every** inventory surface and holds every widget to the geometry: 3px `#FF9B76`, offset 2px (or a 3px shadow ring), inside every box on its containing-block chain that clips (a fixed widget, the plan-limits tab inside a top-layer dialog, is not clipped by that dialog); a hidden native input's ring is looked for on the control drawn for it, an editor that fills a clipping frame on the frame. **Exactly, settled, at both widths:** the inventory runs at 1280×720 and 390×844, measures each ring after its focus transition has finished, wants offset exactly 2px and width exactly 3px (no 3 or 4px offsets), fails a ring that a sticky bar lies over, and walks every surface's widgets backwards too (Shift+Tab scrolls up: a widget comes in at a scroller's top edge, under a sticky heading if there is one). What it found: the palette's foot shrank (flex) and gave up its room, now `flex-shrink: 0` (the list gives way); an Overview row's 1px lift on focus took its settled ring past the board's top, now no lift in Ubuntu (like the boards' cards) and 6px board room; the sound sliders (Quiet 4px) and the Images tiles (3px) ring 2px off; the whiteboard phone tray holds its tools 5px in; a property-panel choice's hidden radio covers its label and a Floor Settings tracker card's radio has a scroll-margin to the card's edges (scrolling up to either brought only its corner into view); the timeline's scroll-padding went (with the widgets' 6px `scroll-margin` it made 11px, more than a phone's 89px timeline could give an image card) and on a short screen a missing image's card is compact. A board card focused mid-move wears the focus ring, not its dashed moving edge. |
| 25 | A short seated screen | At 200% browser zoom the laptop's screen is some 140px tall and Ubuntu's 32 / 36px strip took all of it. Nothing is hidden or shrunk: under 300px of screen the strip keeps to one row that scrolls sideways (5px above and below for the ring), the timeline keeps half the screen, and the screen itself scrolls (Ubuntu's `.tx` is `overflow-y: auto`) when the strip, a banner, the timeline and the prompt box are more than it holds, so the conversation and the prompt box are always reached. On a laptop-sized screen the strip's second row (row 21) leaves the timeline its room; the layout test measures both. |
| 26 | The whiteboard editor's own controls, further | **Phone width:** Excalidraw sizes its phone toolbar for a full-window editor (98vw) and shrinks tools to 1.8rem under 450px; inside the board that pushed tools past the frame. In Ubuntu the toolbar fits the board, its tools keep 32px (44px on a coarse pointer) and wrap onto a second row, centred, and the lock / hand column moves down a row. **Focus:** a tool is a native radio or checkbox drawn invisible: the ring goes on its icon (`input:focus-visible + .ToolIcon__icon`), a property-panel choice on its label. **Forced colours:** Excalidraw paints icons with `color` on SVGs, which forced colours leaves alone, so Ubuntu's white icons vanished on the system Canvas: its tokens map to CanvasText / Canvas, a selected tool to Highlight / HighlightText. **Welcome:** the empty board's heading and hints are the editor's UI, in Excalidraw's hand-drawn face by class: Ubuntu UI text in Ubuntu; text on the board keeps its author's font (row 10). |
| 27 | Stamps, floor plates, busy deletes | A reviewer's verdict stamp has tokens (`--stamp-bg`, `--stamp-pass`, `--stamp-changes`, `--stamp-incomplete`; Quiet the white paper and inks it had): in Ubuntu the raised purple with the status colour as ink and border, the verdict's word kept. The elevator's and Buildings' floor plates are, in Ubuntu, a secondary control (raised purple, strong ring, white number; the row's hover lights the ring) and where you are (`aria-current`) and a press are the selected tab, orange with the dark number; Quiet keeps brushed steel. A busy button that is also natively disabled (the preset's final Delete) is busy, not disabled (row 14 now covers `:disabled` too): the red fill with its dark label and spinner. |

## 2026-10-09 — #215: a report request can be resolved without resuming the worker, and is kept whole

A coder that ended its turn with `input_required` in its report stayed NEEDS YOU / "Waiting for an answer" after the orchestrator accepted the request in its ledger and moved on: nothing in the report changed. The request was also cut to 500 bytes, losing the decision itself.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The resolution signal | A **sidecar file**, `<run>/<stem>.resolved.json` next to `<run>/<stem>.json` (`runs.ResolvedName`), `{"report", "resolved_by"?, "note"?}`, written atomically by the orchestrator. File-based like the report itself: it survives a bridge restart with no store (`TestReportRequestResolution` starts a second bridge over the same directory). No command or protocol call: the orchestrator already writes files in this directory and the bridge never writes there. |
| 2 | Binding | The sidecar resolves the report only if its **mtime ≥ the report's**, compared at read time. A report rewritten later (a new request) asks again; a fresh report path starts over as before. The sidecar is **bound to its report** (round 2): it must carry a string `"report"` equal to the report's file name, so derived names that collide (`coder-r1` / `coder-r1.json`) and another worker's report JSON dropped there resolve nothing. Names ending `.resolved.json` are **reserved**: `runs.ValidReport` makes one an invalid report (`Initial`, `reportWorkers`), so a worker never occupies a sidecar path. A name whose sidecar would exceed 80 bytes has none. |
| 3 | Safety | The same reads as the report: `Lstat`, `O_NOFOLLOW`, `O_NONBLOCK`, regular files only, 4 KiB cap; garbage, a non-object, non-string fields (`null` included), a missing or different `report`, a symlink, a FIFO or an oversize file is **ignored** (the request stays active: a broken resolution must never silence a real request). Note cut to 300 bytes, `resolved_by` to 40, UTF-8 safe. |
| 4 | Tracking | The poller (`reportTracker`, off the hub goroutine) stats the report **and** its sidecar and re-reads when either stamp changed, so creating, replacing or deleting the sidecar is one `worker.upsert` within the poll interval. |
| 5 | Wire | `report.input_resolved {by, note?, at}`; `input_required` stays present (the card shows the note, the text is history). Also `input_truncated` and `input_for`. |
| 6 | An unresolved request is never suppressed by `done` | The agent being `done` says nothing about whether its report's request was settled; the report is the only source. Resolved, the request contributes nothing: `crew.ts` `inputRequired` is null, so lifecycle falls back to the real status, `needsYou` is false, and every consumer (counts, N, inbox, badges, bubbles, delivery) follows because they all read `display`. The delivery walk treats a resolved request like an answered one (the folder is handed in). |
| 7 | The whole request | `MaxInputRequired` 8 KiB (reports are ≤ 256 KiB), UTF-8 safe, flagged `input_truncated`. The bubble and the bar only preview it (the layout cuts); the inbox row has a "Show full request" disclosure (`aria-expanded`, wraps, scrolls, keyboard-focusable); the bar's title and label carry the whole text. |
| 8 | Wording and who decides | "Report request", never "Waiting for an answer" (the agent asked no question); "Asked of the orchestrator (Cog)" / "Asked of you": the report's optional `input_for`, else the orchestrator when the worker has one (`orch_id`), else you. Who is counted is unchanged: an unresolved request is still NEEDS YOU. A worker with a real native question keeps its own card. |

## 2026-10-09 — #226: the overview row gives the now line a line of its own

On the floor overview the AI badge, its age and the live activity chip shared the now line and never shrank, so the now summary (up to `NowMax` = 100 runes) was what gave way: at 1280 × 720 a full-budget line got 200 of the 688 px it needs, at 1440 px 305. The Worker column meanwhile held a name and one model line in a full `1fr`. Each thing now has the column that matches its rhythm.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The live activity chip (#98) | The **Worker column's**, a line of its own under the model meta line (`.ov-who > .ov-actline > .ov-act`): it is about the worker and changes every few seconds; the now line is a 60 s line about the work. It ellipsizes inside the column on its own (never pushes another line), its whole text is its `title`, and the row's spoken label (`rowLabel`) carries it after the model (it did not before). At 760 px it stays in the who area, under the name, before the work. |
| 2 | The now line | **`Now` + the text**, and nothing else but what is about the text itself: the #174 status chip and Retry (and #198's failed refresh) stay at its end, as they did. The flip animation still keys on the now text. |
| 3 | The AI mark of a model's now line (#198) | Under the **since time**, in the since column (`.ov-since .ov-llm`, same badge, `title`, `role="img"`, `aria-label`): both are mono, both are times. `since()` refreshes its age there every tick. The task line's plain `AI` mark stays in its line (it carries no age). The 480 px rules that sent the badge under the now line are gone; the status chip and Retry keep their wrap. |
| 4 | The hint (`Open transcript ↵`, a ghost's `Go there ↵`) | **Takes the since time's place** on hover and keyboard focus, in the same cell (`.ov-since`, `order: -1`), never `position: absolute`, never over another column; the AI mark stays under it (its tooltip stays reachable by pointer). Leave and the time is back. While Retry has the pointer or the focus the hint steps aside and the time shows. Not on a phone (≤ 760 px), as before. |
| 5 | The grid | `34px minmax(206px, 1fr) minmax(0, 4fr) 132px`, `column-gap: 10px` (was `34px minmax(190px, 1fr) minmax(0, 2.3fr) 118px`, gap 18; a floor band of the all-floors page keeps its 18). The since column is as wide as the hint (`Open transcript` and its key in 11 px mono: 131 px); the worker's floor of 206 px keeps a freelancer's indented model line (`claude opus-5-5 medium`, 176 px + the 30 px indent) on one line; the work column takes four parts to the worker's one. A line's label (`Now`, `Task`) is 30 px, the widest (`Task`, 28 px in 11 px mono) and 2 to spare (was 34). The overview's side margin stops at **32 px** (`clamp(16px, 4vw, 32px)`, was 56): only a window wider than 800 px gets less of it, and the board gets that width (the all-floors page's rows shift with it, unchanged otherwise). Measured with a 100-rune now line (`e2e/h226-overview-row.spec.ts`, focused and hovered; the text's room is the line less its label and gap): the text is 688 px in Playwright's Chromium and 704 px in Chrome 149, so the rule is **room ≥ 1.05 × the text** in either, not just no ellipsis. At 1280 × 720 the room is 748 px (1.087× / 1.063×; it was 700), at 1440 px 884 (1.285× / 1.256×). A model's line with a failed refresh still carries its chip and Retry, and gives way to them (#174's rule). |
| 6 | The header | `Worker · doing` over the worker (the name, the model and the live chip), `Working on` over the ticket, the task and the now line (one thing, not three), `Since` over the time and the AI mark. |
| 7 | A row with a live chip and no ticket, task or now line | **`No summary yet`** (quiet italic, where the now line goes): the worker is in a turn (the chip says what it does), so `No turn yet` would be false; the summary is what is missing. A row with neither keeps `No turn yet`. |
| 8 | Not changed | The wall screen (`render/overviewScreen.ts`, never draws the chip), the all-floors page's floor bands (#188), the ticket and task lines, the bridge's budgets (`NowMax`, `TaskMax`, `MaxWaitDetail`; the game clips a detail to 32 itself). |

## 2026-10-09 — #229: the harness is an icon, the model is its name and effort

The overview's meta line read like a log line (`claude opus-5-5 high · coding · pass 0 of 3`): the harness spelt out, a model slug where #200 has a readable name, and an orchestrator's run words. The laptops' stickers were hand-drawn stand-ins (an 11-ray spark on cream, a `>_` on ink).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The harness | **An icon before the worker's name**: the Claude mark for `claude`, the **OpenAI** mark for `codex` (the Codex cloud mark is not used anywhere), drawn **white** (`#fff`; both themes are dark). One source, `render/brandMarks.ts`: each mark's single `<path>` and view box copied verbatim from Wikimedia Commons (`File:Claude_AI_symbol.svg`, CC0, its terracotta fill dropped; `File:OpenAI_logo_2025_(symbol).svg`, public domain, trademarked), the one harness → mark map, an inline `<svg>` for the page (built with `createElementNS`, `role="img"`, `aria-label` and `<title>` `Claude` / `Codex`) and `drawMark` (`Path2D`) for canvases. The row's spoken label keeps the harness name. A harness with no mark gets no icon and says its name before the model, in text. |
| 2 | The meta line | **The model's readable name and effort, nothing else**: `Opus 5.5 high`, `Haiku 5.5 high`; a Codex model by the bridge's `model_labels` label, tidied as #200's pickers do (`GPT 6 Sol high`); an unlabelled GPT slug by rule, the same words (row 7); any other unlabelled id as it is. `derive.ts`'s `modelLabel` became `modelText(harness, model, effort, hireOptions)`, so the look-at bar and the prompt dialog show the same names. `OverviewSource` reads `hireOptions` (the store has it). The look-at bar's redraw key keeps the harness and the exact id (a harness- or id-only upsert still redraws). |
| 3 | Run words | **Out of the meta line**, on the page and on the wall: no phase (`waiting for you` included), no `round N`, no `pass N of M`. The `ORCH` chip says who the orchestrator is, the wall's chip carries the round (`ORCH r2`), and **over cap** is a badge of its own (the page's `Over cap` chip; on the wall an `OVER CAP` chip after the role chip, in both row heights). `orchWords` is gone; the row's `orch` keeps the round and over cap. The 3D crew card keeps its run words. |
| 4 | The wall screen | The same mark, `Path2D`, as tall as the name's capitals and on their baseline; the name starts after it and gives way (ellipsis) to the role and over-cap chips, measured (before, a long name's chip ran into the work column). The work column starts at 36 % of the width (was 33 %) to give the mark its room. An asleep row's mark dims with its name. |
| 5 | The laptop stickers | The `claude` cell carries the Claude mark and the `codex` cell the OpenAI mark, **white on a dark disc** (`#11141c`; the cream and ink discs went); the model family's rim colour still shows round it (the alpha-0 ring, `aRim`). The atlas layout (`claude`, `codex`, `plain`, 128 px a cell) and so the `aLogo` indices are unchanged. |
| 6 | Credits | A **Marks** section after the fonts: each mark with its licence, source and whose trademark it is. |
| 7 | An unlabelled Codex model | **Named by rule, in the words its tidied label would have** (amends #200's fallback, which formatted only Claude slugs and showed a GPT slug as it is: rows read `gpt-6-sol high` on a bridge with the fallback catalog). `world/modelLabel.ts` `codexSlugName`: a strict OpenAI slug `gpt-<major>[.<minor>][-<word>…]` (one or two digits each, lower-case letter words) → `GPT <major>[.<minor>] <Word> …` (`gpt-6-sol` → `GPT 6 Sol`, `gpt-6.1-sol-mini` → `GPT 6.1 Sol Mini`), exactly `spaceSeparators` of the `GPT-6.1-Sol-Mini` label `model/list` reports, so a labelled and an unlabelled model look alike; null for a dated or numbered variant, a second version dot or any other shape. Order in `modelLabel`: the bridge's label (Codex's tidied) → Claude slug → GPT slug → the exact id; the slug rules apply to any harness, as the Claude one already did (the shapes are the vendors' own). Pickers mark such a name `named` (the id beside it or as its tooltip) and duplicate names still show their ids. The bridge's **Codex fallback catalog** (`internal/server/settings.go`) carries `model_labels` too (`GPT-6-Sol`, `GPT-6-Luna`, `GPT-6-Astra`), copied in `HireOptions()`; the game's `DEFAULT_HIRE_OPTIONS` (an older bridge) carries none and relies on the rule. |

## 2026-10-09 — #213 (phase 4 of #209): the dictation command bar

A natural-language bar that takes what dictation software (Wispr Flow: a real paste) puts in it, sends it to `hire.interpret` (#211) on Enter, and routes the answer to the review (#212). It never sends `worker.hire`: only the review's Hire does, through the hire coordinator (#210).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The control | A **separate modal dialog** (`ui/dictation.ts`, `dialog.dictation`), not a palette mode: dictated prose must never fuzzy-match a command. It reuses the palette's input behaviour (modal, the field focused, its keys stopped at the dialog, the opener's focus given back on close, held movement let go and walking stopped on open, `boardsPanel.cancelDrag`) and its frame and place (centred high, clear of the right gutter the plan-limits tab steps into: `corner.ts PALETTE` now names both dialogs). One **`<textarea>`** for the bar's life: never disabled, read-only or rebuilt while an answer is pending or its line changes, so text still arriving lands in it; `input` is the value. |
| 2 | The shortcut | **Ctrl/⌘+Shift+Space** (`dictate`, a `typing` chord, palette row "Dictate a command"; `seat.dictate` lists it for the seat). In the capture phase like ⌘/Ctrl+P (`onDictateKey`), so it works from the city, a floor, a field, under any dialog, the review included (a follow-up is dictated over it), and seated at a laptop in the terminal and the conversation; the pane never gets it (`seatedKey` → `dictate`). Why this chord: every Ctrl/⌘+letter worth having is the browser's (T N W L R K D S F) or the terminal's (readline's Ctrl+A E K U W R…, which the capture phase would steal); Ctrl/⌘+Shift+letter costs the terminal the same Ctrl+letter (it sends upper and lower case alike) or a browser's devtools/private window; Space with Ctrl and Shift is no browser's, no shell's or TUI's, and not the system input-source switch (Ctrl+Space, ⌘+Space). The same chord again, or Esc, closes it. |
| 3 | The mouse | A Menu row, **"Dictate a command…"** (a microphone), above "All commands…", both under one divider: the bar is reached the way the palette is, from every view (the Menu is in the top bar everywhere, seated too). The only Quiet CSS change of this ticket is that pair's divider (`hud.css` `.menu-dictate`); the bar's own CSS is a new file. |
| 4 | Keys in the field | Enter sends (Ctrl/⌘+Enter too, the dialogs' submit); Shift+Enter is a new line; a pasted text keeps its line breaks. An IME's Enter never sends: `isComposing`, `keyCode === 229` (Safari's committing Enter after `compositionend`) and a beat after `compositionend`. Paste and printable keys are never prevented. One request at a time: Enter (or Send) while one is pending does nothing; Send says it is busy (`aria-disabled`), the field stays as it is. |
| 5 | Routing | An ack `error` or a failed request (offline, timeout, disconnect): a direct line in the bar (`ui/dictationModel.ts`), the text kept, nothing hired, Enter tries again. `unsupported` is exactly "Not supported yet.", `unclear` exactly "Your intent is unclear." (neither opens anything). A hire with no review open **always opens the review**, fully specified or not (missing or invalid sections open, as #212 does); the bar closes without handing the keyboard back (the review takes it). A hire with the review open is a **follow-up**: `begin()` before the request, `apply()` after; applied, the bar closes and the keyboard goes back to the review; not applied (the review closed meanwhile, or its hire is on its way) the bar says so. A second review is never opened over one. **An answer belongs to the bar that asked**: closed since (Esc, the chord, the backdrop), or closed and opened again, the game drops it before doing anything with it (`DictationHost.submit(text, live)`, checked in `App.interpretDictation` / `openDictatedReview` right after the await): no follow-up applied, no review, no floor question, no bar closed, the new draft untouched (`e2e/h213-dictation.spec.ts`). |
| 6 | Who owns the pending proposal | The open review: `HireReviewDialog.pendingInterpretation()` (null once closed: Cancel, Esc and a landed hire discard it). The bar reads it at each Enter and keeps no copy; a reply with no review open goes without `pending`, and the bridge answers `unclear`. |
| 7 | The floor | The floor you are on (`hereSession`), which is never ambiguous in the game; with none (the city, a lobby, the street) the bar asks **"Hire on which floor?"** with the palette's floor list (`world/palette.ts`). A picked floor is checked first with the hire's own rules (`resolveHireDesk`: asleep, waking, going to sleep, full, with their messages): refused, the bar says why and the list stays, nothing sent. Esc while it asks closes the bar, as Esc does everywhere in it (the palette's floor list too), with the keyboard given back; opened again, the bar is empty. Otherwise, as a palette command: the bar closes, the modals go, a seat is left, the elevator takes you there, and the review opens at its first free desk (`openHireReview(interp, desk, session)`). The palette rides first and refuses on arrival; for a dictated sentence that would lose the text. |
| 8 | Not a `dialogOpen` dialog | Like the palette. `dialogOpen` blocks the laptops, which stood a seated player up the moment the bar opened; the bar keeps its keys itself. |
| 9 | The fixture's interpreter | `tools/fixture-interpret.ts`: no rule of `internal/hireintent` is ported. One JSON-mode call through the fixture's AI integration to the LLM the game set up (the e2e's fake OpenAI server), whose answer is the final result, checked (shape, intent, the task's span) and passed on with the bridge's sources; errors are the bridge's acks (`not_ready`, `provider_failed`, `timeout` past a bound `sim.interpret` sets, `bad_answer`, `bad_span`, `bad_pending`). The fixture AI's `complete` now says `provider_failed` for a non-200 (it said `bad_response`), as `ai.test` and the bridge's `ai.KindFailed` do; no spec relied on the old code. |

## 2026-10-10 — #214 (phase 5 of #209): the dictated hire end to end, and the rollout

Phases 1 to 4 (#210 to #213) each brought their own unit, DOM and fake-provider e2e tests. This one proves the path
across them in the browser (the bar → `hire.interpret` → the review → Hire → the hire coordinator → the ack → toast,
beep, go-to or none), runs the real configured LLM over the bench and the e2e's sentences, documents the feature
([`dictation.md`](dictation.md)) and decides the rollout. These specs and one full `make e2e`, on this branch from
`origin/main` with phases 1 to 4 merged, are the post-merge verification.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Rollout | **Ship enabled, "Hire in background" off by default** (as #210 shipped it): no flag. The bar, the review and both modes are on for everyone; nothing changes for a player who never opens the bar or the setting. This holds unless the user's manual real-harness smoke test (one Claude Code and one Codex hire dictated in background mode) or the Wispr Flow check on the Mac surfaces problems; then the bar goes behind a flag in a follow-up. |
| 2 | New specs | `e2e/h214-full-path.spec.ts`: background on (Settings switch → Ctrl+Shift+Space → a complete sentence → the populated review, zero `worker.hire` → a model changed → Hire → one `worker.hire` with the reviewed tuple, the review gone at once, the ack held by `simclock=manual`: you walk, nobody seats you, no toast, no beep → the ack: exactly "Agent spawned successfully.", one beep, no inbox item, no notification, no go-to); background off (the same with an effort changed: the review waits in `hiring`, then "Hired … Here they come." and the go-to to the laptop's conversation, no beep); background on and muted (the toast, no sound). `e2e/h214-edge-cases.spec.ts`: one independent test per edge case (row 4). |
| 3 | Shared helpers | Two specs needed each, so they moved: `e2e/dictation.ts` (the fake OpenAI server and its canned answers, `setUpLlm`, the bar's helpers; was in h213) and `e2e/hireProbe.ts` (the beep, toast and notification probe, `simAdvance`; was in h210; its first-load guard is now `sessionStorage['hire-probe']`). h210 and h213 import them, unchanged otherwise. `.ts` files outside `*.spec.ts` are not specs (`playwright.config.ts testMatch`), as `e2e/m7.ts`. |
| 4 | Edge cases | Repeated Enter: in the bar while a SLOW answer is on its way (one `hire.interpret`, one `hire.prepare`, one review), then Enter, Enter, Enter and a click on the review's Hire in the foreground with the ack held (one `worker.hire`; the review is `hiring`). Two dictated hires on their way: desks 14 and 15, each ack its own toast and beep, no cross-talk. A review being edited (an effort picked, the task typed in, the caret in it) while an earlier dictated hire completes: its toast and beep, the review not closed, reset or overwritten, the keyboard still in the task. A dictated follow-up whose interpretation fails (HTTP 500): the review unchanged under the bar, the bar's error line, nothing hired; Esc gives the keyboard back to the review and a working follow-up fills it. A model change after a dictated effort: GPT-6 Sol `ultra` → GPT-6 Astra keeps `ultra`; → GPT-6 Luna clears it (no level in its place, no `ultra` chip), Effort opens with attention, Hire off; a pick turns it on. A dropped connection (`__h3d.reconnect()`) and a timeout: each the outcome-unknown warning, no success toast or beep, one `worker.hire` after the reconnect. The timeout is the client's real one (`net/client.ts`, `worker.hire` 90 s), shortened to 4 s in that test by an init script that maps a 90 000 ms `setTimeout` to 4 000 ms; its late ack brings the worker and says nothing. No fixture knob was needed. |
| 5 | A defect the timeout test found | The background outcome-unknown toast ended "The task stays in its hire dialog." for a dictated hire too, whose review hands the coordinator no draft (`prompt: null`): no hire dialog keeps that task. The sentence is now added only when the hire came with a draft (`HireCoordinator.notify`; unit test in `hireCoordinator.test.ts`); the hire dialog's toast is unchanged. Keeping a failed or unknown dictated hire's task somewhere (the epic's "preserve the submitted task") is not done by the review in background mode: a follow-up. |
| 6 | The dry run | `docs/research/dictation-hiring-bench/dryrun` gained `-extra <file>` (a set `e2e`), p90 and a count of the cases that must not be ready (unclear, unsupported, any `clarify_*`) but came back ready. `e2e-phrases.json` holds h213's and these specs' sentences in the bench's schema, labelled for the live catalog (the fake-only `FAIL` / `SLOW` sentences left out; GPT-6 Luna with `ultra` is a `clarify_selection`: Luna has no `ultra`). Its results stay out of the repository (#211 row 11): the pull request has them, labelled a dry-run interpretation check. |
| 7 | Docs and help text | [`dictation.md`](dictation.md), linked from the README: the setting, the chord and the Menu row, example phrases (not a grammar), "Not supported yet." and "Your intent is unclear." word for word, effort always stated or chosen, the review, background and foreground. `docs/shortcuts.md` already lists the chord (generated, unchanged). The Settings line "Hire in background: close the dialog at once, beep when the agent is up" is accurate for the hire dialog and the review alike and stays. No new dialog, panel or stylesheet: `e2e/ui-surfaces.ts` and `ui-quiet-check` are untouched. |
| 8 | Not automated | The real-harness smoke test (one Claude Code and one Codex hire dictated through the bar in background mode: the worker starts, the task arrives, one toast, one beep, you stay in view) and the Wispr Flow insertion check on the Mac browser are the user's, by hand, recorded in the pull request. |

Coverage, each #214 Work bullet and each #209 acceptance bullet owned by phase 5:

| Bullet | Where |
|---|---|
| Full path, background on (interpretation, review, confirmation, the delayed-ack window, toast, beep, no go-to) | `h214-full-path` "background on: dictated, reviewed and changed…"; muted: "background on and muted…" (the dialog's: h210 "muted: the toast, no sound") |
| Full path, background off (foreground unchanged) | `h214-full-path` "background off (the default)…"; the dialog's: `h116-hire-goto`, h210 "off (foreground)…" |
| Preference persistence, off by default | h210 "the setting is off by default, kept across a reload…"; `hirePrefs.test.ts`; `h214-full-path` reads it unset on a fresh profile |
| Repeated Enter | `h214-edge-cases` "repeated Enter…"; bar only: h213 "provider errors and timeouts…"; Hire click twice: h212 |
| Two independent pending hires | `h214-edge-cases` "two dictated hires on their way…"; the dialog's: h210 "a dialog edited while an earlier hire ends…" |
| Reopening and editing a dialog while an earlier hire completes | `h214-edge-cases` "a review being edited while an earlier dictated hire completes…"; the dialog's: h210, same test |
| Provider error during review | `h214-edge-cases` "a dictated follow-up whose interpretation fails…"; with no review: h213 "provider errors and timeouts…" |
| Model change after a dictated effort | `h214-edge-cases` "a model change after a dictated effort…"; `hireReview.dom.test.ts`, `world/hireReview.test.ts` |
| Disconnect and timeout, unknown outcome | `h214-edge-cases` "a dropped connection…" and "…does not answer in time…"; the dialog's disconnect: h210; both codes: `hireCoordinator.test.ts` |
| Upload, launch and prompt-delivery failures | h210 "an image that does not upload…", "a launch failure and a task not delivered…" (a dictated hire has no images) |
| Fill / paste, Enter, review and zero hires, exact tuple, no duplicates; composition, multiline, focus, all views, floor selection | h213 (the five phrases, input, views, floor tests); edits before Hire: `h214-full-path` |
| Real-provider dry run (pass rate, latency, cost; nothing not-ready comes back ready) | `dryrun -extra e2e-phrases.json`, recorded in the pull request |
| Real-harness smoke test; Wispr Flow | manual, the user's (row 8), recorded in the pull request |
| Docs, settings help text | row 7 |
| Rollout | row 1 |

## 2026-10-10 — #232: the activity is the since column's words, one AI sparkle by the line's label

A working row said what it did twice: a third line under the model (`.ov-actline`, the `.ov-act` pill, #226) and `working 9m` on the right, beside a lamp that already flashes. The AI mark was in two places: the task line's `AI` badge after its text, the now line's `AI · 2m ago` under the since time (#198). Amends #226 row 1 (the activity line) and row 3 (the AI mark under the time).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The activity | **Plain words in the since column, in place of `working`**: `Investigating · grep -n … · 9m`, the since text's weight and mono, no border, pill or badge. Only while the row's display is `working` and the activity is usable; working with none is `working 9m` as before; every other state (needs you, waiting, idle, done, asleep) keeps its own word and time, never an activity it may still carry (an idle worker's background `Waiting`). The Worker column is the mark, the name, the chips and the model: no third line. The row carries the verb and the detail apart (`OverviewWorkerRow.act`) and joined (`activity`, for the spoken label and `No summary yet`). The wall still never shows it. |
| 2 | Width and cut | The last column is **`clamp(256px, 23.5vw, 320px)`** (was 132px; 301 px at 1280, 320 at 1440): wide enough for the longest verb and time (`Looking at an image · 12h 04m`) and a useful part of the detail (13–25 characters of the fixtures' at 1280, Quiet and Ubuntu). **Only the detail is cut** (the one shrinking item, ellipsis); the verb, the separator and the time never shrink, and none of them sits in a box that cuts with an ellipsis (round 1's single cut run clipped `Looking at an image` by 20 px and gave the verb a false `Testi…` when two items shared the shrink). When the room after the verb is under 10 characters the detail wraps to a second line of its box, out of sight (`flex-wrap`, one line high, overflow hidden): `Looking at an image · 1h 04m`, never a hanging `· …`. The time stays at the column's right edge; the whole activity is the box's `title` and in the row's label. The detail counts for nothing in the column's least size (`width: 0`, grown to its own width), so the column's least size is the verb and the time. ≤760 px a working row's column is 46vw (at least its verb and time): the detail shows when it fits. ≤480 px the activity is its **verb over its time** beside the name and the model, the detail dropped (the title and the label keep it), the column at most 38vw but never less than the verb (`Investigating` at 390 px takes 116 px and the chip under the name moves down a line rather than cut it). |
| 3 | The time | **`r.since`** (the status's start), so it keeps counting through activity changes and summary writes, `Waiting` included. The 20 s tick rewrites only the time's own span, the now line's age and its sparkle's words, and the row's label; the row is not rebuilt. |
| 4 | The hint | `Open transcript ↵` goes **under the time** (absolute in the since cell, the line a model badge used to take): it hides nothing, moves nothing and grows no row; it steps aside while Retry is hovered or focused, and is not shown on a phone. |
| 5 | Headings | `Worker` · `Working on` · `Doing · for` (were `Worker · doing`, `Since`). |
| 6 | The AI mark | **One sparkle**, a filled four-point star (`ui/dom.ts` `SPARKLE_D`, `ICON.sparkle`, 24×24 in the icon convention, `currentColor`, 13 px; 14 in Ubuntu), **hugging the line's label** (`TASK ✦`, `NOW ✦`, 3 px; the label on the text's baseline and the sparkle centred on the text's capitals, not on the small label's box, which sat it 1.4 px low: `translate: 0 calc(50% - 0.5cap)` in the text's face and size), on the task and the now line alike; never after the text or in the since column. The text follows the label at the line's gap (7 px); no width is kept for a sparkle a line does not have (a fixed label slot left dead space, and the user said so). Chosen against a stroked star and a two-sparkle by a 1× side-by-side: the stroke blunts the points into an outline at 13 px and the second sparkle is a 1–2 px speck. Its wrapper is `role="img"` with `aiTitle()` as name and tooltip (`AI-generated summary of this worker's recent activity (its last 5 steps); generated 2m ago. …`; a task line: `AI-generated summary of what this worker was asked to do.`); the `<svg>` stays `aria-hidden`. Forced colours: CanvasText, asleep rows and Ubuntu too. |
| 7 | Freshness | **Quiet words after the line's text** (`.ov-ai-meta`, where #174's chip already was), **on the text's first baseline** like the label (round 1 put them on the last baseline of a wrapped now line with `align-self: last baseline`: alone in that group the box fell to the line's bottom edge, a pixel or two under the text, and the user saw it low): a model's now line `just now`, `2m ago` (`now_at`); a failed refresh keeps the age and adds #198's chip and Retry (`R` unchanged); a pending model line is the fallback with `AI pending` (or `AI waiting`) as quiet words, no outline and no sparkle (the line shown is not the model's); a task line and a now line with no time: the sparkle alone, no age made up. No `refreshing`: the digest has no such state. |
| 8 | The now line's room | It may take **two lines** (`-webkit-line-clamp: 2`, the whole text in its title) rather than lose its end. The wider since column (row 2) costs the h226 fixture's 100-rune now line its one line at **1280 and at 1440** alike (1280: 510 px of room beside the label, sparkle and age; 1440: 651 px against 648 px of text): two lines at both, never cut, the row 85 px (was 66 at 1440). Accepted over a narrower activity column, which left only `pn…` of a detail at 1440. #226's contract is now: never cut in width or height, at most two lines at 1280 and 1440, and a row whose now line takes one line is no taller than its name and model. The task line and the ticket stay one line with an ellipsis. |
| 9 | The wall | The outlined `AI · 2m ago` box is gone. The task and now lines are drawn **under their labels** (`TASK`, `NOW`: the board's mono, small, quiet, on the text's baseline), the same sparkle (`SPARKLE_D` as a `Path2D`) right after the label of a line the LLM wrote only, centred on the text's capitals, and that line's words (its age, a failed refresh) quiet after its text; a task and a now line sharing a wall line each get their own label (and sparkle), so a task the LLM did not write never wears the now line's sparkle and age. Short of room a line's words drop the failed refresh, then the age, down to the sparkle alone, while its text keeps 14u; then the text is cut. Two lines on one that do not fit: the now line keeps what it needs, the task a share (35%), and a task with too little room for its label and some text goes (what it does now beats what it was asked, as for the ticket's title). A line with no time keeps its failure words (only the age goes). The layout is a pure function (`segPlan`), vitest; the redraw key carries each line's words (`lineSegs`), so the age still redraws once a minute and never a second. |

## 2026-10-10 — #223 (part 1 of #11): the literal handoff and the filing cabinet

A worker's latest completed final reply filed as written, no model call and nothing sent to the worker, and the floor's
filing cabinet the later modes file into too. The JSONL rules come from the #223 research (Claude Code 2.1.267–2.1.296,
codex-cli 0.145.0–0.162.1; its redacted fixtures are `internal/transcript/testdata/*/handoff_*.jsonl`).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The worker's shortcut | **F** ("file"), the user's call over the ticket's H: H stays "Hire a worker at the first free desk". F at a worker opens the filing dialog for its latest completed reply; without one it says why (no completed reply, working, interrupted or a new prompt came, no cabinet, over 1 MiB) and offers nothing else: never a summary in its place. E at the floor's filing cabinet opens the cabinet (new scope `aim:cabinet`; T picks the cabinet too). |
| 2 | One parser | `transcript.HandoffTracker` takes the same `TurnEvents` as the follow-up and wrap-up trackers (txLines, one parse per line, every line with its byte span); `TurnEvent` gained the raw `stop_reason`, Claude's uuid, requestId and sessionId, the image blocks, Codex's message id and `phase`, the turn ids and `session_meta`. `FollowupTracker.Turn.ID` now includes the turn's place and ids (two identical replies are two turns; `followup_seen` still keys on the text's hash). |
| 3 | Claude's candidate | A message is all main-thread lines of one `message.id`, its text blocks joined in order with `"\n"` (`HandoffSeparator`, the follow-up join). It is published only once **closed** (the next line is not one of its own: R9b/D7), so the second text line of a two-line final never gives a half candidate. `end_turn` and `stop_sequence` (D4) are candidates; `refusal`, `max_tokens` (D1) and `tool_use` never; thinking-only and blank text never. **A null `stop_reason` is a candidate only when `system/turn_duration` confirms the turn ended** (deviation from D2's "never": 3 of the corpus's 4 null-stop finals had `turn_duration` after them, and this repo's follow-up fixtures are such two-line null-stop finals; without `turn_duration`, e.g. headless, null is never one). A new prompt invalidates the reply as the latest (R13). |
| 4 | Codex's candidate | `response_item` assistant messages only (R1); the turn by position, `task_started` → `task_complete` (R2; the stale 0.146 passthrough `turn_id` is never used); its last `final_answer`, in a phase-less legacy rollout its last assistant message, else `last_agent_message` (no transcript row, so no inline button; F still files it); a turn with a `final_answer` and a null `last_agent_message` keeps the final (D6); a `final_answer` with `questions` is a candidate (D5); parts joined with `"\n"` (D3, unseen). Aborted, unfinished, subagent rollouts and child turns: none (R4, R5). |
| 5 | Identity and the text | The candidate id hashes harness, session, Claude's message/request/line ids or Codex's turn and message ids, the source, the byte spans and the text's SHA-256 (the revision). The bridge shows only that (`Worker.handoff`); `transcript.Resolve` reads the spans again from the very file the tail read (`os.SameFile`, the size), re-parses those lines with `TurnEvents` and checks the hash: a replaced, truncated or changed source is `handoff_source`, a stale id `handoff_unavailable`. The body is that raw join: never `Turn.Text` (trimmed), DOM text or a clipped preview. |
| 6 | The preview | A command of its own, `worker.handoff_preview` (not in the ticket's list: the candidate carries no text, and the dialog must show the exact text): the first 64 KiB of the resolved text, the derived title, the provenance and whether a send home can be offered. |
| 7 | Filing | `internal/handoff`, the one writer (standard library and the store; `TestHandoffIsALeaf`). `.tmp` in the floor's directory, `fsync`, then **`link` to the first free name** and the `.tmp` removed: the "atomic rename" of the ticket without ever replacing a file (`rename` would overwrite a colliding name; `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` needs x/sys). Then the row and its FTS row in one transaction; a failed registration removes the file again, a crash between them leaves a complete file the startup reconcile registers from its front matter. The front matter is the bridge's (`floor`, `worker_id`, `cwd`, `source_turn`, `source_items`, `source_hash` and `op` added to the ticket's list, so an orphan can be registered, hired from and found again by a retry), each value a JSON-quoted string, the body after it unchanged. Containment is checked through every directory, not only by the path's text: a floor's or the trash's directory that is a symlink is refused (`O_NOFOLLOW` alone guards only the last component). Cap: 1 MiB of text, refused (`too_large`), never clipped. |
| 8 | Idempotency | `op` (one per dialog preview, `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, a unique column) answers the first handoff on a repeat; the same reply (candidate id and revision) of the same worker whose file still exists does too, so a lost ack or a second dialog never files twice. An `op` reused for another reply is `bad_request`. |
| 9 | Send home | Only after the handoff is filed and indexed, through `worker.fire`'s own path (`fireWorker`, `newFireOp` with the worktree kept, its own `worker.fire` audit row, `beginFire`), checked again then: not working, not blocked, no ask, `worker.fire`'s own refusals. The ack does not wait for the fire: `send_home: "started"` (the walk-out follows `worker.removed`) or `"skipped"` with why; the handoff stands either way. The lounge (#10) is not built: a worker not sent home walks back to its desk. |
| 10 | Search | FTS5 `handoff_fts(title, label, worker_name, branch, body)`, rowid = `handoff.id`, `porter unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`; bm25 weights title 10, label and worker 3, branch 2, body 1. A query is cut to 256 bytes, control characters made blanks, at most 8 whitespace terms, each quoted with `"` doubled, the last a prefix, all required; blank = the newest-first list. Snippets carry U+E000 / U+E001 and the game builds text nodes and `<mark>`s from them (`snippetRuns`); the preview is Markdown through the boards' DOMPurify allowlist. |
| 11 | Hire from handoff | The reviewed hire (#212) seeded by `fromHandoff`: the handoff's harness and model when the catalog has them (else empty and "Needs a new choice", its word kept), **no effort** (a handoff records none: the human picks it), its `cwd` as the folder, and the task `Read <path> and continue the work. Invoke the skills it suggests.`; origin "From the handoff". |
| 12 | The cabinet in the room | The prop's drawers show manila folder tabs, bottom drawer first, 8 a drawer (`drawerFill`); a filing plays the worker carrying a folder to the cabinet and back (the M6c delivery walk, key `<worker>@handoff:<id>`, `deskToCabinetRoute`); a worker sent home meanwhile walks out from where it stands, like a freelancer mid-delivery. The cabinet overlay is a screen dialog for the limits plate. |
| 13 | `worker.fire.handoff` | Still reserved and refused. When it comes it files through the same service and rules; until then a handoff with a send home is `worker.handoff` with `send_home: true`. |
| 14 | Latest at submit, not at the last read (review fix) | The hub's candidate trails the usage tracker's read (2 s). So the preview and the filing first read the transcript lines written after the tracker's last read (`Session.Main`'s delivered offset, kept with the candidate) through a `HandoffTracker` resumed at the candidate (`transcript.StillLatest`): every rule that ends a candidate in the tracker ends it there, and the reply must still be the latest (`ErrNotLatest` → `handoff_unavailable`). `Resolve` also refuses a transcript shorter than when the reply was seen (a truncation that drops `turn_duration` but keeps the reply's lines). The candidate is kept with the worker's agent session it was read under: a session change withdraws `Worker.handoff` in the same upsert and refuses a submit of the old one. |
| 15 | The completion lines and the observed session (final review fixes) | A file truncated and grown again past its old size is the same inode and no shorter, and the reply's own lines can survive it while the `turn_duration` (or closing line, or `task_complete`) that made it a candidate is gone. So the tracker hashes every line it reads (offset, bytes, newline) from where the message (Claude) or the turn (Codex: `task_started`, else after the last turn's end) began, and seals a candidate with that hash and the region's end when it becomes the candidate (`Candidate.Region`, `RegionFrom`, `Through`; not in the id). `StillLatest` and `Resolve` re-read the region with the tail's own line splitting and refuse a mismatch (`ErrSourceChanged` → `handoff_source`); `StillLatest` reads the later lines from the region's end, not from the tracker's last read, so a line between them rewritten as a prompt is `handoff_unavailable`. The region is bounded like the lines since (64 MiB, else `too_large`). The candidate is pinned to the agent session herdr reports (`usageWorker.agent`), not to the rollout `findCodex` found by a Codex agent's cwd: herdr reporting none keeps it offered, and herdr reporting that same session later offers it again under it (the offer is keyed by the reported session too); a candidate under another session is hidden and refused, never deleted by the view. |

## 2026-10-11 — #224 (part 2 of #11): Ask the agent, and an evidence-based cache estimate

The worker's own agent writes a handoff through its own `handoff` skill, filed by the bridge into #223's cabinet; the
dialog explains, as an estimate, whether the agent's prompt cache is likely warm. The research is
`docs/research/handoff-cache.md` (TTL policies and their sources).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Entry point | The ticket's "H opens the common filing dialog" is **F** (#223 decision 1: H stays hire). The F dialog gains a mode choice: **Save last message** (#223, still the default), **Ask the agent**, and **Use configured LLM** shown disabled (#112: "The bridge sends selected transcript context to the LLM provider and model set in Settings, not <worker>’s agent. Not available yet."). Each names what happens and who receives input. Nothing switches mode for you, whatever the cache estimate says; a worker with no literal candidate still opens on Save last message and says why. |
| 2 | Composer `/handoff` | Intercepted in `startPrompt`, before any translation and before the takeover hooks, by `TerminalOnly`'s first-token rule: the trimmed text starts with `/handoff` and then a blank or its end. Prose, quoted examples, `/handoff/x` and `/handoffs` stay prompts. It becomes the same request (`agentAsk`), its ack a `PromptResult` with `handoff`; a second `/handoff` while one runs answers it (`existing`), never a second prompt. The game keeps a composer `/handoff`'s op while its ack is unknown (`disconnected`, `timeout`, `offline`) and resends the same text to the same worker with it; an answer, a refusal, another text or another prompt drops it. Typed directly into the PTY it stays native. Only with a cabinet (`--handoff-dir`); without one a `/handoff` is an ordinary prompt. |
| 3 | Skill discovery, spelling | The worker's command catalog (`worker.commands`' own) must list a skill `handoff` that no built-in shadows; missing is `handoff_skill_missing` (a partial catalog says the skills could not be listed); nothing is sent. The spelling comes from the harness rules (`harness.SkillCall` through `PromptTranslator.SkillCall`: Claude `/handoff`, Codex `$handoff`); `internal/server` still imports no `harness` (`TestPromptRulesAreInTheHarnessTable`). No skill file is edited: the request text overrides the skill's temp-directory default. |
| 4 | The prompt | One prompt: the skill call and the instruction (blank: `summarize the conversation`; a custom one replaces it; ≤ 4 KiB), "write it to `<path>.tmp` first, then rename that file to `<path>`", the bridge's front matter values (`date` the request's time, floor, worker, branch, harness, model, session, `source: "agent"`), "one specific H1 title of at most 80 characters", and a last line `(H3D-HANDOFF <nonce>)`. It must fit `herdr.MaxMessage` and, for Claude, be typeable. Sent on the worker's input lane only while it is idle or done, checked again on the lane: never queued behind a running turn. |
| 5 | Tying the turn | `transcript.NewMarkerTracker`: the wrap-up's turn rules (offset, Prompted, TookOver, Ended, Void) with the bridge's prompt found by its marker (`Contains`, not the wrap-up's footer suffix). Checked on real transcripts: Claude Code 2.1.296 records `/handoff args` as `<command-message>`/`<command-name>`/`<command-args>` with the arguments verbatim (line ends kept) and the skill's text as an `isMeta` line; `asking()` reads the tags as typed. codex-cli 0.162.1 records `$handoff args` as plain text and then **the skill's text as a user message of its own** (`<skill>\n<name>…`): it is now injected context (`codexInjected`), not a person's prompt, which also fixes a `$skill` prompt read as a takeover for wrap-ups. |
| 6 | Reservation and adoption | The path is `<handoff_dir>/.pending/<floor>/<op>.md` (`handoff.Reserve`), a hidden directory the startup reconcile never registers from: an agent-written file is never filed without its `handoff_request` row. Leftovers of the op are removed at reservation; files of no pending request go to the trash at startup (`CleanPending`). `handoff.Adopt` requires the file complete (no `.tmp`, a regular file, `O_NOFOLLOW`, ≤ 1 MiB + front matter), drops the agent's front matter whatever it says, and files the text through `File` (the bridge's front matter with `source: agent` and `op`, the H1 title else #223's fallback, a free name in the floor's directory, the row and its index), then removes the reserved file. A failed request's file goes to `.trash/<floor>/agent-<op>.md`. The package stays a leaf; prompting, awaiting and file checks live in `internal/server`. |
| 7 | Completion | Both, in either order: the marked prompt's turn ended in the transcript, quiet and herdr `idle`/`done` with no question for the wrap-up debounce, **and** the file complete. A file first waits for the turn (a half-written file is never read); a turn first gives the file `AgentHandoffGrace` (5 s). Failures keep the worker, say why on `Worker.agent_handoff` and file nothing: no handoff within 10 minutes, `worker.handoff_cancel`, the transcript gone or the agent session changed, a person taking over (`worker.prompt`, `worker.keys`, `worker.answer`, `worker.answer_ask`, `worker.answer_followup`, or a prompt or interruption in the transcript after the bridge's), the turn ending without the file, or only its `.tmp`. Answering the worker's own permission question counts as taking over (the ticket's rule), so a Claude that asks before writing outside its checkout needs that permission granted up front. |
| 8 | Idempotency | `handoff_request (op PRIMARY KEY, …)`, a partial unique index of one `pending` per worker, the in-memory `byOp` map: a retry or reconnect with the op answers the request (`existing`) and never sends a second prompt; a settled op is answered from its row (kept 30 days). The handoff row's own `op` makes a second filing impossible. A retry after a failure is a new op (new nonce, new path). A wrap-up and a request never run on one worker together (`busy` either way). |
| 9 | Restart | A request whose prompt was sent resumes from its stored offset while its timeout runs (the transcript read again from there; the tracker void if those lines were already read); one never sent, or out of time, fails with "restarted" in its reason. |
| 10 | Send home | Only after filing and indexing, through #223's `handoffSendHome` (`worker.fire`'s path, worktree kept), its result on the request (`send_home_result`). A failed request never sends home. |
| 11 | Cache evidence | `Usage.cache` (`proto.UsageCache`, additive, compared by value in `sameUsage`): provider, model, the Codex auth route, the retention's source (`observed_1h` / `observed_5m` / `observed_mixed` / `documented` / `assumed` / `unknown`), its TTL range, the last relevant request's start **as an interval**, the expiry interval, the last cache-read tokens and the context tokens. The state is computed when shown (`usage.CacheState`, the game's `cacheState`): `warm` only before the earliest expiry, `expired` only after the latest, else `unknown`; never a promise. |
| 12 | Claude evidence | Main thread only (subagents, sidechain lines never). `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` and `_5m` decoded directly (the pricing's `write1h` inference is untouched). Per `requestId`: the start lies between the latest preceding prompt or tool result and its first record (never the last record; no fixed 60 s deduction; no trigger, no lower bound, never warm). A read-only request refreshes the timer and keeps the policy; zero 1h writes in it is no proof of 5m. A compaction forgets the evidence (`unknown`, "compacted") until the next request; so does a main request on another model ("model changed": another prefix), relevant or not, until a relevant request on the new model. Not done: the statusline's `prompt_cache` reaches only a statusline command's stdin, so the bridge cannot read it (an opt-in hook could tee it later); a `/model` switch with no request since is not detected. |
| 13 | Codex evidence | Model from `turn_context`, the turn's `task_started.started_at` / `task_complete.completed_at` as the request interval, `cached_input_tokens`, compaction resets, and a `turn_context` naming another model resets ("model changed"), as does a `session_meta` naming another provider ("provider changed"). OpenAI's policies only when `session_meta.model_provider` is `openai`; another provider or none named: `unknown`, no TTL. Policy by model and route: on the API, GPT-5.6 and later at least 30 minutes with no upper bound (never `expired`), earlier models 5–60 minutes (`documented`); signed in with ChatGPT the lifetime is not documented: `assumed`, no TTL, always `unknown`; no `auth.json`: the model's range, `assumed`. Only `auth.json`'s `auth_mode` is decoded. |
| 14 | Dialog copy | "Estimate · Likely warm / May have expired / Unknown"; not warm: "Agent cache may have expired. Generating a handoff can process the current conversation context again (about N tokens)." with N the context tokens, never the JSONL's size; the evidence line (retention and its source, the last request, the reused tokens, why unknown); "Save the last message instead". No dollar figure. |
| 15 | Live verification | Codex: `TestCodexShareBridgeAgentHandoff` (the real codex-cli 0.162.1 TUI, mock inference, herdr-world over its WebSocket, the fixture's own `handoff` skill in its CODEX_HOME): filed as an agent handoff, and a turn that writes nothing fails. It found decision 5's `<skill>` message; the mock now skips it like `<environment_context>`. That run lifted the fixture's version gate for 0.162.1 by hand: 0.162.1 is **not** added to `VerifiedCodex`, because the suite's `TestCodexShareBridgeRegression/10_sleep_and_wake` fails with it (the wake's resume `failed`), on this branch and on its base `82ae8b6` alike; every other suite passed. Claude: `TestIntegrationAgentHandoffClaudeSmoke` (opt-in, `H3D_HANDOFF_REAL=1`: one real `claude-haiku-5-5` turn in a throwaway herdr session through the user's own `handoff` skill): typed `/handoff …`, the skill wrote `<path>.tmp` and renamed it, filed with the bridge's front matter and the agent's H1. It found that a transcript first written after the bridge last looked for it (every 30 s) was "not readable": a request's transcript is now looked for every 2 s and a starting request waits for it about 6 s; an agent never prompted has none, and is refused with that reason. |

## 2026-10-12 — #248: Resolve conflicts with a worker, and task levels

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Task levels: where they live | **Superseded by #40 (rows 1–3 below):** the levels are the player's own list. Was: three fixed levels, small to large: **Very trivial**, **Trivial**, **Slightly complex** (ids `very_trivial`, `trivial`, `slightly_complex`; a rename only changes the label). Each is a harness + model + effort, unset until set in Settings → Task levels, stored **per browser** (`h3d.hire.taskLevels`, `game/src/ui/taskLevels.ts`, guarded like `hireModelPrefs.ts`: missing, throwing or malformed storage, or a malformed level, is unset). **Not on the bridge:** the hidden models a level depends on (#194) are per browser already, so a shared level could point at a model this browser hides. A level keeps its model while the bridge does not list it, so it works again when the model comes back. The hire is sent as before (`worker.hire` with `harness` / `model` / `effort`): the bridge never sees a level. |
| 2 | A level that cannot be used | **Fallback and the requested level superseded by #40 (row 3 below);** what makes a level unusable is unchanged. Its model hidden in Harness models (hidden wins), not in the bridge's catalog, or an effort the model does not take (`effortsFor`, `extra_efforts` included). It is shown off with the reason as its tooltip, never dropped. A request falls back nearest first, smaller before larger (`trivial` → `very_trivial` → `slightly_complex`); none usable: the usual pickers with a note and a way to Settings → Task levels. No level set: the usual pickers, no "Choose a more complex model…". |
| 3 | The hire dialog | `HirePrefill.level` opens it in task-level mode: one chip per set level instead of Harness / Model / Effort; "Choose a more complex model…" shows the usual pickers preset to the level (`resolveVisibleChoice`), "Back to task levels" returns. The switch lasts while the dialog is open and is never saved. A reviewer's `choice` never applies in that mode. A plain hire (H, Ask a worker…, Review with a worker, a dictated hire) has no `level` and is unchanged. |
| 4 | The resolve prompt, and who merges | `resolveConflictsPrompt(pr, method)` (`world/boards.ts`), one plain-text version for every harness: in a worktree of its own (`git worktree add --detach` when it has none; never switch the branch of a shared checkout), `gh pr checkout <n> --detach`, merge `origin/<base>`, resolve keeping both sides' intent, run the tests, `git push origin HEAD:<head_branch>`, wait for the checks, `gh pr merge <n> --squash|--merge|--rebase` (the panel's merge-method select, one to one). A conflict that is not mechanical, or failing tests or checks: stop and report. **The worker merges**, because that is the point of the button. Trade-off: that merge skips the panel's confirmation and its `pr.merge` audit-log row; revisit (the worker only resolves and pushes, the human merges from the panel) if the audit gap matters. |
| 5 | Worktree | A resolve hire sets `worktree: true` whenever `worktreeUsable(git)`, whatever Settings → Display "worktree by default" says (`HirePrefill.worktree`): checking the PR out in the floor's shared folder would switch the branch under every worker there. No worktree possible (not a repo, a failed fetch): the hire goes without one and the prompt's own-worktree step covers it. |
| 6 | The PR footer | Three groups that wrap whole: workers (left), merge (middle: the method select with Merge… / Merge anyway…), Open in the provider (right). One primary: **Resolve conflicts with a worker** on a `conflicting` PR (never `mergeable` or `unknown`), else **Review with a worker**, which stays as a quiet button beside Resolve. Resolve follows `whyNoHire` like Review. The select stays on a conflicting PR (it sets the prompt's method); Merge… stays off with its reason. The **Comment** button goes: the comment box is right below it. |

## 2026-10-12 — #247: `@r:` repositories on other floors

In a transcript, `@r:` (or `@repo:`) lists the repositories and folders configured on the other floors of the worker's
building and puts in something the worker can use at once. Decisions 1, 2, 4, 5 and 8 of the issue:

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | A new trigger? | No: **a query prefix inside the `@` source**. The popup has one source per trigger (`completion.ts`), so the transcript's `@` source is a composite (`laptop/atSource.ts`): a query starting `r:` or `repo:` goes to the repo source with the prefix taken off, anything else to the file search as before. `complete.ts` and `completion.ts` are unchanged. `@r`, `@repo` (no colon), `@rx:` and `@./r:x` stay file searches; a file whose path starts with `r:` is found as `@./r:…`. The label follows the query ("Repositories on other floors" / "Files and folders"), and a row's key names its side, so a switch never keeps a stale active row. The file popup's footer hints at `r:` when it has nothing else to say |
| 2 | What is "configured"? | **Stored settings only**: `settings.repo` and `settings.default_cwd`. The bridge's `suggested` values (what it guessed from the floor's workers' checkouts) are ignored, though hiring falls back to them: the list shows what someone chose to configure. A floor with neither is not listed; a folder-only floor whose folder is missing is hidden |
| 4 | Who scopes and checks? | **The bridge**, in a new read-only `worker.repos {id}`: it leaves out the worker's own floor, keeps its building's floors by index (and, with the switch, the other buildings' by `sort` and then the unassigned lot), and says per row whether the folder is a checkout of the repo (`StatDir`, then the floor suggestion's origin lookup, `RepoOf`, equal ignoring case and `.git`). The checks run off the hub goroutine, each lookup bounded (3 s) and cached (30 s), failing open: a failed lookup is "not cloned", never an error of the list. The client ranks (a new opt-in subsequence tier in `rankByKeys`: the repo name, `owner/repo`, the folder's basename, the folder, the floor) and draws.
