A worker hired but whose prompt was not sent (herdr refused it, Codex's hooks review) → **`failed`**, naming the worker, who stays: the task did not reach anyone, so it needs attention; a retry hires a new worker. `failed` and `skipped` stay in view until dismissed. |
| 9 | Never destructive | No schedule path sends a worker home or deletes a worktree, branch or workspace; every fire test checks herdr's and git's calls and the audit log for it. The one rollback a fire can cause is `worker.hire`'s own: a hire that fails after herdr made its workspace closes that fresh workspace (a fresh worktree only if clean and its branch still at its base), as any failed hire does. |
| 10 | Store and view | SQLite `schedule`, one row per schedule, every transition written first (`updated_at`). The view (snapshot `schedules`, `schedule.upsert` / `schedule.removed`) is the upcoming, the failed and skipped not dismissed, and each floor's last 50 `done`; older `done` rows are deleted. Cancelled and dismissed rows are kept, out of view; a command on one is `invalid_state`. A schedule's floor and source cannot change; `update` replaces the rest of its spec. |

## 2026-10-16 — #74: the server rack and the host's stats

A short server rack on every floor, between the lounge window and the whiteboard, with a monitor on it that shows the
bridge host's CPU, memory and (on Arch) pending updates, and LEDs that follow its disks. Protocol: "Host stats".
Code: `internal/hoststats`, `internal/server/hoststats.go`, `game/src/render/rack.ts`, `game/src/rack/link.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sampling only while looked at | **One `hoststats.Sampler` per hub**, started by the first connection that sends `host.watch` and stopped by the last `host.unwatch` or disconnect. Between those it has no goroutine, no timer and no open file; `Stop` waits for the goroutine and a frame handed to the hub gives up with its run's context, so stopping never waits on the hub. A frame of an earlier run is dropped by a generation number. The limits poller (every client, broadcast to all) was not the model; `board.watch`'s per-connection counting was. |
| 2 | Read `/proc`, no gopsutil | A sample is ~30 µs; the cost is in watching, not in reading. gopsutil computes the same numbers (`Used = Total − Available`, guest time left out), keeps one program-wide CPU baseline the sampler would have to bypass, is cross-platform for a Linux-only bridge and adds 1 direct and 8 indirect modules. Half the parsing was in `server/load.go`, which now reads through `hoststats` (hire capacity unchanged; `hoststats` imports nothing of the module but `execpath`). Files are read into a reused buffer. |
| 3 | 5 s and 1 s | `host.stats` every 5 s (first one after 1 s from a baseline taken at the start); `host.disk` every 1 s as a separate small frame, because a 5 s average would smear a burst and the LEDs would feel late. The stats ticker starts with the sampler, so the second frame comes 4 s after the first. Nothing is queued per watcher: a slow client is dropped as for every other frame. |
| 4 | Updates | `checkupdates` only: it syncs a throwaway database, needs no root and cannot cause a partial upgrade; the bridge never runs `pacman -Sy`, anything as root or an upgrade. Supported = Arch (`ID` or `ID_LIKE`) **and** both `checkupdates` and `fakeroot` resolve (`execpath.Resolve`, and fakeroot's directory goes on `checkupdates`' `PATH`); asked again at every start. `CHECKUPDATES_DB` is a `checkupdates/` directory next to `world.db` (the script links `local` and creates `sync/` inside it and removes `db.lck` on exit, so not the state directory itself). **At most one attempt every 12 h, successful or not:** a failure keeps the last good count and its time and waits the full 12 h rather than hit the mirrors again from a bridge that is offline. The due check is noticed on the 5 s frame (an in-memory comparison) and never runs more than that. A run cancelled with the last watcher still counts as an attempt (review round 2: restoring the old time let watch, unwatch, watch cycles start a mirror sync each), and leaves the last good count alone. It is stopped with SIGTERM to its process group (checkupdates' trap removes `db.lck`), SIGKILL after 3 s; and since a killed run can still leave `db.lck` in the bridge's own `checkupdates/` directory (which would fail the next check for another 12 h), the bridge removes that one file once the group is reaped, never in the shared default directory. |
| 5 | Not audited | `host.watch` / `host.unwatch` are not written to the audit log (every other command is): a game sends them as the player turns, and they read only the host's own counters. |
| 6 | Disk LEDs | Physical disks only (`/sys/block/<dev>/device`); disk *i* (by name) drives server *i*, and the servers past the last disk mirror the *totals*, each with its own phase. A level is `log10(1 + IOPS) / log10(5001)`: 0 is dark, 1 IOPS a blip (0.08), 100 a flicker (0.54), 5000 near solid. The blink is in the shader (a time uniform, a kind, level, phase and jitter per instance), so the **calm fallback blink is the shader's too** (kind 2, a slow 0.25–0.55 Hz): the ticket said a colour write at a few Hz as `DeskSet.update` does; writing nothing per frame is cheaper than that and looks the same. The rates are written once per `host.disk` frame, only when a level changed. |
| 7 | Looked at | **The rack's whole box in the camera's frustum**, at least 3 % of the view's height (`RACK_VISIBLE`: its LEDs can be seen from across the room, the monitor need not be readable), on the floor you stand on (not its fire escape, the lobby, the street or the plan view), the tab visible, the bridge connected. `host.watch` after 300 ms of it, `host.unwatch` 10 s after it stops (a glance does not flap it); a hidden tab, another floor or leaving the floor unwatch at once; a dropped connection resets without a command and a live one watches again. The frustum test is `PropViewer`, which the #118 wall screen now shares. |
| 8 | The screen | One canvas texture, drawn only when the mode or the numbers change (so once per `host.stats`, and the clock is the sample's `HH:MM:SS`, static). Bars: green, amber above 75 %, red above 90 %. Not watched: the last frame, dimmed; a frame older than 30 s when a watch begins is grey ("stale") until the next arrives; no frame yet, "Reading the host" or "Standing by"; a bridge that answered `unknown_command` to `host.watch`: "No host stats on this bridge" (the command goes into `unsupported`, never asked again, no toast). The updates line is absent when `updates` is. |
| 9 | Cost | Three draws a floor: the merged body (frame, servers, vents, monitor, keyboard: vertex colours), one `InstancedMesh` of 20 LEDs and the screen; the e2e bench counts +3 draws against the rack hidden. No canvas, buffer or per-LED work per frame (the e2e checks draws and writes stay within the frames received). |

## 2026-10-16 — #63 (part 2 of #61): a schedule holds its worktree

A schedule stores its `cwd`. When that is a linked worktree, sending its worker home with "delete worktree" or a
wrap-up armed with a delete would remove the checkout, and the schedule would fail hours later. Now a schedule that may
still fire holds the worktree, and nothing in the bridge deletes it (protocol: "Scheduled agents", "Worktree hold").
Code: `internal/server/schedulehold.go`, the checks in `startFirePrepare`, `newFireOp`, `fireCheck`, `runFire`,
`startWrapUp` and `wrapUpClean`, and the removal's reservation (`reserveRemoval`; `removingRefusal` in
`scheduleCommand`).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What holds | A schedule in `scheduled` or `waiting` whose `cwd` is the worktree's top or under it, **whatever its `worktree` flag**: a worktree hire still finds its repository from `cwd`. `running`, `done`, `failed`, `skipped`, `cancelled` and dismissed ones hold nothing. |
| 2 | Comparing paths | Both sides cleaned and, where they exist, symlinks resolved (`filepath.EvalSymlinks`, as repository discovery does); then equal, or a prefix ending on a separator (`/x/wt` holds `/x/wt/sub`, not `/x/wt2`). The worktree's side is the checkout (`worktree.checkout_path`, else the worker's cwd) when the command arrives, and git's top level (`readTree`) wherever git is read. |
| 3 | No cached hold | The hold is derived from the schedules each time it is asked (`Hub.scheduleHolds`, on the hub goroutine, which owns them; a copy is then matched off it, so path resolution never runs under the hub while git does). Cancel, fire and dismiss release it with nothing to clear. |
| 4 | The reason | `used by scheduled agent '<title>' at <due>`; several holders: the earliest due is named, then ` and <n> more`. One string for `worker.fire.prepare`'s `reason` and the `invalid_state` messages (`invalid state: <reason>`). It comes before a dirty or unpushed reason: commit and push would not make the delete possible. |
| 5 | "Local" on the bridge | The zone of the bridge's clock (`Options.Now`; `time.Local`, from `TZ` or the host, in production), `2006-01-02 15:04 MST`. A schedule's own reasons and prompts stay UTC (#62): they go to an agent and must not depend on the host. The hold's reason is for the person at the send-home dialog, on the same host. Tests inject a clock in a fixed zone. |
| 6 | When it is checked | At command time, not only in the dialog: `newFireOp` (worker.fire, worker.wrapup's arm) refuses a delete against the checkout, and a CLEAN looks before it and goes home with `keep`; `fireCheck` (before anything is touched, and again after the agent exited) matches git's top, so a worker whose cwd is under its worktree is held by a schedule at the top. |
| 7 | A wrap-up armed before the schedule | Its CLEAN **falls back to keep**: the worker goes home, the workspace closes, worktree and branch stay; the note `<reason>: the worktree is kept` is in the `worker.fire` audit row (`wrapup.reason`, and a step). Not `needs_you`: the agent reported clean, and keep is what the person would choose now. Never a delete. |
| 8 | A hold during the agent's exit | `worker.fire` keeps the worktree with a `note`, as it does for a tree that changed then; a wrap-up's send home does the same rather than its strict failure, since keeping is safe. |
| 9 | Outside the bridge | `git worktree remove` or `rm` are not prevented; the schedule fails when it fires (#62: `working directory <cwd> no longer exists`). |
| 10 | The game | The send-home dialog already showed `reason` ("Deleting is off: <reason>. …") and offers only keep. It recognises a hold by the reason's prefix (`scheduleHold`, `world/hire.ts`; the prefix is part of the protocol): the callout then says the worktree stays until that agent starts or its schedule is cancelled, and "commit and push first" is said only when something is left on the desk (dirty or unpushed). A `worker.fire` / `worker.wrapup` refused `invalid_state` for a hold (a schedule made after the dialog looked) shows the bridge's reason, not the asleep-floor text the other `invalid_state`s of `worker.fire` get, and the dialog looks again and falls back to keep, as after `not_clean`. |
| 11 | No schedule between the last check and the removal (review R1) | A hold read on the hub and then matched while git runs leaves a gap: a `schedule.create` acknowledged during the last git checks would still see its worktree removed. So the last check runs **on the hub, in the step that reserves the worktree** (`Hub.reserveRemoval`, right before `herdr worktree remove`): a schedule acknowledged before that step is seen and the worktree kept (`worker.fire`: a `note`, as #8; a wrap-up's CLEAN: keep, as #7). Until the removal ends, removed or failed (`releaseRemoval`, before the result goes out), `schedule.create`, `schedule.update` and `schedule.retry` (a retry that waits holds again) whose `cwd` is in that top answer **`busy`**, not `invalid_state`: nothing is wrong with the schedule or its state, the conflict passes within seconds, and once the worktree is gone the `cwd` check says why. `run_now` and `cancel` act on a schedule that already holds or releases. The reservation is in memory only: a restart ends every send home in flight (none resumes), and an armed wrap-up that resumes after one checks again at its CLEAN. |

## 2026-10-16 — #31: copy the full path of a transcript file reference

An agent writes `[handoff document](/tmp/handoff.md)`: the transcript showed the label and dropped the path (only
`http(s):` / `mailto:` links keep an `href`), so it could not be copied. Now a file reference keeps the label and
highlighting, and activating it opens a popover with the full path and **Copy file path**. Code:
`game/src/laptop/fileRef.ts` (which destinations, the path, the checkout join), `fileRefPopover.ts` (the popover),
the md purifier's hooks and `setMarkdown` in `transcriptRender.ts`, `.tx-file-ref` / `.tx-fref*` in `transcript.css`;
tests `fileRef.test.ts`, `fileRefPopover.test.ts`, `transcriptRender.test.ts` ("#31 file references"), `e2e/h31-copy-file-path.spec.ts`; the
popover is the `file-path` surface of `e2e/ui-surfaces.ts` (#216's theme audits and screenshots).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Not a wider allowlist | The URI allowlist is unchanged: a file path as an `href` would navigate and resolve against the page's origin. The sanitiser's `uponSanitizeAttribute` hook takes the `href` of a file reference off the anchor and keeps its path as `data-tx-file` (set in `afterSanitizeAttributes`, after DOMPurify's own checks; raw HTML is text, so markdown cannot plant it). Trusted code (`markFileRefs`) makes the anchor a button. |
| 2 | What is a file reference | An absolute path (`/x`), a home one (`~/x`), a Windows drive path (also as markdown hands it over, its backslashes encoded: `C:%5Cx`; only a letter, a colon and a slash pass as a drive), a relative one (`./x`, `../x`, `docs/x.md`, `README.md`), a `file:` URL with no host or `localhost`. Not: any other scheme (`javascript:`, `data:`, `vbscript:`, `blob:`…), a network path (`//host`, `\\host`, `file://host/…`), `#anchor`, `?query`, a relative path whose first segment has a colon (a scheme to a browser, also after decoding), a control character, over 4096 characters. A markdown image of a local file (shown as a link, never an `<img>`) is one too. |
| 3 | Faithful text | A destination is a URL to markdown: what the parser encoded (spaces in `<…>`, Unicode, `"`) is decoded back, and `%20` an agent wrote is a space too. Escapes (`\_`) are the parser's. A malformed `%` stays as written and nothing throws. A `#fragment` (`src/app.ts#L42`) is not part of the path; `%23` is a literal `#`. |
| 4 | Relative paths | Joined to the worker's checkout (`Worker.cwd`, read from the store when the popover opens; `.` and `..` resolved, never above the root) and copied in full; the popover says what was written. Checkout unknown (or not an absolute path): the path is shown and copied as written, and it says it is relative to the checkout. Never resolved against `location`; `~` is not expanded. |
| 5 | The interaction | The reference is `role="button"`, in the tab order, `aria-haspopup="dialog"` / `aria-expanded`, its path as its `title`. A click (not a drag that selects its text), Enter or Space opens a non-modal `role="dialog"` with the keyboard on Copy; Tab goes between the path and Copy; Esc closes it and gives the keyboard back to the reference (it never reaches the seat, which would stand you up); a click outside closes it. Its keys are its own (no timeline scroll, no question digits) but the text zoom. |
| 6 | Copy | `TranscriptRenderHost.copy` (none: `navigator.clipboard.writeText`). Copied: the button says "Copied" for 2 s and a polite live region says so. Refused: a visible message names the problem and the recovery ("press Ctrl+C", ⌘C on a Mac) with the path selected and focused, so the copy key does it. The path is `user-select: all`. |
| 7 | Placement | The popover is in the transcript's root, not the scroller, so it is never clipped; below the reference (above when there is more room), kept inside the root and the window, scaled with it. Copy never scrolls away (review U2: a short seated screen opened it with Copy below the popover's fold, focused but unseen): only the body, the path and its note, shrinks and scrolls, and the popover is never shorter than Copy and two lines of the path (all of it when shorter). When neither side of the reference has that much room it takes the roomier side and moves over the reference, as little as it must, inside the root (`placeY`, `fileRefPopover.test.ts`). It follows the reference as the timeline scrolls and through a row drawn anew (the same reference in the same row), and closes when the reference scrolls out of view or goes. App chrome: the UI theme's raised surface; the path in the transcript's mono. |
| 8 | Out of scope | The boards' Markdown (`ui/boards/markdown.ts`: boards, the cabinet, the follow-up card) keeps its own rules. |

## 2026-10-16 — #81: "Open in GitHub" on a Linux helper

`POST /api/open` answered 502 `opening a browser needs macOS` on every OS but darwin. Now `SystemOpen`
(`internal/helper/open.go`) runs `open <url>` on macOS and `xdg-open <url>` on Linux, and names the OS elsewhere.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Is a command that is still running a failure? | **No.** `xdg-open` stays in the foreground as long as the `.desktop` app it started lives (browser not running). The opener waits a 3 s grace window: exit 0 → opened; non-zero exit or no such executable → error with the exit code and the first 512 bytes of output (`502`); still running → opened, **not killed**, reaped by a background goroutine (no zombie), its late exit unseen. The old 15 s `CommandContext(...).CombinedOutput()` would have killed a good launch and reported it failed. |
| 2 | Pipes | stdin none. stdout and stderr are **one pipe the helper owns** (`os.Pipe`, the write end handed to the command as an `*os.File`, so Go starts no copy goroutines and `cmd.Wait` returns at the command's exit, never later because a descendant holds the pipe; `Cmd.WaitDelay` is not used). The helper closes its write end right after `Start`; one reader goroutine keeps the first 4 KiB, discards the rest until EOF (every writer gone) and then closes the read end, never earlier, so a browser the opener started that logs to the inherited stdout/stderr later never gets EPIPE/SIGPIPE. After a non-zero exit the answer waits at most 150 ms for that reader to reach EOF (so output already written is in the diagnostics), no longer, so a descendant cannot delay it. Round 1 used `WaitDelay` and `ErrWaitDelay`: a late non-zero exit near the end of the window, with a descendant on the pipe, came back after the timer and was reported as success (reviewer repro `sleep 4 & sleep 2.75; echo failure >&2; exit 4`), and the pipe was closed under later writers. A timer that fires at the same moment as an exit still reports the exit. |
| 3 | The request's context | Ending (the game went away) ends the wait with that error and **leaves a launch in flight alone**: killing it would only make a half-opened browser. A context already done starts nothing. |
| 4 | macOS | Shares the runner: `open` returns at once, so success is `204` and failure `502` with diagnostics, as before; only the 15 s kill is gone. |
| 5 | Environment | Inherited whole (`Cmd.Env` unset); no display, runtime dir or browser path is set. The user-service requirements are in CONTRIBUTING.md, "Linux user service". |
| 6 | Tests | `newSystemOpener(goos, command, grace)` takes the OS, the command constructor and the window, so every OS is driven on any host; most tests run real fake `xdg-open` scripts on `PATH`, never a browser. |

## 2026-10-16 — #11: notify core and Telegram one-way alerts

The bridge sends a Telegram message to your phone when a worker needs you or finishes (one way: nothing it
receives does anything but pair). `internal/notify` holds what is provider-neutral: the inbox rule ported from the
game, the episodes and the done settle, the settings with their test gate and environment override, the
never-blocking sightings and the bounded sender with its backoff, and the `Destination` / `Channel` interfaces.
`internal/notify/telegram` is the Bot API behind them; `internal/app` wires it in. Code: `internal/notify/{rule,tracker,
service,notify,clock}.go`, `internal/notify/telegram/{api,channel,file}.go`, `internal/server/notify.go`,
`internal/store/notify.go`, `internal/proto/notify.go`; the game's `world/notifySettings.ts`, `ui/telegramSection.ts`,
`tools/fixture-telegram.ts`. Tests: `go test ./internal/notify/...` (an `httptest` Bot API, a fake clock: no real
sleeps, never api.telegram.org), `go test ./internal/server -run 'Notify'`, `game/src/world/notifyVectors.test.ts`,
`game/e2e/h11-telegram.spec.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Layering | The core imports nothing of the module and no `net/http`; `server`, `store` and `proto` never import `internal/notify/telegram`; `app` does (`TestNotifyCoreIsProviderNeutral`). The core reaches Telegram only through `Channel` (a `Destination` with the token, the pairing and a fingerprint). |
| 2 | Keeping the game's inbox rule and the bridge's in step | **Shared fixture vectors.** The rule stays in the game; Go ports it (`notify.Judge`) and `internal/notify/testdata/inbox_vectors.json` pins both: `go test ./internal/server -run TestNotifyInboxVectors -update-notify-vectors` writes each case as the protocol carries it (`proto.Worker` → JSON → back → `notifyFacts` → `Judge`), and the vitest feeds the same workers through the real `WorldStore.workerView`, `inboxReason` and `inboxQueue`. CI runs vitest when that file changes. |
| 3 | The rule | Needs you: `(crew.needsYou ∨ ask ∨ follow-up ∨ wrap-up stuck) ∧ ¬asleep`; else waiting (idle/done with a `waiting` activity) is no candidate; else `displayStatus(effectiveStatus)` (blocked → needs you, done → done; a shared Codex worker blocked only by an async question works). `since` = ask.since ?? follow-up.at ?? status_since; the reason is `inboxReason`'s. Candidates: needs you, any role; done unless a freelancer with “freelancers” off. The inbox's read/dismissed state is the browser's: the bridge ignores it. Asleep floors never notify. |
| 4 | Trigger | `Hub.emit` hands the service a look (`notify.Observation`, from the hub's current `proto.Worker`) for every `worker.upsert`, `worker.status`, `worker.activity` and `floor.upsert` (each of the floor's workers), and `worker.removed`. `Observe` appends to a bounded list under a mutex (past 4096 waiting: dropped and counted); the service's loop and sender are goroutines of their own. The emit path never waits on the network (`TestNotifyEmitNeverBlocks`, `TestObserveNeverBlocks`). |
| 5 | Episodes and settle | An episode is (worker, needs/done, since). Needs you notifies at once, once per episode; done after 5 s if it is still the same episode (cancelled when it leaves, settles again when it comes back). A worker's first sighting is known silently (the bridge's start, a herdr session seen again: no burst of old alerts); an episode that may not notify when it starts (floor asleep, the feature or its event off, the channel not ready, a freelancer's done not listed) is known silently too, so switching on later, and a floor waking, send nothing old. The clock is injected (`notify.FakeClock`). |
| 6 | Text | Plain text, no `parse_mode`, `link_preview_options.is_disabled`: `<name> · <reason>` and one line, the overview's now line (else its task line), else the transcript's last assistant text line, else none; `digest.Scrub`bed, one line, 200 runes; the channel removes its token from it too. A worker whose ask has a secret question gets no line at all. |
| 7 | Token | `telegram.json` (0600, atomic, next to the database; `--telegram-file`, `HERDR_WORLD_TELEGRAM_FILE`) holds the token, the bot's id and username and the paired person. `HERDR_WORLD_TELEGRAM_KEY_FILE` (`--telegram-key-file`) gives the token from a file instead: then the panel cannot change it (`env_locked`) and the file keeps no token. Set from the panel it is checked with `getMe` first; another bot's token unpairs. Views carry `key_set` and the last four characters only. Go's `*url.Error` prints the request URL (`/bot<token>/…`): its URL is rewritten and every message scrubbed of the token and of its secret part (`TestTokenNeverLeaks`). |
| 8 | Pairing | Pair shows a one-time code (8 of an alphabet without 0/O/1/I/L, `ABCD-EFGH`, 10 minutes) and starts a `getUpdates` long poll (25 s, the HTTP bound 15 s longer) **only while it waits**. The first poll asks for the newest update only (`offset=-1`: the backlog is forgotten), every later one confirms with `update_id+1`. The first private-chat message whose text is the code (spaces and dashes ignored, any case) pairs its `from.id` (int64; not the chat id), which every message then goes to as `chat_id`; that person gets one greeting. Every other update, from anyone, is dropped without a reply. Polling stops when paired, cancelled or expired. A 409 (another poller or a webhook) shows `conflict` and backs off; no inbound port, no webhook. |
| 9 | Settings | One `notify_settings` row: enabled, events `needs_you` / `done`, quiet (`disable_notification`), freelancers (default off, the inbox's default). `HERDR_WORLD_NOTIFY_ENABLED` (`--notify-enabled`) wins and is `env` in the view (`env_locked` on change; it needs no test, the channel must still be ready). Switching on needs a token (`not_configured`), a paired person (`not_paired`) and a delivered `notify.test` of that token and person within 30 minutes (`test_required`). |
| 10 | Disabled | Nothing leaves the machine: no send, no poll. Only Test, Pair and a token's check talk to Telegram, each a user's action. |
| 11 | Audit | Bridge rows `notify.send` (worker id, kind, attempts, HTTP status, error code) and `notify.pair` (outcome, ignored updates, failed polls, the last status): never a text, a code, a token or a Telegram id. `notify.token.set` is audited as `{"key_given":true}`; the game's `loggedArgs` redacts its `key`. |
| 12 | Backoff | A queue of 32 alerts (a full one drops and audits `dropped`); at most 4 attempts; a 429 waits `retry_after` (more than 2 minutes: given up), a 5xx or a network failure 1, 2, 4 s (capped at 30 s); 401/404 is `unauthorized`, 403 and 400 “chat not found” `blocked`, which the view's state shows (`blocked`) until a delivery works. A Telegram outage never stalls the hub. |
| 13 | Protocol and game | `World.notify`, `notify.update` (the state flat), `notify.get` / `set` / `test` / `token.set` / `token.clear` / `pair.start` / `pair.cancel` / `unpair`. Settings → Telegram (Integrations, kept on the bridge): the token, the account (the code large, the bot's chat linked), the test, then the switch and its options, as three ordered steps since each needs the one before. The browser-kept “Notifications” section (desktop notifications, the chime) is unchanged. |

## 2026-10-16 — #12: the Telegram section's destination status and keyboard

#11 built Settings → Telegram (Integrations): it is #12's "Notifications" section, so nothing moved and the protocol
did not change. #12 adds one destination status at its top and keeps the keyboard in the section through every
action. Code: `game/src/ui/telegramSection.ts` (`telegramDestination`, `land`), `.settings-tg-dest*` in
`settings.css`; tests `telegramSection.dom.test.ts`, `e2e/h11-telegram.spec.ts` (the acceptance journey and a
keyboard-only one), `TestNotifyTokenSetAuditUnparsableArgs`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The status | From the `notify` state alone, first match: no token → **Not configured** (a refused token is never saved, so a failed check lands here; the command's error says why); `last_error` → **Error**, Telegram's reason (the bridge keeps it until a message gets through or the token or account changes, whatever the switch; `blocked` is one); a pairing retrying (`conflict`, `error`) → **Error**; no account (a code waiting or not) → **Not configured**; else **Paired**, alerts on or off. The separate "Last message not delivered" line is gone: the status says it. |
| 2 | Screen readers | One live region, the section's status line. A status change outside an action (the phone sent the code, a message bounced) is said there. While an action is on its way the status shows changes but says none; when it ends, its line is followed, in the same message, by the destination whenever that changed since it was last said ("Unpaired. … Not configured. …"), unless the line already holds it. Which change was the action's is not guessed: an event-only save during which another window switched alerts off, or a worker's message bouncing between a test's success and its ack, looked like the action's own and went unsaid (review R1). A repeated phrase after the user's own action is the cost, chosen over a missed change. |
| 3 | Focus | Controls stay natively disabled while a command is on its way; after it the keyboard lands on: token saved → Pair (Enter or the button); Pair → Cancel pairing, then the paired phone → Send test message; Cancel pairing / Unpair → Pair; Remove token → the token field; Test and the switches → themselves; a failure → the control used. Never pulled from where the user went meanwhile. |
| 4 | The sim | `tools/fixture-telegram.ts` forgets the last error on pair, cancel and unpair, like the bridge's `Service.after`. |
| 5 | Out of scope | `digestSection.ts` loses the focus the same way (a control disabled while busy); not changed here. |

## 2026-10-16 — #13: reply from Telegram into the worker transcript

A reply (Telegram's reply-to) to a notification types its text into the worker the notification was about, as the
game's `worker.prompt` would. Code: `internal/notify/{notify,reply,service,fakes}.go`,
`internal/notify/telegram/{api,channel}.go`, `internal/store/notify.go`, `internal/server/{input,notify}.go`,
`internal/proto/notify.go`; the game's `ui/telegramSection.ts`, `world/notifySettings.ts`, `tools/fixture-telegram.ts`.
Tests: `go test ./internal/notify/...` (`reply_test.go`: the round trip through `FakeChannel` with no HTTP; the
telegram package's `reply_test.go` against the `httptest` Bot API), `go test ./internal/server -run 'Reply'` (the fake
herdr control), `go test ./internal/store -run NotifyMessages`, `telegramSection.dom.test.ts`, `e2e/h11-telegram.spec.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Layering | The core stays provider-neutral (`TestNotifyCoreIsProviderNeutral`). `Notification` names its worker (never in its text); `Destination.Send` names the message it became (`notify.Ref`: chat and message id; a zero message id is none and never stored); `Channel` gains `Listen`, `Hold` (decision 8), `Confirm`, `Answer` and hands the paired person's messages to `Hooks.Reply`. The service checks and routes; the hub types (`Options.Prompt`). `FakeChannel.Deliver` and `FakeStore` test the whole core without HTTP. |
| 2 | One reader | Two `getUpdates` consumers of one token get Telegram's 409. The pairing's poll became the one loop (`telegram.reader`): it polls while a pairing waits **or** replies are on for a paired account, one call at a time, and gives each update to the pairing (its code) or to the replies. Any change of what it polls for (a pairing starts or ends, replies on/off, unpair, a token) cancels the poll under way and it looks again. `allowed_updates: ["message"]`: edits are never read (superseded by #14 decision 11: `callback_query` too). Before it goes idle or stops, it confirms what it read (one poll from the offset), so a restart does not read it again. |
| 3 | Who counts | A reply is the paired account's own: `chat.type == "private"`, `from.id` the paired id, not `is_bot`, no `sender_chat`; anything else is dropped without a word (the bot never answers a stranger). A forward (`forward_origin`) or a message through another bot (`via_bot`) is refused with an answer. `reply_to_message` counts only when it is the bot's own message; else it is no reply (the hint). |
| 4 | Confirmation | Typed: a 👍 `setMessageReaction` on the reply (one call, no message, nothing overwritten). Not typed: `sendMessage` replying to it (`reply_parameters`, `allow_sending_without_reply`) with one fixed sentence; never the typed text or anything of the hub's error (herdr's may quote the pane), and the token is scrubbed as in every message. Reported when the lane ends, so herdr's failure and a worker gone meanwhile are said too. |
| 5 | The map | SQLite `notify_message` (chat, message) → worker, written when a notification is delivered: worker ids are minted at hire and survive a restart, and bridges restart on every release. Bounded on each write: the newest 500, none older than 7 days before the newest; a reply to an older or unknown one gets "I no longer know which worker this was, reply to a newer notification". |
| 6 | The route | `worker.prompt`'s body became `queuePrompt` / `queueInput`, shared by the client's command and the replies: `promptSourceOK`, `herdr.ValidMessage`, `checkTyped`, the `w.Ask` refusal, terminal-only built-ins, the harness translation, `tookOver` / `agentTookOver` / `clearFollowup`, the same lane (game and phone typing serialise) and herdr's allowlisted calls. A reply has no client: no command reply, no `worker.prompt` audit row. The refusals it must tell apart wrap sentinels (`errAskWaiting`, `errLaneFull`), their messages unchanged. A `/handoff` from Telegram is refused: the agent handoff is a client's command with its own audit and answer. |
| 7 | Backlog | Replies off: no poll. Switched on: the first poll is `offset=-1` (the backlog forgotten but the newest) and a message is typed only when dated **after the second** they were switched on; anything else is dropped without a word. Telegram's `date` is whole seconds, so "before the switch" cannot tell a message of that second sent while off from one sent just after: both are dropped (the trade-off: a reply sent in the very second of switching on is lost silently; reply again). The newest update `offset=-1` returns is still read (it may be a pairing's code); the date rule is what keeps it from a worker, on that path and on a poll that was already under way for a pairing. Replies on at a start (a restart): what came while the bridge was away is read; typed when sent within 2 minutes (a release takes seconds), else answered as not sent. |
| 8 | Switching off | Switched off, unpaired, paired anew or the token changed: nothing read before is typed, even a reply already past its checks. The channel stamps each reply with an epoch (bumped by every change of the switch, the token and the recipient) and the service hands it to the hub only inside `Channel.Hold`, which runs the hand-off under a lock those changes take first: once `notify.set`, `notify.unpair` or a pairing returns, no earlier reply reaches a worker. The gate is the channel's, not the service's, because pairing someone else is committed inside the channel's reader (the service hears of it after the greeting and a confirm, two HTTP calls). The hand-off is `worker.prompt`'s queueing on the hub (not the lane's typing), so the changes wait at most that long; nothing on the hub goroutine takes the lock. Overlapping `notify.set` commands (they run off the hub) are serialised, so the channel listens as the settings committed last say. |
| 9 | Limits | 4096 characters (Telegram's own message limit; within the hub's 16 KiB); 6 replies a minute to one worker, 20 in all; a queue of 64 replies; the bot's answers 10 a minute, more not sent, so a flood never makes the bot flood back. |
| 10 | Audit | `notify.reply` (client `bridge`): the worker id, the text's bytes and runes, the outcome; never the text. Logs carry the worker and the outcome only. |
| 11 | Settings | `replies` in `notify_settings` (a migration, default off), apart from `enabled`; switching on needs a token and a paired account, no test (nothing is sent to switch it on); no environment override. It stays on across unpairing: it reads only the account paired at the time, and the UI says it is paused while there is none. Settings → Telegram: **Allow replies**, a block of its own under the notification options with what it agrees to. |
| 12 | Out of scope | Tap-to-answer buttons for an ask (#14). |

## 2026-10-16 — #14: tap-to-answer buttons in Telegram for asks and follow-ups

A worker's ask (#57) or follow-up (#90) comes to the phone with a card per question and a button per answer; a tap
answers it as the game would. Code: `internal/notify/{notify,rule,tracker,service,reply,buttons,fakes}.go`,
`internal/notify/telegram/{api,channel}.go`, `internal/server/{ask,followup,notify}.go`; the game's
`ui/telegramSection.ts` (what Allow replies agrees to). Tests: `go test ./internal/notify/...` (`buttons_test.go`
through `FakeChannel`; the telegram package's `buttons_test.go` with a `notify.Service` against the `httptest` Bot API:
single, multi toggle + Done, Other, secret, stale and old taps, answered in the game, follow-up yes_no/choice/open,
others' taps, replies off, a rate-limited edit), `go test ./internal/server -run 'TestCard|TestNotifyFacts|TestReplyErrorKinds'`
(the fake herdr control: a tap answers the ask on its lane and the transcript records it; answered in the game first;
a secret Codex ask; a follow-up's prompt).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Gate | Cards only while `replies` is on: the taps are read by the replies' one `getUpdates` loop, and Allow replies is what agrees to answering workers from the phone (its note in the game says the cards hold the question and its options). Replies off, a notification is as before. No new setting. |
| 2 | What leaves | The notify core gets the cards' texts with the worker's facts (`notifyFacts`): an ask's header, question, options with description and preview; a follow-up's label, question, options, context, recommendation and a fallback's tail. An ask with **any** secret question gives none of its texts (as `notifyLine` already gave no line): `worker.answer_ask` needs every question in one call, so a secret one makes the whole ask the game's; one card says "Answer this one in the game", no button. |
| 3 | One card per question | A message per question (an ask has at most 4), one button per option (at most 8) on a row of its own (labels numbered, cut to 40 characters), Other…/Done/Cancel on the last row. Plain text, no `parse_mode` (no escaping of the agent's text). Each option keeps its share of the text (at most 3000 of 4000 UTF-16 units for all of them), a description 200 characters, a preview one 120-character line; the question gets what is left: Telegram counts UTF-16 units up to 4096, and a clipped message would lose the last options. Cards are sent quietly (`disable_notification`): the notification rang already. A follow-up of more than 4 questions (a grill round can have 30) gets no cards: 30 messages would hit Telegram's per-chat limit (about one a second) and bury the chat. |
| 4 | Callback data | `<8 hex token>:<action>` (`o<n>`, `x`, `d`, `c`; at most 11 bytes of Telegram's 64). The card is found by the tapped message (chat, message id), and its token must match that card's session; the tool_use_id or turn_id never leaves the bridge. The state is in memory, bounded (32 sessions): a restart makes old cards stale, which is what they are (a tap on one is answered "No longer open" and its buttons are taken away with `editMessageReplyMarkup`). |
| 5 | Collect, then answer | `worker.answer_ask` takes every question at once, so the answers are collected across the cards and handed to the hub when the last is complete (`Options.Answer`, the hub's `cardAnswer`): `queueAnswerAsk` / `queueAnswerFollowup`, the bodies of the two commands split from their client like #13's `queuePrompt`, so the checks, the lane and the herdr calls are the game's. A follow-up is answered whole too (its answer could leave questions out; the game's card sends them all). An answered question's card waits for the others without buttons; Cancel stays on the others. Follow-ups have no Cancel: `worker.answer_followup` has none (dismissing is the game's). |
| 6 | Typed answers | Other… and Answer… send a force-reply prompt of its own as a reply to the card (a message with a force reply cannot be edited, and the card must be; Bot API 10.3's `force_reply` on an inline keyboard is not relied on). A reply to the prompt is the typed answer, checked like the hub would (an ask's: one line, no control character, at most 4 KiB) so a bad one is refused in the chat and the prompt stays open, not after the other answers; it is never typed as a prompt. A reply to an open card gets the ask's hint. The cards are remembered in `notify_message` like notifications, so a reply to one after it ended types into its worker as #13 does. |
| 7 | Every tap answered | `answerCallbackQuery` at once, before any edit or the lane (the lane can take seconds; Telegram drops a late answer): "Sending…" when it sends, a short reason when it does not, nothing for a toggle. A tap the channel does not hand over (someone else's, another chat, replies off during a pairing), or one the service refuses (64 taps already wait for the replier), is answered there without text. Answers to taps are not counted in the bot's answer window (#13 decision 9): a spinner must always stop. |
| 8 | Edits | Each card's last shown text and buttons are kept and an edit that changes nothing is not sent; the toggles of one `getUpdates` batch make one edit (the replier edits after the batch). Telegram's 400 "message is not modified" is still taken as done; a 429 waits its `retry_after` once (up to 10 s), the way delivery does. Other edit failures are logged and the card stays as it was. |
| 9 | Answered elsewhere | The service loop sees every worker change: when what a worker asks (its ask's tool_use_id, its follow-up's turn) changes, the replier edits that session's cards to "Answered in the game." (not while its own answer is on its way: then the hub's outcome says it). The hub's `errStale` (the ask or follow-up replaced or answered meanwhile) is `ReplyClosed`: "No longer open". No network call is made on the loop: it queues the change for the replier, which owns the cards. |
| 10 | Gates | As replies: the paired `from.id` in its private chat (`chat.id` is `from.id`), not a bot; the switch on; `Hold` with the tap's epoch, so switching off, unpairing or pairing anew stops a tap read before, and the answer's hand-off runs inside `Hold` too. Switching replies off edits every open card to "Replies are off" (a tap read later finds no session). 30 taps a minute on one worker's cards, 60 in all (a multi-select of 8 is 9 taps): over that the tap is answered "Too many taps". |
| 11 | allowed_updates | `["message", "callback_query"]` on every `getUpdates` (Telegram keeps the last list given: #13's `["message"]` would keep dropping taps). |
| 12 | Audit | `notify.tap` (the worker, ask or follow-up, the question and option index, the action, the outcome) and `notify.answer` (the worker, the kind, how many questions and choices, the typed answers' bytes and runes, cancel, the outcome); a typed answer's reply is `notify.reply` `typed_answer`. Never an answer, a label or the typed text. |
| 13 | Out of scope | Screen-parsed permission and trust menus (`Worker.question`, `internal/server/question.go`): #88. |

## 2026-10-17 — #64 (part 3 of #61): the schedule dialog and manual creation

The game shows and makes #62's scheduled agents: a HUD dialog with the floor's schedules in three sections and every
Part 1 action, one create / edit form, "Schedule follow-up…" beside a reply's Handoff button, and toasts when a schedule
starts, starts late, is skipped or fails. Client only: no wire change. Code: `game/src/world/schedule.ts`,
`world/scheduleTime.ts`, `ui/schedules.ts`, `ui/scheduleForm.ts`, `ui/schedules.css`, `Hud.toast`'s action, the
follow-up button in `laptop/transcriptRender.ts`, `App.openSchedules` / `openFollowup` / `scheduleToasts`; the fixture
bridge's `tools/fixture-schedule.ts`. Tests: `scheduleTime.test.ts` (Temporal as the oracle), `schedule.test.ts`,
`schedules.dom.test.ts`, `scheduleForm.dom.test.ts`, `hudToast.dom.test.ts`, `transcriptHandoff.dom.test.ts`,
`tools/fixture-schedule.test.ts`, `e2e/schedules.spec.ts`; the dialog and the form are surfaces of `e2e/ui-surfaces.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where `running` goes | **Upcoming**, at its due time, with a "Starting" tape and no actions: it is not done yet, and Run now, Edit and Cancel all answer `invalid_state` for it. It moves to Scheduled successfully (or Needs attention) with the upsert that ends the fire. No toast for `running`: the toast is for the outcome. |
| 2 | What "fired" means | A live upsert into `done` (the hire went through) is "started"; into `failed` or `skipped` says so with its `state_reason`. Each toast has the title and a **Show** button. |
| 3 | Late | `fired_at − due_at` over the bridge's 5-minute grace (`ScheduleGrace`) is late: the toast says "started 3h late" and the row a "+3h late" badge (`spanText`: `7m`, `2h 20m`, `1d 4h`). Never for a negative value (Run now before its time) or within the grace, whatever the policy. |
| 4 | Wall clock to UTC | **Temporal's `disambiguation: 'compatible'`**, without Temporal (stable Safari lacks it): `wallToEpochMs` takes the zone's offsets a day either side; one candidate that reads back wins, two (a fall-back overlap) give the **earlier** instant, none (a spring-forward gap) uses the offset before the change, so the time moves later by the gap's length (30 min on Lord Howe, not a fixed hour). `daylight.zonedInstant` (a gap one hour later, an overlap's second showing) is the scene clock's and is unchanged. The resolver matched Temporal on 1.26M quarter hours (12 zones, 2025–2027) in the research; the tests compare it with Node 26's Temporal on every quarter hour of the 2026 transition days of Brussels, New York and Lord Howe, and every 7 h of 2026 in five zones. The oracle is typed locally in the test, so no `esnext.temporal` lib reaches the app's program. A "tomorrow" oracle must convert a `PlainDateTime`: `ZonedDateTime.with` keeps today's offset in an overlap (`offset: 'prefer'`) and gives the later instant. |
| 5 | The grammar | Relative: `in` + durations of `d` / `h` / `m` (and their words), summed: `in 24h`, `in 90m`, `in 1h30m`, `in 2 days`; elapsed time from now, so `in 24h` across a DST change is 24 real hours (and keeps now's milliseconds). Wall clock in the browser's zone (`browserZone()`, passed in: the parser reads no host clock or zone): `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` (also `T` between, seconds allowed: a `datetime-local` input's value), `today HH:MM`, `tomorrow HH:MM` (the zone's next calendar date, however long that day is), a bare `HH:MM` (today; once passed, the error says "tomorrow HH:MM"). A trailing `UTC` (or `Z`) reads both the date and the time in UTC (`tomorrow 10:45 UTC` is 10:45 on the next UTC day); with `in …` it is an error. Now or earlier is an error (the bridge refuses it). Relative input is read again at Save and every 15 s while the form is open, so the shown time stays true. |
| 6 | Showing a time | The row's stamp is the local `HH:MM` and date; beside it the UTC hint (`08:45 UTC`, with the date when UTC's differs) and a live countdown, which ticks once a second only while the dialog is open and is updated in place (no re-render). The form shows `Sun 18 Oct 2026, 10:45 CEST · 08:45 UTC · in 23 h 59 min` before Save is enabled. Names are English and the zone's short name is US English's, or British English's when US English only has `GMT+2`, so the text reads the same on every host. |
| 7 | The form's prefill | "New": `hire.prepare` for the floor, as the hire dialog (`formFromPrepare`: the floor's default or last hire, its folder, the worktree from Settings). A floor that cannot prepare (a real floor asleep: `hire.prepare` refuses it) falls back to the floor's settings (`default_hire`, `default_cwd`) with a note that the folder and worktree are checked when saved: a schedule for an asleep floor is what `wake_floor` is for. Edit: the schedule as it is (the floor fixed, its handoff kept unless changed, its `due_at` sent back to the millisecond while the When field still reads as it opened, the picker's echo of it included: read back, the minute-only wall clock would lose the seconds, and in a fall-back overlap the second showing would become the first, an hour early; the preview shows the kept instant with its own offset, `02:30 CET`). The follow-up: the source worker's harness, model, effort and cwd, `hire.prepare` with that cwd for the git base, an empty prompt, no time. Floor default to Save: `wake_floor` is always sent (its absence means on). |
| 8 | One op per reply | "Attach this message as a handoff" files a literal handoff on Save (`worker.handoff {mode: 'literal'}`), then `schedule.create` with its path. The form keeps **one op per reply** (worker, candidate, revision) for the page's life and, once the filing acks, its path: a refused create keeps the form open and a re-save does not file again; nor does the form opened again on the same reply after a Cancel. The path is reused rather than the op resent because the bridge checks the candidate before the op: after a new prompt the same op answers `handoff_unavailable`, not `existing`. The op is resent only when no answer came (a time-out, a drop), and then the bridge answers `existing`. A filing that acks after the form was cancelled or closed is still recorded on the reply. While a save is in flight, Floor and Folder are locked and the spec is the one Save read; a new open or a close ends it (its answer is dropped). A filing error schedules nothing and says why in the form; Cancel before Save files nothing. |
| 9 | Toasts | Detected in `onWorldEvent` before the store takes the upsert: only a schedule the store knows, whose state changed. A snapshot (first load, a reconnect) resets the book, so nothing in it is a transition; a re-delivered upsert has the same state. A transition that happened while the game was disconnected and comes in a `since_seq` replay toasts once, as `wrapUpToasts` does. No toast while the dialog is open: its row shows the change. |
| 10 | A toast's click | `Hud.toast(msg, ms, kind, soft, action?)`: with `{label, run}` the toast is a control (pointer events and a pointer only then, a **Show** button for the keyboard, the text in the live region as before); a click on it or its button runs the action and hides it. A plain toast, or one that timed out, takes no click and holds no button. Existing callers are unchanged. The schedule toasts open the dialog on the row (`openSchedules({focus})`: its floor, marked, focused). |
| 11 | Entry points | The Menu's Schedules (in "This floor" on a floor; in "City" elsewhere, on all floors) and a key-less palette row, "Scheduled agents". No keyboard shortcut (out of scope), so `docs/shortcuts.md` and `?` are unchanged. `openSchedules({floor?, allFloors?, focus?})` is the one way in (Part 4's boards will use it). |
| 12 | All floors | Off by default on a floor; outside a floor the dialog opens on all floors (the toggle is off there). All floors keeps the sections and groups each section's rows by `Building › floor` (`inboxWhere`), in the city's order; each floor's last 50 done are its own, so one busy floor never hides another's. |
| 13 | Errors | A refused command (`invalid_state`, `not_found`, `busy`, `bad_request`, no answer) is said in the dialog's or the form's alert line, with the bridge's reason and what to do, not only in a toast. An edited schedule that moves on or goes while its form is open says so in the form, and Save stays off. |

## 2026-10-17 — #88: tap-to-answer buttons in Telegram for screen permission and trust menus

A worker blocked on a permission prompt or a folder-trust menu that the bridge reads off its screen
(`Worker.question`, `internal/server/question.go`) gets #14's card: a button per choice, answered as the game's
`worker.answer`. Code: `internal/notify/{rule,tracker,service,reply,buttons}.go`, `internal/server/{question,notify}.go`;
the game's `ui/telegramSection.ts` (what Allow replies agrees to). Tests: `go test ./internal/notify/...`
(`TestCardMenu*`: through `FakeChannel`, and over the `httptest` Bot API: the card, others' taps, a stale menu, keys
left unresolved, replies off), `go test ./internal/server -run 'TestCardMenu|TestNotifyFactsMenu|TestMenuText|TestMenuErrorKinds'`
(the fake herdr control: a digit for Claude's permission prompt, down and Enter for its trust dialog, a stale hash
sends no key, a timeout's unresolved card, a choice menu gets none, no secret of the screen on the card; `TestNotifyFactsMenuCommandLine`: a command line that asks is not on the card), `TestCardMenuOnceAcrossReadGap`, `TestCardMenuOnceAcrossManyMenus`, `TestCardMenuCap` (one card per menu across a failed screen read and other menus; no card past the cap).

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which menus | `permission` and `trust` only, with 1-9 choices, while the worker is `blocked` and asks no ask or follow-up (those win: their cards are #14's). A `choice` menu gets none: it is mostly an ask's dialog read off the screen before its transcript says so, and its card would close as "Answered in the game" the moment the ask arrives. |
| 2 | What leaves | The menu's choices' labels only; what it asks is a fixed line per kind, ours ("It asks for your permission.", "It asks whether to trust a folder."), not the screen's question line: `questionText` takes the nearest line above the menu that asks, and a command line in the dialog can be that line (`$ curl -u user:pass 'Do you want to continue?'`). A label that may name a command is never searched for the command's end (a command can hold " commands" or a backtick itself): one that says what not to ask about again, as Claude Code's "Yes, and don't ask again for <prefix> commands in <dir>" and Codex's "Yes, and don't ask again for commands that start with `…` (p)" do, is cut where the command would start ("Yes, and don't ask again for …", a trailing one-letter key such as Codex's "(p)" kept); any other that says "ask again" or "command" is "…", unless it is one of the captured screens' labels known to name none (Claude's accept-edits choice: `menuLabelsKept`). Its Yes or No is the screen's, never ours: a tap sends that choice's number. In any other label a span in backticks, or one the screen cut off, becomes `` `…` ``; then `digest.Scrub`. No line above the menu (`menuContext`: the question, the command, the edit, a trust dialog's folder) nor the excerpt: a command line can hold a secret no pattern catches (`mysql -p…`, `--token …`), and a label must not be a side door for it. Checked against the captured screens (`TestNotifyFactsMenuCommandLine`, `TestNotifyFactsMenuScreens`; `TestMenuTextNamesCommand` for labels made to leak): Claude's labels are Yes, No and what to always allow (a directory, accept edits), Codex's quote their command, the trust dialogs' are fixed. A label can still name a folder (Claude's "always allow access to <dir>"); the copy says so. Done at the hub (`notifyFacts`, next to `notifyLine`), so the notify core never holds the raw text. The card says what it is about is not sent: a person approves knowing to look in the game for the command. |
| 3 | No Cancel | A `permission` menu has a Yes and a No choice by construction, both trust dialogs a quit choice; Esc differs by agent and kind (Claude's trust dialog exits). The game sends Esc only for a menu without a No (`answer.ts`), which these have. |
| 4 | When the card opens | The question is read off the screen after the worker turns blocked (`questionLoop`), so the needs-you notification mostly goes first, with no menu. The replier keeps the blocked spell (`status_since`) whose notification went and each worker's menu as the loop saw it, and opens the card when both are there for the same spell, whichever comes second (`openMenu`). A menu without a notification (a worker first seen blocked: a restart) gets no card. A new menu in the same spell (another hash) closes the old card and gets its own: its old buttons would only be stale. A menu gets one card while its spell lasts: the replier keeps, with the spell, the hashes that got a card, so a menu gone a moment (the hub publishes none while a screen read fails) and back with its hash gets no other, its old session forgotten or not, however many other menus came between. None is forgotten: past 32 (`maxCarded`) a spell opens no more cards (its notification went; the game answers its menus), since forgetting a hash would make it eligible again. The memory stays bounded: 32 hashes per worker's spell. |
| 5 | Answer path | `queueAnswer` is `worker.answer`'s checks and lane, split from `startAnswer` like #14's `queueAnswerAsk`; `cardAnswer` hands it the card's hash and the choice's number (`notify.Answer.Hash`, `Choice`; the hash never leaves the bridge: a button's data is #14's token and `o<n>`). So the screen is read again on the lane, a hash that no longer matches or a worker no longer blocked is `errStale`, and the keys are the game's: the digit on a numbered menu, arrows and Enter on a trust dialog. A worker no longer blocked when the tap is queued (`errInvalidState` for the game) is "No longer open" on the card. |
| 6 | Unresolved | Keys sent and the worker still blocked after `AnswerTimeout` (`AnswerResult.resolved: false`): the card says "✓ Sent: <choice>" and "It still waits on its menu: look at it in the game.", without buttons, and that menu gets no other card (the session is forgotten; the spell keeps the hash as carded, decision 4). Not a refusal: the keys went, as the game's toast says; `notify.answer` `unresolved`, not an error. A guess at the screen's state (sending again, a new card) could pick twice. |
| 7 | Stale | A new hash, the worker no longer blocked or the floor asleep: the loop sees what the worker asks change and edits the card "Answered in the game."; a tap that gets to the hub first is refused there ("No longer open"), no key sent. A restart makes the cards unknown (#14 decision 4). No new state: the hash changes on every re-block (`questionHash` folds in `status_since`). |
| 8 | Audit | #14's rows with `kind: "menu"`: `notify.tap` the option's index, `notify.answer` one choice and the outcome; no `worker.answer` row, never a label, a question or the hash. |

## 2026-10-17 — #65 (part 4 of #61): the departures boards

The schedules are visible in the office without opening anything: a departures board on every floor and one in each
building's lobby, both drawn from `store.schedules` (#64's `ScheduleBook`). Client only: no wire change. Code:
`game/src/world/departures.ts` (the text model, the lobby's plan, the click-to-scope mapping),
`render/departuresBoard.ts` (the boards' meshes and textures), the prop `board.departures` in `world/layout.ts`, the
lobby's board box in `world/building.ts` (`LobbyLayout.departures`), `render/props.ts`, `OfficeView.tickDepartures`,
`LobbyView.tickDepartures`, `App.stepDepartures` / `lobbyTarget`, the `aim:departures` scope and `departures.open` in
`input/shortcuts.ts`, the HUD's prompt. Tests: `world/departures.test.ts`, `render/departuresBoard.test.ts`,
`world/layout.test.ts` and `world/building.test.ts` (placement), `e2e/departures.spec.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the floor's board stands | **On top of the board partition**, centred over the clock, its face on both sides (`departuresBoardBox`: 3.2 m wide, 2.5 to 3.9 m up, posts then a 1.28 m face, 22 cm deep, a centimetre proud of the partition on each side). The desks and the corner office see it over the north face, the floor's lobby over the south one: it is straight ahead when you step off the elevator, above the Issues / PRs / Services boards, which stay where they are (0.8 to 2.3 m on the south face; the clock 1.78 to 2.3 m on the north). Mid-room, so it is on no outer wall: clear of every window, the whiteboard and TV, the cabinet, the mezzanine (slab, posts, stair) and the overview screen on every desk count and outline (`layout.test.ts` checks its box against every other prop's, the mezzanine and the walls, and that it is the pick from 1.6 m on either side, over the partition). Not the south wall by the elevator: from the desks the 2.5 m partition hides it. Not the partition's north face: the desks see it, the elevator does not. Not solid: nothing walks at 2.5 m. No floor-plan label (the partition's three are there already). |
| 2 | Where the lobby's board hangs | **Centred on the lobby's north wall**, facing the elevator and the front door: 5.6 × 2.8 m, 1 to 3.8 m up, 10 cm deep. From the elevator the lines to its corners pass clear of the reception's feature wall (`building.test.ts`); the corner plants are metres away. One group per floor **of that building** (`lobbyDepartures` takes the building's floors and ignores every other schedule), the top floor first like the floor directory. The groups' plan (`lobbyBoardPlan`): three departures a floor while a line can stay 14 cm tall, else two, **never one**; the biggest type that fits (at most 24 cm a line), in columns no narrower than 2.2 m (two on this board). A building has no floor cap (the bridge caps workers a floor, not floors a building), so one too tall for a page at two lines (over 8 floors here) is shown in **pages** of even size, as few as hold it at two lines (9 floors: 5 + 4; 12: 6 + 6, at three lines; 16: 8 + 8 at two), each a plan of its own for that many groups: the same type as a building of that many floors, so as readable. The page turns every **10 s** on the wall clock (`lobbyPage`, a pure function of it, so a board back in view is on the page it would be on; the turn is the model's `nextAt` too), a redraw per turn, and the header says which (`Floors 12–7 · 1/2`, by the logo). Not a bigger board: it would buy a few floors, not every count, and the elevator's sightline past the feature wall bounds it; not more columns: a group under 2.2 m cuts its titles to nothing. A turn is a cut (no flip, no glide), so battery saver and reduced motion change nothing; out of view nothing turns or draws (row 6). A group's pick box is its cell: it opens the floor **the board shows** in it (the model it drew, not the clock), an empty cell of the last page the board (every floor). |
| 3 | A floor's "logo" | Floors have no logo in the protocol (only `Building.logo`, M3d). A group shows **its floor's number on a flap** (the platform number) and its name; the **building's logo** is in the board's header next to "Departures" and the building's name (drawn from the logo cache's texture, keyed on its hash). |
| 4 | States | **Scheduled**: a countdown (`in 42s`, `in 12m 07s`, `in 4h 05m`, `in 2d 3h`); **Due now** (sun yellow) while a scheduled one is past its time by up to the bridge's grace; **Boarding** (a teal chip) for `waiting`; **Departing** (a blue chip, its time dimmed) for `running`, the dialog's "Starting": it has fired and is leaving; **Delayed +Xm** (a red chip). Late, precisely: an upcoming schedule **that has not started** (`scheduled` or `waiting`) whose `due_at` is **more than `SCHEDULE_GRACE_MS` (5 min) ago**, by `spanText(now − due_at)` (`Delayed +12m`, `+2h 20m`, `+1d 4h`). It wins over Boarding (a waiting one past the grace is delayed). A running one departs however late it fired: how late it started is the dialog's badge and the toast's (#64 decision 3), from `fired_at`. Times are on the browser's clock (the dialog's zone): `10:45` today, `Sun 10:45` within six days either side, else `24 Oct 10:45`. **Empty**: "No departures scheduled" (the floor's board adds "Look here and press E to schedule an agent."). A bridge before #62 (`known` false): "This herdr-world can’t schedule agents yet", the dialog's words. The attention count (failed and skipped not dismissed) is a red line on the floor's board ("2 need attention") and on each group's line in the lobby; upcoming ones past the rows shown are "+N later". |
| 5 | Cadence | A row due **within an hour, either side**, reads to the second and the board looks every second; beyond, to the minute, looking on the minute and at the instant a row comes within the hour (`nextAt`). Each row's text is computed from the clock rounded down to its own step, so between two looks the text cannot change (tested); the HUD's line has no countdown and changes only when a departure moves on. |
| 6 | Redraws and the frame policy | The board computes its **text model** only when the schedules changed (`ScheduleBook.version`: a snapshot's reset, an upsert, a removal) or the clock reached `nextAt`, **compares it with the last one drawn and redraws only on a difference**; a texture is uploaded only then. The comparison is of what is drawn (a row's time, title, state and status, not its id: a row replaced by one that reads the same is no redraw; a lobby group's floor id is kept, as its pick box opens it). At most one picture a wall-clock second (on the second, where the countdowns turn, so a tick is never pushed late). Out of view or under 4 % of the view's height it computes and draws nothing after its first picture, and catches up when it is back. An idle frame (nothing due) is two comparisons per board: no model, no allocation, no view test; while a look is due and the board is out of view, the in-view test (`PropViewer.share`, allocation-free) runs each frame until it is back, as the overview wall's does every frame. The frame policy (`render/framePolicy.ts`) is unchanged: the loop renders at its own rate and a new picture shows on the next frame; nothing here asks for frames. Unlit `MeshBasicMaterial`, not tone mapped, its own program key (`h3d-departures`), no light of its own (M17). |
| 7 | Long titles | Cut with an ellipsis at a **measured** width (`fitText`, the overview wall's), before the status column with a gap. The column keeps room for the widest status of the rows shown: a chip's text as it is, a countdown its widest form (`in 23h 59m`), so the cut moves only when a row's state changes, never with the seconds, and every title of a board (or a group) is cut at the same place. |
| 8 | Look | The overview wall's (#118 rows 3, 8, 9): its ink glass and scanlines, its sun yellow, its flap tiles (each time is four flaps with a seam, the colon two dots), the shared status palette for the chips (white on each ≥ 4.5:1) and the attention line (red on the ink ≥ 4.5:1; vitest). The HUD's face, like the wall: Departure Mono and Archivo load only with the overview page (#59). No flip animation: it would redraw every frame of a flip. |
| 9 | Opening | **E or a click** at the floor's board (either face): the dialog on that floor (`openSchedules({floor})`). In the lobby, E or a click at a **group**: that floor's; anywhere **else on the board** (its header, margins, the gap between columns): **every floor's, every building's** (`{floor: null, allFloors: true}`). Picked like the reception and the elevator: each group is a pick box of its own over its cell, a centimetre proud of the board's box, so it wins the nearest hit. A click (`App.clickDepartures`) goes the way a print's or a holder's tag does: from the crosshair while the mouse looks, else from the pointer, through the same pick as the crosshair's target (`targetAlong`), and it acts on the first click: a click on a board opens the dialog instead of taking the mouse; anywhere else it takes the mouse as before. The floor's board opens on a click too: it is the same board as the lobby's, and the issue's "click a group" reads as how a board is used, so both behave alike; its neighbours on the partition (Issues, PRs, Services) stay E only, as they were. The prompt shows both (`E` `Click` Schedules). One scope, `aim:departures` (E, `docs/shortcuts.md`); the lobby's targets carry their floor (`Target.floor`: undefined on a floor's board, a floor id, or null for elsewhere) and their label (`Departures: <floor>`), which the HUD's prompt key now includes. T picks the floor's board after the PRs board. |
| 10 | Live, reconnects, replay | Both boards read `store.schedules` only. A snapshot resets the book (a reconnect gives the same picture: rows by id, no duplicates, nothing stale), a re-delivered upsert gives the same model (computed again, not drawn), a removal or a cancelled / dismissed upsert drops the row. A floor renamed reaches the lobby's board (`setDepartureFloors`); a building's new shape (another floor) rebuilds the lobby, as before. |

## 2026-10-17 — #93: hire presets, on one floor or global

A hire preset is a named first prompt with the harness, model and effort to hire it with, so a job that comes back
("Deploy a release to A, B and C" on a small model) is saved once and hired again in two clicks. Code: the bridge's
`internal/store/hirepreset.go`, `internal/server/hirepreset.go`, `internal/proto/hirepreset.go`; the game's
`world/hirePresets.ts` (the book and the pure rules), `ui/hirePresets.ts` (the tab, the list and the editor), the exact
mode of `ui/hire.ts` (`HirePrefill.preset`), `ui/deskTabs.ts`; the fixture's `tools/fixture-hirepreset.ts`. Tests:
`go test ./internal/store -run HirePreset`, `go test ./internal/server -run HirePreset`, `world/hirePresets.test.ts`,
`ui/hirePresets.dom.test.ts`, `ui/hire.preset.dom.test.ts`, `tools/fixture-hirepreset.test.ts`,
`e2e/h93-hire-presets.spec.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Its own concept | A **separate table, type and namespace** (`hire_preset`, `hire_preset.*`), not `team_preset` / `preset.*`: a team preset is five roles of a run, a hire preset one worker with a prompt. Not the game's task levels either: those have no prompt, live in the browser and fall back by design; nothing is migrated between the three. |
| 2 | Scope | **One floor** (its floor id: a real floor's session name or a virtual floor's own id) or **global** (`floor` NULL / absent: every floor of every building of this bridge). No building scope, no base-session scope: two virtual floors on one session, and a session's real floor beside them, keep their own lists. Renaming, moving to another building, hiding, sleeping or a session missing from discovery for a while change nothing; deleting a virtual floor deletes its own presets in its cleanup transaction (never promoted to global, never moved to the base floor), global ones stay. Cross-bridge sharing is out of scope. |
| 3 | Names | The team presets' rule (trimmed, 1–40 bytes, no control characters), unique **ignoring case within a scope** (the store's unique index on (scope, `name_key`), the check in the transaction; the client's book mirrors it). The bridge compares names with full Unicode case folding: `name_key` is x/text's `cases.Fold` of the name, then `strings.ToLower`. **The bridge's equality must be at least as coarse as the game's** (`toLowerCase` in the book's `nameTaken` and the fixture), or it stores two names the game considers one and then refuses to save either: SQLite's `lower()` folds ASCII only (`Équipe`/`équipe`), and Go's `strings.ToLower` alone differs from `toLowerCase` on a word-final `Σ` (`ς`) and on `İ` (`i` plus U+0307). Folding is coarser on purpose: `STRASSE`/`straße` are one name on the bridge and two in the game, so the game sends the second and shows the bridge's `name_taken` on the name field like any other clash. `ToLower` after the fold because x/text folds Cherokee capitals to small letters and small letters to capitals. Checked once over every code point (Node 26's ICU and x/text, both Unicode 17): no name pair the game joins gets two keys; a newer Unicode in the browser than in the bridge could reopen that. Team presets keep `lower()` (out of #93's scope). The same name on a floor and globally is allowed and both stay listed, each in its group: a floor's never hides a global one. |
| 4 | Validation | On create and every update, the bridge checks the name, the scope (a floor it knows, unless the update keeps the preset's own floor, so a floor not seen for now stays editable), the prompt as a first prompt (required, 32 KiB, no NUL, no leading `--`, typeable for Claude) and the tuple against the full catalog (`validHireIn`, the catalog's own rule; no model slug is hardcoded). The editor checks the same before it sends. Update replaces the whole spec (scope included): no "was the floor left out on purpose?" ambiguity. |
| 5 | Using one | Nothing hires by itself. **Use** opens the ordinary hire dialog at that desk with the saved prompt and choice; the player reviews and presses Hire, which is `worker.hire` through the shared `HireCoordinator` (capacity, worktree, a virtual floor's wake, background hiring, failed and unknown outcomes): one Hire, one worker, as often as wanted. The folder, worktree, desk and name are the floor's and the dialog's, never saved in a preset (so a global one fits every project). Edits in the form are that hire's only; the preset changes only through its editor. |
| 6 | Exact choice | The form from a preset holds the saved harness, model and effort **as they are**: `prepare` assigns them instead of resolving, `resolveForm` (Settings changes, a failed hire) leaves them, `options()` keeps a model Settings hides offered and picked (`offeredOptions`, the precedent of the other pickers). When the whole catalog lacks the harness or model, or the model does not take the effort (`exactProblem`), the saved values stay in the form and in a callout that says why, no chip is checked for what is missing, and Hire is blocked until the player picks; `pickExact` makes an effort picked never move a missing model to another one. The list marks such a preset "Needs a new choice". The stored preset is never rewritten by a catalog change. |
| 7 | Drafts | A desk's unsent first task (text, images, file chips) is never replaced silently: Use on such a desk asks (**Keep my task** / **Replace it**), and only Replace makes the preset's text the desk's draft. On a desk without one the preset's text opens like any handed-in first task (the desk's draft is untouched until it is edited). Cancel, a refused hire and an unknown outcome keep the dialog's usual draft and recovery behaviour. Save as preset… keeps text and file mentions; images are left out and the editor says how many. |
| 8 | Live changes | The book follows `hire_preset.upsert` / `.removed`; an open hire form is a copy and never changes with them, and a submitted hire is the coordinator's captured args. An open editor whose preset changed elsewhere (a newer `updated_at`) says so and offers **Load the saved version** (saving otherwise overwrites); one deleted elsewhere says so and its Save becomes **Save as new preset**. |
| 9 | Older bridge | No `hire_presets` in the snapshot (absent is not empty): the tab is still there and says "This herdr-world can’t keep hire presets yet. Update the bridge to use it."; Save as preset… is not offered. Team presets and task levels are untouched. |
| 10 | Placement and look | A third desk tab, **Hire preset**, beside Hire worker and Hire orchestrator, in both dialogs that show the tabs (← → cycle the tabs that can be picked). Its own dialog, like the start-run dialog the orchestrator tab opens, in the desk dialogs' navy and the team presets' rows: **This floor** (with the floor's name) then **Global** (every floor of every building), each row its name, the readable model and effort (the catalog's labels) and the prompt's first two lines, with Use, Edit and Delete (confirmed). The plate is a recipe card with a folded corner. Below 480 px the three tabs share the width without icons. |

## 2026-10-17 — #95: hire presets' "Add herdr hiring instructions" and the classifier's nudge

A worker hired from a preset whose prompt starts other agents ("for each pull request, spawn a subagent") put them in
split panes and on its session's real floor: nothing told it what `run.start` tells an orchestrator. A preset can now
add the same two lines (the placement line and the floor line) when it is used, and the editor asks to tick that when
the classifier says the prompt hands work to other agents. Code: `internal/server/hire.go` (`startHire`),
`internal/server/runprompt.go` (`hireInstructions`), `internal/server/hirepreset.go` (`hire_preset.check`), the
store's `migrationHirePresetInstructions`; the game's `world/hirePresets.ts` (`nudgeWanted`, `instructionsBlocker`),
`ui/hirePresets.ts` (the box and the question), `ui/hire.ts` (the per-hire box, "What's added"),
`ui/hireCoordinator.ts` (`HireRequest.added`); the fixture's `tools/fixture-hirepreset.ts` and `fixture-sim.ts`.
Tests: `go test ./internal/store -run HirePreset`, `go test ./internal/server -run 'HerdrInstructions|HirePresetCheck'`,
`world/hirePresets.test.ts`, `ui/hirePresets.dom.test.ts`, `ui/hire.preset.dom.test.ts`, `ui/hireCoordinator.test.ts`,
`tools/fixture-hirepreset.test.ts`, `e2e/h93-hire-presets.spec.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Who adds the lines | **The bridge**, on `worker.hire` with `herdr_instructions: true`; the game only sends the flag. `startHire` appends `"\n\n" + hireInstructions(floor)` (`placementLine`, a blank line, `floorLine`: one copy of the text, the one `run.start` uses) for the floor after `floorArg`, so a virtual floor gets its own `v-…` id and a client from before floor ids its session's. The prompt is replaced **before** `hireShape`, so size and Claude's typed check see the whole of it, and `compose` translates only the player's text (#39); setting only `compose` would let `hirePromptFor`'s fail-open send an over-limit task without the lines. The flag without a prompt is `bad_request`. |
| 2 | What the flag means | "The current hiring instructions for the floor the preset is **used** on", never a frozen text or a stored floor: a global preset fits every floor, presets follow a later change of the text (for example an agent-facing CLI) with no migration, and Save as preset… can never save `h3d_floor=<this floor>` into a prompt. Stored as `hire_preset.herdr_instructions INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` by a new migration (the #93 one is released): every older preset is off. `hire_preset.update` replaces it with the rest of the spec. |
| 3 | Showing what goes out | `hire.prepare`'s ack carries the exact lines for its floor (no new round trip, no copy of the text in the game); the hire form shows them under a collapsed **What's added**, with the first prompt's bytes including them. |
| 4 | Per hire | A form from a preset has its own box under the preset note, ticked to match the preset (#93 decision 5: edits are this hire's only); unticking it sends the prompt without the lines and leaves the preset as saved. The coordinator captures the flag with the other args (#93 decision 8). Plain Hire worker, Hire orchestrator, dictated and scheduled hires have no box and are unchanged. Save as preset… seeds the editor's box from the form's (off from a plain form). |
| 5 | Size | The form blocks Hire with a reason when the task, the blank line and the lines are over 32 KiB (or there is no task); the coordinator resolves images' paths against 32 KiB minus the added bytes and says so; the bridge refuses the composed prompt as a backstop. |
| 6 | Older bridges | `decodeArgs` refuses unknown fields, so the game must know first: the snapshot's `herdr_instructions: true` (absent is not empty, #93 decision 9). Without it the editor shows no box and no question but "This herdr-world can’t add hiring instructions yet.
