Update the bridge to use it.", the form shows no box, and nothing sends the field. `hire_preset.check` is `unknown_command` there (the game's `unsupported` set). |
| 7 | The nudge | `hire_preset.check {prompt}` → `{delegates, score}`, packup.detect's shape: one `ai.Classifier.Classify` with state `{"prompt"}` and a question that covers both words people use ("start, spawn, hire or hand work to other AI agents (subagents, workers, coders, reviewers)"), bounded at 1.2 s, failing open (no classifier, not ready with no call, an error, a timeout: delegates false). It runs on the editor's Save (create, edit, Save as preset…) only with the box off, no `h3d_floor` in the prompt, the check supported and the classifier `ready`; never on Use or Hire, and the game gives up at 1.5 s. A "yes" asks **in the dialog** (the Keep my task / Replace it pattern, not a second modal): **Turn on and save**, **Save as is** (that preset is not asked about again until its prompt text changes; the game remembers the answer per preset id for the page's lifetime, a new preset's under the id its create returns), Esc back to the editor without saving. The box is only ever ticked by a click. No keyword fallback: it would be noisy on exactly the "Cloudflare Workers / web worker / user agent" negatives. Prompt text is never logged or audited, only its length. |
| 8 | Threshold | Fixed at **0.7** (followup's default; packup uses 0.85): a false positive is one dismissible question, a miss puts agents on the wrong floor. The ~30-prompt bench the ticket suggests was not run in this change (it needs a live classifier); the threshold is one constant in `internal/server/hirepreset.go` to move once it is. |
| 9 | A hint, not placement | As `floorLine`'s comment says, nothing makes the agent tag its workspaces; when it does, `inheritFloors` places them on that floor (#58 stage 3). On a real floor the token names the session itself and changes nothing; the placement line still helps there. A bridge that places spawned agents itself (an agent-facing CLI) is out of scope; only what the flag adds would change. |

## 2026-10-17 — #33: a refused or unanswered dictated background hire keeps its task

In background mode the review closes at Hire and hands the coordinator no draft (`prompt: null`, #214 decision 5), so a
dictated hire that was refused or got no answer (dropped connection, timeout) lost its task: only a toast was left, and
the review reopened only for a refusal, only when nothing else was open, and only when the one review instance had not
been opened for another dictation since. Code: `ui/hireReview.ts` (`Reviewed`, `giveBack`, `restore`, `heldReviews`,
`reopenHeld`), `ui/hireCoordinator.ts` (`HireRequest.kept`, `backgroundOutcomeText`, `unknownText`),
`world/palette.ts` (`heldList`), `ui/palette.ts` (the held rows), `ui/hire.ts` (`DialogHost.toast`'s action), `app.ts`
(`reviewHeld`). Tests: `ui/hireCoordinator.test.ts`, `ui/hireReview.dom.test.ts` ("a task given back"),
`world/palette.test.ts` and `ui/palette.dom.test.ts` ("held reviews"), `e2e/h214-edge-cases.spec.ts`.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What is kept | **What was reviewed when Hire was pressed**, a snapshot taken in `hire()` (floor, desk, the choice with its origins, label, folder, worktree and what of them was changed, the prepare, the pending interpretation, the open sections), never the instance's fields: a newer dictation's `open()` overwrites those, which is exactly the case that lost the task. `not_sent` keeps its old path; the review never produces it (no draft to resolve). Foreground and the hire dialog are unchanged. |
| 2 | Given back at once | When the hire ends `failed` or `unknown` and **no `dialog[open]`** exists and the floor can take a hire (there, not asleep unless virtual), the review opens again from the snapshot in `ready` (a new `epoch` and `opening`), the keyboard on the first open section (`focusFirst`, never Hire). No `hire.prepare` is sent: it would overwrite the status line, and the bridge checks the caps on the next Hire anyway. |
| 3 | Held | Otherwise the snapshot is **held in memory** (a `Map` in the review, by a serial id; a reload drops it, as nothing else of a review survives one) and the open dialog is never closed, reset or overwritten. Two refused hires at desks 15 and 16 are two held reviews. The toast's **Review again** and a palette row open one; the row heads the commands while it is held (`Review again: Desk 15 of pastry`, `Not hired` / `No answer`, the task's first 80 characters; found by its name, task, "hire again", "failed hire", "kept task"). A held review is dropped once it is opened: from then on it is an ordinary review (Esc, Cancel). |
| 4 | Busy | Used while the review is open: "A hire review is open: finish or close it first. The task for Desk 15 of pastry is still kept." (the palette's `reviewHeld` does not dismiss the overlays then); with another dialog open: "Close the open dialog first. …"; a floor gone or asleep: why, "… is still kept.". It stays held each time. Other dialogs are dismissed by a palette pick as for any palette command. |
| 5 | One message per outcome | Opened again: **the review's status line says it, no toast** (`HireRequest.kept` makes the coordinator skip its `failed` / `unknown` toast; the review says it instead). Refused: `failureText` as the foreground's line. Unanswered: "No answer about the hire at Desk 15 of pastry: the connection dropped. It may still have gone through: look at the desk before you hire again." Held: the coordinator's text plus " The task is kept: review it again here or from the command palette." with the action (error / warn as before). The hire dialog's toasts are unchanged, "The task stays in its hire dialog." only with a draft. The background hire dialog still shows its toast and reopens (not part of this change). |
| 6 | The toast is not the route | The HUD has one toast at a time, and while a modal is open the toast sits under it and cannot be clicked: the held toast often goes unseen while the next review is being edited. The palette row is the route that lasts; the toast's action is the shortcut when the player is free. |
| 7 | The worker came anyway | After an unknown outcome the late ack may seat the worker at the desk. `refresh()` (every world change) re-renders an open review, and `takenBy()` → `blocker()` turns Hire off with "… just sat down at Desk 15." and the explicit "Hire at Desk 16 instead"; a held review opened later reads the world the same way. Never another desk silently. No change was needed. |
| 8 | No `worker.hire` | Giving back, holding, the toast's action, the palette row, a reconnect and a late ack send nothing; only a new press of Hire, a new hire with a new key (the epoch moved). Every new e2e asserts the count. |
| 9 | A stale test | The timeout test shortened the client's `worker.hire` bound by mapping a 90 000 ms `setTimeout` (#214 row 4), but #58 raised the bound to 120 s, so the test waited on a timer that never came and failed on `main`. It maps 120 000 ms now. |

Coverage:

| Bullet | Where |
|---|---|
| Refused, nothing open: the review again with the reviewed values, ready, a new Hire is a new hire | `h214-edge-cases` "a refused dictated background hire: its review opens again…"; `hireReview.dom.test.ts` "refused with nothing else open…" |
| Unknown, disconnect: the review again with the status line | `h214-edge-cases` "a dropped connection while a confirmed dictated hire is on its way…"; `hireReview.dom.test.ts` "no answer (disconnected / timeout)…" |
| Unknown, timeout with a late ack: the desk taken, no enabled Hire | `h214-edge-cases` "…does not answer in time…"; `hireReview.dom.test.ts` "no answer, then the worker sits down after all…" |
| Held: another dialog open, recovered via the palette; busy while it is open | `h214-edge-cases` "refused while the next dictation's review is open…"; the toast's action and two held: `hireReview.dom.test.ts` "another dialog open…", "two refused…" |
| A review being edited is not closed, reset or overwritten | `h214-edge-cases` "a review being edited while an earlier dictated hire completes…" and "refused while the next dictation's review is open…" |
| Coordinator silent for a kept task, toast text | `hireCoordinator.test.ts` "a caller that keeps the task…", "backgroundOutcomeText…" |
| Palette rows | `world/palette.test.ts`, `ui/palette.dom.test.ts` "held reviews" |

## 2026-10-17 — #53: a walk-out no longer holds its old desk

After a send-home, hiring at the same desk was refused ("Someone is already on the way to Desk 6.") until the outgoing
worker had walked the whole route (packing, the fire escape, the street: ~55 s from storey 0, ~123 s from storey 6),
while the desk already showed its "+" for most of that. `App.heldDesks`, which all the hire paths read (explicit desk
and the top bar's Hire, the start-run desk, `nearestFreeDesk`, the dictated hire and its review), counted the desk of
every walk under way. Code: `app.ts` (`heldDesks`, `applyReserve`, `packingDesks`, `walkAway`). Tests:
`src/app.rehire.test.ts`, `e2e/m4.spec.ts` ("#53: a desk whose worker was sent home…").

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | What holds a desk | **A hire on its way to it (`Reservations`) or a new hire dropping in to it (a `kind: 'in'` walk)**, besides the seat itself (`resolveHireDesk`). A walk-out (`kind: 'out'`) never does: `worker.removed` with `sent_home` comes after the bridge removed the worker, the backend already lets a hire reuse that desk (`TestSendHome`), and what is left of the walk is decoration. `Arrivals.yieldDesk` (the newcomer's drop-in, `yieldTakenDesks` on its upsert) already makes such a walk give the desk up for good. |
| 2 | A report's walk (`kind: 'deliver'`) | **Does not hold its desk either.** While its freelancer is in the store, the seat check refuses the desk. Once the bridge removed it (`leaveDelivery`), a hand-over still finishing is chained to a walk-out (`Arrivals.chain`) and the seat is free like any sent-home worker's; the office then shows the desk without a seat and not `away`, so with its "+", and a desk showing its "+" must be hireable. A newcomer's `yieldDesk` hands the chair back (`out: null`); the chained walk-out starts standing and shows its desk `free` from its first frame, so it never sets the newcomer's desk `away`. |
| 3 | A hire on its way while the walk-out packs | Reachable now (the top bar's Hire, `nearestFreeDesk`, a dictated hire: aiming at the desk needs its "+"). The reserved look (chair in, no laptop, no "+") used to overwrite the packing every frame, so the worker mimed packing things the desk no longer showed, and the desk was rebuilt twice a frame. **The walk keeps the desk's look until its "+" would come back** (`applyReserve` skips a desk an un-yielded walk-out still shows), **then the desk shows reserved**, and a walk's `away: null` on a reserved, unseated desk is the reserved look (`walkAway`), so nothing flips between "+" and reserved each frame. The reservation does not make the walk yield: a hire can fail, and there is no newcomer yet (`yieldDesk` would then return false on the upsert, and the drop-in's figure would wait for the ack). |
| 4 | The hire fails | The release sees the walk on the desk and leaves the look to it: the next frame shows the packing (or the "+", once due) again. After the walk is over, the release gives the desk back at once (unchanged). |
| 5 | The new worker sits down | Unchanged: its upsert yields the walk (it gets up and goes with what it packed so far, never shows the desk again), and `applyReserve` leaves a seated desk alone. |

## 2026-10-17 — #22 (phase 1 of #20): the native child-agent model and protocol

The provider-neutral record of the subagents a worker's own Claude Code or Codex session runs, its events and how it
survives a restart, with a fake source to build against before the providers land (phases 2, 4 and 6 of #20 are sources;
phase 3 renders it, phase 5 opens a child's transcript). Code: `internal/childagents` (`Registry`, `Source`,
`Evidence`), `internal/childagents/childagentstest` (the fixture source and scripts, test code only),
`internal/server/children.go` and the hooks in `hub.go` (`childSession` on every worker refresh, `Remove` beside the
workers' other per-worker state), `game/src/world/children.ts`, `decodeChild` in `game/src/net/protocol.ts`. Docs:
`protocol.md` "Native child agents (#22)".

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the generation lives | **On the worker, `child_generation`**, and on every child and `child.removed`. It changes exactly when `agent_session` does, which already sends a `worker.upsert`: the replacement and the new generation arrive in one event, before any child of the new session, and the snapshot carries every worker's generation, also with no children (a client needs it to drop a late one). No `children.reset` event and no `child.removed` per dropped child: the `worker.upsert` is the explicit signal. `syncFloorWorkers` compares workers with `reflect.DeepEqual`, so the generation must not move otherwise; it does not (only `Registry.Worker` sets it, on a session change). The children themselves are never on the worker. |
| 2 | What a generation is | **The next value of one bridge-wide counter**, not a per-worker count: a worker that is removed and comes back (identity matching can keep its id) always gets a higher one, and the bridge keeps nothing of removed workers. Compare per worker only. A bump is a non-empty `agent_session` that changes, to another or to none; a first session after none (`""` → X) keeps the generation, since evidence of an unbound session never attaches. Herdr losing a session for a moment drops the children; they come back with the replay when it returns (row 5). The counter restarts with the process, which the epoch change covers. |
| 3 | How the bridge drops an older generation's evidence | **Evidence names the root agent session, never a worker or a generation.** Sources know sessions (Claude's `session_id`, Codex's root thread); the registry binds each worker to its current session and resolves evidence through that binding, so evidence of a replaced session finds no worker and is dropped (`ErrUnbound`), whatever the source thought the generation was. A session another worker already holds is not bound twice (its evidence stays with the first). Phase 4 can bind the session the usage tracker finds for a Codex worker herdr knows none of through the same `Registry.Worker`. |
| 4 | Provisional ids | A row's id is `ch_` + 16 hex of sha256(worker, generation, provider, the first id seen and whether it was the native or the dispatch id). The root indexes both ids (`native`, `dispatch`, provider-scoped); evidence with both links them, so the provisional row gains `native_id` and keeps its id; evidence by either finds it. A dispatch that already has another native id is a conflict and dropped. Two rows that turn out to be one child (a native-only hook before the link) merge into the one seen first, with a `child.removed` for the other: the only removal reconciliation makes, and unavoidable without guessing a pairing by time. The row that stays keeps every field either row observed, never older evidence over newer: each row remembers when evidence last set each last-wins field (the root's observation count, so a replay gives the same answer), and a merge takes the later one (`state` with its `source`, `ended_at` also when a resumed `running` cleared it, `name`, `type`, `description`, `transcript`); the earliest `started_at`, the latest `last_activity_at`. Not by `last_activity_at`: a row often has none, and evidence that sets a field need not move it. Deterministic ids make the same evidence give the same rows (the frames fixtures are stable), but the graph's identity is the tree, not the ids: the rebuild tests compare trees. |
| 5 | Persistence | **None.** The children's truth is the providers' evidence (transcripts on disk, the server's threads), and a stored graph would be stale after a restart (a process lost, a child finished meanwhile). The `Source` contract is a replay: whenever the hub binds a worker to a session it has not bound (every worker after a restart, a new or first session), it calls `Replay(session)`, and the source sends everything it can find for it again, in observation order, through `Run`'s emit. `Replay` never blocks (the hub calls it on its goroutine); `emit` returns once the hub applied the evidence. Tested by a fresh registry fed by replays only, and by a restarted hub on the same database with the source's evidence (the same trees) and with an empty source (no children). |
| 6 | Unknown is a value | A new child is `state: unknown`, `parent: unresolved`, `transcript: unavailable` until evidence says otherwise; a state outside the list is read as `unknown` (dropping the evidence would lose its other fields). Evidence fields merge: a set field replaces, an empty one keeps; `started_at` keeps the earliest, `last_activity_at` the latest; `running` without an end clears `ended_at` (a resumed child). The registry has no clock and no timeout, so quiet logs never complete anything. |
| 7 | Parents | Evidence names a parent by its native or dispatch id. One not seen yet leaves the child `unresolved` and remembers the name; the parent showing up resolves it in the same observation (the parent's upsert first). A removed parent leaves its children `unresolved`, waiting for it. A parent that would make a cycle is ignored, also through a merge (row 4): the row kept takes the parent of the two rows observed later if it is still one (not the row itself, which a parent naming the other row would be, nor a descendant of either), else the other's, else `unresolved`, and the dropped row's children go under it through the same check (`link`), `unresolved` should one make a cycle. `childTree` (and `childagentstest.Tree`) show a row whose parent chain comes back to it under the root worker anyway, so a bad parent can never hide a row and its descendants. Evidence cannot say "unresolved" (Kind is `root` or `child`): not naming a parent leaves it as it is. |
| 8 | The fake source | `FixtureSource` (`Emit`, `Replay`, `Wait`) is driven step by step by the tests, never by a timer, so the frames are deterministic: a session change is the test publishing herdr's observation and waiting for its `worker.upsert`, and `Wait` covers the replays it asked for. Scripts (`internal/childagents/testdata`) say what happens and the trees each worker must end with; the same files drive the registry tests, the hub test and Vitest (via the frames). |
| 9 | A dev way to see it | **The game's fixture bridge plays the frames** (`?ws=/fixture/children-<name>`) instead of a hidden `herdr-world` flag: the fixture's sessions could never match a real bridge's workers, and the recorded frames are what a bridge sends, with no production path. |
| 10 | Limits | 500 children per root session (evidence of a new one beyond is dropped, `ErrFull`); `name` and `type` at most 128 bytes, `description` 1000, cut on a rune boundary. |

Coverage:

| Bullet | Where |
|---|---|
| Fixtures end to end: two concurrent, nested, unresolved then resolved, provisional then native (and a merge), merges that keep every field and make no cycle, every state, a session replaced with late old-session evidence, a worker removed | `internal/childagents/testdata/*.json`; `TestScripts` (registry), `TestChildFixturesEndToEnd` (hub: frames and a fresh snapshot), `children.frames.test.ts` (decoder and store) |
| Provisional to native keeps the row; merge; conflict | `TestProvisionalReconciledToNative`, `TestMergeKeepsTheRowSeenFirst` |
| Generations: bump rules, old session dropped, removed and back, a session held by another worker | `TestGenerations`; `children.test.ts` "drops an old-generation upsert…", "a child of a newer generation…", "removes a child…" |
| Unknown parent and state are values, never guessed | `TestDefaultsAreUnknown`, `states.json`, `unresolved.json`; `children.test.ts` "never guesses…" |
| Parents: cycles, a removed parent | `TestParents`; `childTree` in `children.test.ts` |
| A merge keeps every observed field (the later of two) and makes no cycle | `TestMergeKeepsEveryObservedField`, `TestMergeMakesNoCycle`, `merge.json`; `childTree` "shows a row in a cycle…" in `children.test.ts` |
| Restart rebuilds from evidence, nothing persisted | `TestFreshRegistryRebuildsFromReplay`, `TestChildAgentsRebuiltAfterRestart`; evidence before its worker: `TestChildEvidenceReplayedOnBind` |
| Wire names | `TestChildWire` (`internal/proto`) |
